credentials, one blocked integration, or the completion of a prior version do
not by themselves justify a no-change result. Complete all independent safe
work.

For Mode B2, no-change is allowed only when the exact Smarter Justice artifact
and central mission have been audited and no safe material central improvement,
contract correction, coordination defect, launch-control correction, or owner-
action preparation justifies a release.

Mode B1 is not a code-release classification. A coordination-only cycle may end
without a ZIP when the register, queue, dependencies, decisions, evidence dates,
and action packets are current and no central code build was authorized or
materially justified.


===============================================================================
COORDINATED LAUNCH COMPLETION, ACTIVE OWNER COLLABORATION, AND SECURE GATE CLOSURE
===============================================================================

This section is the single canonical launch-completion and owner-assistance
standard for this prompt. Later sections may reference it but must not create a
conflicting duplicate protocol.

CURRENT OWNER LAUNCH TARGET AND TEMPORAL TRUTH

Roger's current target is the strongest safe accepted public, attorney-
demonstration, professional-enrollment, and freemium-service launch scope
achievable for use beginning Monday, August 3, 2026, in America/New_York.
This newer explicit owner date supersedes the prior Sunday, August 2, 2026
sprint target.

- On or before August 3, 2026, treat that date as the active immediate launch-
  readiness target.
- After August 3, 2026, do not erase, silently extend, or pretend the target was
  met. Record the exact result as achieved, partially achieved, missed,
  superseded, or owner-replanned, and continue remaining launch work as an
  overdue launch objective or under the newest explicit owner date.
- The date never authorizes fabricated acceptance, unsafe activation, concealed
  defects, skipped tests, unreviewed commercial claims, or false production
  status.
- The passage of the Monday target does not expire the four-portal readiness,
  public-free-core, $15 USD monthly pricing, claim/member separation, human
  demonstration, or owner-assistance decisions. They remain controlling until
  Roger expressly supersedes them.

MONDAY, AUGUST 3, 2026 LAUNCH OBJECTIVES

Complete the strongest safe connected scope needed so that, where exact
acceptance supports it:

1. Public legal users can use the portal's core legal starting help, official-
   resource navigation, attorney and firm search, basic Document Help,
   memory-first question preparation, and selected public tools without charge.
2. The demonstrated homepage, search, exemplary attorney profile, exemplary
   firm profile, selected public tool, Document Help entry, professional-value
   page, claim or enrollment continuation, QR/short-link path, and mobile
   experience are top-tier and ready for an attorney to evaluate immediately.
3. Attorneys can understand and, where Tier B/C gates are accepted, enroll in a
   $15-per-month covered-attorney membership through Smarter Justice.
4. Firms can understand and, where Tier B/C gates are accepted, enroll in a
   $15-per-month claimed-firm membership plus $15 per month for each covered
   attorney profile.
5. Public users who exceed a reasonable free allowance for high-cost AI, OCR,
   document processing, storage, or comparable services receive a transparent,
   explicit, owner-approved cost-recovery option rather than a surprise charge
   or silent service failure.
6. Every unresolved real-world launch action that Roger or a qualified person
   can perform is presented to him promptly as a concrete secure Owner
   Assistance Request, not left passively in a backlog.

TOP-TIER MONDAY PRIORITY ORDER

Unless a P0 or stricter safety requirement controls, prioritize:

1. Public and professional first impression.
2. The exact 15-second, 2-minute, and 10-minute attorney-demonstration paths.
3. A reliable free public-user starting path and useful non-AI fallback.
4. Accurate attorney and firm profile pages, search, claiming, and correction.
5. Smarter Justice professional enrollment, $15 pricing presentation, and
   payment test readiness.
6. Hosting, domain, monitoring, support, rollback, and post-deployment truth.
7. High-cost AI or document services only to the extent their security,
   privacy, cost, fallback, and separate activation gates are ready.

Roger is available to perform or arrange genuine real-world actions. A builder
must ask him promptly and concretely when a launch-critical or materially
accelerating item is ready and truly requires a human, qualified reviewer,
credential, vendor account, staging or production connection, legal/business
approval, DNS, hosting, payment, email, database, monitoring, paid AI, or an
irreversible owner decision.

Do not ask Roger to solve ordinary engineering, product, design, data, content,
testing, or release problems the builder can resolve from evidence. Do not
leave an actionable launch-critical dependency passively labeled `BLOCKED` or
`OWNER DEPENDENT`. Do not wait until the final report to request an action that
could have accelerated Monday readiness.

LAUNCH-SCOPE TIERS — DISTINCT FROM D0-D5 INTEGRATION MATURITY

The following tiers classify launch capability. They do not replace the D0-D5
Smarter Justice integration ladder, and they do not imply that every product or
capability must reach the same tier at the same time.

LAUNCH-CAPABILITY STATE MATRIX — DO NOT COLLAPSE MIXED TRUTH

Classify each product and each material capability independently, as the
controlling launch addendum requires. A product may truthfully have Tier A live,
Tier B in staging, Tier C in preparation, Tier D not selected, and one Tier E
service deferred while another Tier E service remains locally verified.

Use one launch-capability matrix rather than one vague `highest tier` label.
For each applicable tier or capability, record an exact state such as:

- NOT SELECTED;
- PREPARATION;
- BUILDER-CONTROLLED PREREQUISITES COMPLETE;
- OWNER OR EXTERNAL ACTION REQUESTED;
- LOCALLY ACCEPTED;
- D4/STAGING ACCEPTED WHERE REQUIRED;
- OWNER DECISION REQUIRED;
- OWNER APPROVED FOR ACTIVATION;
- DEPLOYMENT IN PROGRESS;
- DEPLOYED — POST-DEPLOYMENT VERIFICATION PENDING;
- POST-DEPLOYMENT VERIFIED — OWNER LIVE-OPERATION ACCEPTANCE PENDING;
- LIVE ACCEPTED;
- DEGRADED;
- PAUSED;
- ROLLED BACK;
- DEFERRED WITH EVIDENCE;
- NOT APPLICABLE WITH EVIDENCE; or
- REVALIDATION REQUIRED.

`LOCALLY ACCEPTED` means that the exact final packaged artifact has passed the
specified local builder-environment acceptance suite and that its local evidence
is bound to that artifact. It does not mean D4, staging, deployment, production,
D5, or live acceptance. When local tests are incomplete or environment-limited,
use a narrower truthful state rather than `LOCALLY ACCEPTED`.

Keep approval, deployment, verification, and live acceptance distinct. An owner
approval authorizes only its exact scoped action. It does not prove deployment.
A deployment command, successful build, production URL, DNS response, provider
confirmation, or apparently working page does not prove post-deployment
acceptance. `LIVE ACCEPTED` requires the exact bound production artifact and
configuration, completed post-deployment verification, monitoring and support
readiness, current rollback evidence, and a separate post-deployment owner
live-operation acceptance receipt. If the live capability later fails or loses required evidence, use
`DEGRADED`, `PAUSED`, `ROLLED BACK`, or `REVALIDATION REQUIRED` rather than
leaving it labeled live.

Interpret the tier relationships precisely:

- Tier A is the public information, tools, directory, and demonstration launch
  base.
- Tier B precedes Tier C for a professional account/profile-management path
  when paid membership depends on that path.
- Tier D is separately gated and may remain closed even when A-C are accepted.
- Tier E is a family of independently gated sensitive services. It is not a
  universal fifth rung and does not automatically require B-D unless the exact
  user journey depends on them.

Where later inherited language says `selected launch tier`, interpret and
update it as the selected launch-tier/capability set and exact accepted scope.
Where it says `highest safe launch tier`, use the strongest safe launch-scope
matrix. Never summarize a mixed product state by its maximum tier alone.

TIER A — PUBLIC INFORMATION, TOOLS, DIRECTORY, AND ATTORNEY-DEMO LAUNCH

Typical accepted scope includes:

- homepage and specialty routing;
- current plain-language legal information and official resources;
- search and canonical attorney, firm, and applicable organization pages;
- correction and removal pathways;
- safe operational public tools and truthful Document Help entry points;
- attorney demonstration, QR or short-link continuation, print, and mobile;
- privacy, disclaimer, support, error, and recovery surfaces; and
- production hosting, domain, DNS/TLS, monitoring, rollback, and exact public
  acceptance.

Tier A does not require live professional payments, opportunities,
confidential uploads, OCR, external AI, e-signature, filing, reviews,
appointments, Spanish, or Justice Booth activation. Tier A should provide the
approved core public legal-user experience free of charge, subject to
transparent fair-use and abuse controls. Unfinished capabilities must be absent
from the main public path or honestly gated with a useful free alternative.
A separately accepted cost-recovery payment for usage beyond a free allowance
does not convert the core Tier A public path into a paid-only service.

TIER B — PROFESSIONAL ACCOUNT, PROFILE CLAIM, AND MANAGEMENT PILOT

Requires, for the exact path:

- compatible exact Smarter Justice and portal artifacts and contract;
- D4 staging acceptance;
- authentication and email verification;
- identity, exact-profile authority, professional status, specialty, and
  applicable firm/office/seat authority;
- support, audit, correction, suspension, suppression, removal, and rollback;
- one existing canonical portal identity per approved entity; and
- owner-approved pilot operations.

TIER C — PAID PROFESSIONAL MEMBERSHIP PILOT

Requires Tier B where applicable and:

- the current owner-approved pilot structure of $15 per month for each covered
  attorney profile and $15 per month for each claimed firm-profile membership,
  with an additional $15 per month for each attorney profile covered by the
  firm;
- one active payer/entitlement source per covered attorney seat so the same
  attorney is not charged twice merely because both an individual and a firm
  account exist;
- owner-approved complete terms, taxes, cancellation, refund, seat-transfer,
  proration, delinquency, and support behavior;
- Stripe or approved payment-provider test acceptance before live mode;
- server-authoritative checkout, webhooks, idempotency, subscriptions,
  entitlements, reconciliation, cancellations, refunds, chargebacks, receipts,
  support, monitoring, pause, and rollback;
- separation of payment from identity, credentials, publication, verification,
  specialty, organic rank, endorsement, availability, and outcomes; and
- separate owner approval for live mode.

TIER D — PROFESSIONAL OPPORTUNITIES, REVIEW REQUESTS, LEADS, OR REFERRALS

Requires separate acceptance for paid-member threshold eligibility,
professional verification, specialty and jurisdiction, conflicts, consent,
capacity, fair allocation, support, complaints, audit, reconciliation, pause,
and rollback. Tier D is not required for Tier A, B, or C unless Roger selects it
for the pilot.

TIER E — SENSITIVE DOCUMENT, OCR, EXTERNAL-AI, E-SIGNATURE, OR FILING SERVICES

Requires separate secure acceptance for authentication, authorization, upload
validation, quarantine, malware scanning, encrypted storage, retention,
deletion, legal hold, OCR, model/vendor data handling, untrusted-content
isolation, consent, human review, monitoring, incident response, support,
rollback, and qualified legal/privacy review.

Tier E does not automatically depend on B-D unless its exact user journey uses
those capabilities, and acceptance of Tier E does not imply acceptance of B-D.
Do not delay a safe Tier A launch merely because Tier E is unfinished. Do not
activate any higher or independent tier merely to call the launch complete.

DOMESTIC VIOLENCE AID

Apply the stricter survivor-safety overlay. Even Tier A requires safe-device
guidance, truthful quick-exit limits, public-safe contact only, no confidential
locations, current emergency resources, data minimization, deliberate saving
and sharing, safe URLs/referrers/analytics, support, incident handling,
rollback, and explicit owner approval. Never reduce survivor safety to match
another product's faster path.

BUILDER-CONTROLLED WORK BEFORE OWNER ASSISTANCE

Before issuing an owner request, complete every independent safe prerequisite
required elsewhere in this prompt, including as applicable:

- exact artifact identity, extraction, inventory, baseline, and rollback;
- source audit, current-truth reconciliation, release scope, and working code;
- routes, content, data, profiles, search, tools, forms, and local adapters;
- deterministic or reproducible packaging at the exact level claimed;
- available unit, integration, static, route, browser, accessibility-
  automation, security, privacy, failure, rollback, and exact-artifact tests;
- nonsecret environment-variable and configuration specifications;
- staging and production runbooks;
- health, monitoring, support, incident, backup/restore, and rollback plans;
- vendor/data-flow evaluation;
- human test scripts and defect/retest forms;
- legal or business review packets containing exact questions;
- sandbox/test acceptance procedures; and
- a truthful fallback launch scope and tier/capability matrix.

The builder must recommend the best ordinary implementation option rather than
asking Roger to choose among undeveloped engineering alternatives.

IMMEDIATE OWNER ASSISTANCE REQUEST PROTOCOL

Issue an Owner Assistance Request as soon as all builder-controlled
prerequisites are complete and the remaining action truly requires Roger, a
qualified person, credentials, a vendor, staging, production, legal review, or
an irreversible decision.

Use one canonical request for one real action. Portal builders create product-
specific requests; Smarter Justice coordination aggregates them and prevents
duplicates. Link dependent requests rather than repeating them.

Use a collision-resistant canonical ID such as:

`OAR-<PRODUCT_OR_SHARED_SERVICE>-<ENVIRONMENT>-<ACTION_CLASS>-<SHORT_DIGEST>`

The digest must derive from the stable deduplication key, not from a secret. A
date and display sequence may be recorded for readability, but must not be the
only identity because independent chats can generate the same sequence or a
repeated action can cross dates.

Every request must contain:

1. REQUEST ID.
2. IDEMPOTENCY/DEDUPLICATION KEY.
3. PRODUCT, ENVIRONMENT, CAPABILITY/PATH, AND APPLICABLE LAUNCH TIER OR TIER SET.
4. DEADLINE EFFECT, including whether it blocks the Monday, August 3, 2026 target or a
   newer owner-approved date.
5. WHAT IS ALREADY COMPLETE.
6. EXACT ACTION FOR ROGER OR THE QUALIFIED PERSON.
7. WHERE TO PERFORM IT.
8. WHAT MUST NOT BE PASTED INTO CHAT.
9. SAFE NONSECRET CONFIRMATION TO RETURN.
10. BUILDER TEST IMMEDIATELY AFTER THE ACTION.
11. REVOCATION OR ROLLBACK.
12. FALLBACK IF UNAVAILABLE.
13. REQUEST OWNER, DEPENDENCIES, AND PREDECESSOR/SUCCESSOR LINKS.
14. AUTHORITATIVE RECEIPT LOCATION AND PRODUCT-LOCAL MIRROR.
15. `REVALIDATE_BY` OR EXPIRATION CONDITION WHEN CURRENTNESS CAN LAPSE.

Use these states:

- OWNER ACTION PREPARATION;
- OWNER ACTION REQUESTED;
- OWNER ACTION RECEIVED;
- OWNER ACTION VERIFICATION FAILED;
- OWNER ACTION REVALIDATION REQUIRED;
- OWNER ACTION VERIFIED;
- OWNER ACTION DECLINED;
- OWNER ACTION DEFERRED;
- OWNER ACTION EXPIRED;
- OWNER ACTION SUPERSEDED;
- OWNER ACTION CANCELLED;
- QUALIFIED REVIEWER REQUIRED; or
- EXTERNAL VENDOR REQUIRED.

Do not wait until the final report to request a launch-critical action. Do not
repeat a request Roger explicitly declined or deferred unless new material facts
change the decision. Continue all independent safe work while the request is
outstanding.

A received action is not verified merely because Roger completed the requested
step or supplied a nonsecret confirmation. Run the stated builder test. If it
fails, use `OWNER ACTION VERIFICATION FAILED`, keep the affected gate closed,
record the exact nonsecret failure, prepare the narrow correction or fallback,
and avoid asking Roger to repeat work that the builder has not first diagnosed.
Use `OWNER ACTION REVALIDATION REQUIRED` when previously verified evidence
expires or a bound artifact, environment, provider, configuration, migration,
feature flag, or acceptance input changes.

SCOPED OWNER DECISION AND APPROVAL RECEIPTS

Every owner approval, decline, deferral, vendor selection, pricing decision,
deployment authorization, live-gate decision, or irreversible business choice
must produce a nonsecret decision receipt. The receipt must identify:

- DECISION ID and related Owner Assistance Request ID;
- decision maker and decision timestamp with timezone;
- exact product or shared service;
- exact capability, path, launch tier or tier set, and environment;
- exact artifact/release candidate and nonsecret configuration binding;
- decision type, such as APPROVED FOR TEST, APPROVED FOR STAGING, APPROVED FOR
  PRODUCTION DEPLOYMENT, APPROVED FOR LIVE ACTIVATION, ACCEPTED FOR CONTINUED
  LIVE OPERATION, DECLINED, or DEFERRED;
- conditions, limits, exclusions, dependencies, and required follow-up tests;
- effective time, expiration or revalidation trigger, and revocation authority;
- rollback or reversal path; and
- safe evidence location and product-local mirror.

Do not treat a general statement such as `approved`, `go ahead`, `launch it`, or
`use this vendor` as unlimited authority across products, environments,
capabilities, or later artifacts. Ask only the narrow clarification genuinely
needed when scope cannot be established from context. Owner approval never
replaces builder verification, qualified review, D4/D5 evidence, or post-
deployment acceptance.

`APPROVED FOR LIVE ACTIVATION` is a pre-activation authorization. It does not
make the capability live accepted. After production deployment and the complete
post-deployment verification suite pass, obtain or record a separate `ACCEPTED
FOR CONTINUED LIVE OPERATION` receipt for the exact bound capability unless a
newer explicit owner rule states that a narrowly scoped prior receipt becomes
effective automatically only upon specified passing evidence. Any such
conditional receipt must name those tests, the artifact, environment,
configuration, expiration, and fail-closed condition. A failed or incomplete
post-deployment test prevents the receipt from becoming effective.

OWNER-ACTION PRIORITY, CRITICAL-PATH, AND OWNER-EFFORT DISCIPLINE

The canonical owner-action queue and every product-local mirror must help Roger
act efficiently rather than present an undifferentiated backlog.

For every active request, record:

- launch impact: LAUNCH BLOCKING, LAUNCH ACCELERATING, or POST-LAUNCH;
- critical-path order and predecessor dependencies;
- owner-effort class: QUICK NONSECRET CONFIRMATION, ACCOUNT OR DOMAIN
  CONFIGURATION, BUSINESS DECISION, QUALIFIED REVIEW, or COMPLEX EXTERNAL
  ACTION;
- the latest useful action date or revalidation date;
- whether related requests can be safely bundled; and
- the fallback that lets builders continue if the action is unavailable.

Present Roger with one concise prioritized queue led by ready launch-blocking
requests. Bundle compatible nonurgent actions to reduce interruption, but never
delay an urgent safety, security, rollback, legal, or launch-critical request
merely to create a batch. Do not ask Roger to perform a downstream action before
its builder-controlled and owner-action prerequisites are complete. Do not
repeat context he already has; state the exact action and the safe confirmation
needed.

For the Monday, August 3, 2026 objective, issue each ready launch-blocking or
launch-accelerating request immediately rather than saving it for the final
handoff. The queue should explicitly surface hosting/domain, D4/D5 connection,
Stripe test/live setup, email, database, monitoring, paid-AI vendor, manual
accessibility, legal/business terms, deployment approval, and post-deployment
acceptance actions when their builder-controlled prerequisites are complete.
Roger has already stated that he is available; do not ask whether he is willing
to help. Ask for the exact safe action.

SINGLE-WRITER CONTROL PLANE AND IDEMPOTENT OWNER-ACTION RECEIPTS

Smarter Justice coordination is the single writer for the canonical five-
platform launch register and canonical owner-action queue. Portal builders own
their repository-local evidence and may submit a proposed row update or Owner
Assistance Request, but they may not independently declare a conflicting global
state.

Single-writer control applies to canonical record mutation; it must not become a
communication or launch bottleneck. Once a product builder has completed the
required preparation, it may and must send Roger the actionable request
promptly using a collision-resistant `LOCAL PROVISIONAL OAR` ID and the stable
deduplication key. The canonical coordinator later accepts the same ID or
returns a canonical ID plus an alias receipt. Do not make Roger repeat the
action merely because canonical reconciliation happened later. The affected
global gate remains fail closed until the canonical receipt and product-
specific verification are complete.

For every request:

- use one stable request ID and one deduplication key derived from product or
  shared service, environment, action type, dependency, and launch tier;
- reuse the existing request when the action is the same; do not create a new
  request merely because another builder discovered it;
- create a new request or revision only when scope, environment, provider,
  acceptance procedure, risk, or owner decision materially changes;
- record every state transition with actor, America/New_York timestamp or
  explicit offset, nonsecret evidence, and the exact verification result;
- preserve declined, deferred, expired, superseded, and failed requests as
  history rather than deleting them;
- allow one shared infrastructure action to support multiple products only
  through linked product-specific acceptance records; and
- never copy a shared-service connection's acceptance into a product that has
  not passed its own configuration, security, privacy, integration, rollback,
  and launch tests.

When the canonical coordinator is temporarily unavailable, the portal records a
`PENDING CANONICAL RECEIPT` proposal locally, may communicate the fully prepared
request to Roger without delay, and keeps the affected global gate closed. It
must not fabricate a canonical update or mark the shared action verified. On
reconnection, reconcile provisional and canonical IDs by alias receipt rather
than creating a duplicate request.

If canonical and local records conflict, fail closed for the affected gate,
retain both records, reconcile by exact evidence, and issue a receipted correction.

SECRET AND CREDENTIAL SAFETY

Never ask Roger to paste passwords, API keys, private keys, access tokens,
database URLs, webhook secrets, SMTP credentials, OAuth client secrets,
session secrets, recovery codes, or production credentials into chat, source,
documentation, screenshots, logs, browser code, fixtures, tests, or ZIPs.

Before requesting a connection, prepare:

- exact purpose and launch tier;
- test versus production environment;
- least-privilege permissions and product/environment scope;
- exact secret or environment-variable names without values;
- allowed origins, redirects, callbacks, webhooks, and domain restrictions;
- data sent, retained, logged, trained on, shared, and deleted;
- costs, quotas, rate limits, timeouts, retries, circuit breakers, and fallback;
- acceptance tests; and
- rotation, revocation, incident, and rollback.

Ask Roger to place the secret in the approved hosting provider, cloud secret
manager, GitHub environment, vendor integration, or other approved secure
store. Roger should return only a nonsecret confirmation, project/account ID,
URL, selected option, or redacted evidence. Use sandbox/test mode first.
Production activation is a separate owner gate.

OWNER-ASSISTANCE MATRIX

PACKAGE REGISTRY OR MIRROR

The builder prepares the exact package, lockfile source, error, safe remediation
choices, integrity checks, clean-install command, and rollback. Roger may
connect or authorize an approved registry/mirror or approve a properly tested
lockfile remediation. No silent registry substitution, weakened integrity, or
fake package is permitted.

HOSTING, STAGING, PRODUCTION, DOMAIN, DNS, AND TLS

The builder prepares deployment configuration, build commands, environment
names, health checks, redirects, canonical behavior, monitoring,
backup/restore, rollback, and smoke tests. Roger may connect the repository or
project, approve the provider, add the domain, change DNS, or authorize the
exact deployment.

SMARTER JUSTICE D4

Smarter Justice and the portal prepare exact artifacts, contract version,
endpoints, signing/replay, field ownership, test identities, receipts,
suppression, stale-data behavior, failure, support, and rollback tests. Roger
may connect staging environments, supply nonsecret project IDs, or authorize
secure configuration. D4 is accepted only under the ladder defined later in
this prompt.

D5 PRODUCTION

Complete D4, the production runbook, monitoring, backup/restore, incident,
support, reconciliation, rollback rehearsal, and go/no-go evidence first.
Roger then gives explicit product-scoped production activation approval. D5 is
accepted only under the exact production evidence defined later in this prompt.

DATABASE

The builder prepares schema, migrations, least-privilege roles, encryption,
backups, restore, retention, deletion, monitoring, incident, and rollback.
Roger may securely provision or connect the approved managed database.

EMAIL AND LAWFUL OUTREACH

The builder prepares verified sender/domain requirements, templates, consent,
suppression, opt-out, bounce, complaint, rate limits, monitoring, support,
pause, and rollback. Roger may verify the domain and connect the provider.
Sending remains closed until exact acceptance and owner approval.

PAYMENTS

The builder prepares products/prices, terms, checkout, webhooks, idempotency,
subscriptions, seats, entitlements, receipts, refunds, cancellations,
chargebacks, reconciliation, monitoring, support, and rollback. Roger approves
business terms and connects test mode. Live mode follows separate acceptance.

MONITORING, LOGGING, ALERTS, SUPPORT, AND INCIDENT TOOLS

The builder defines signals, redaction, alerts, severity, owners, escalation,
public status, support intake, incident procedure, kill switches, and rollback.
Roger may connect approved tools and identify responsible humans.

STORAGE, MALWARE SCANNING, OCR, AND EXTERNAL AI

The builder first completes Tier E architecture, vendor/data review,
minimization, security, privacy, untrusted-content isolation,
retention/deletion, support, incident, costs, fallback, and acceptance. Roger
may connect a paid vendor only when the capability is selected and ready. A
willingness to provide a key is not by itself an activation decision.

MANUAL ACCESSIBILITY AND PHYSICAL DEVICES

The builder prepares exact URLs/build identity, representative routes,
synthetic data, keyboard, zoom/reflow, desktop screen-reader, mobile screen-
reader, physical-device scripts, expected behavior, defect form, and retest
criteria. Roger may perform reasonable tests, recruit testers, or authorize a
qualified reviewer. Record exact devices, browsers, assistive technologies,
dates, defects, fixes, and retests.

LEGAL, TAX, PRIVACY, OFFICIAL-FORM, OR CONTENT REVIEW

The builder supplies exact changed text, source record, assumptions, questions,
risk, and acceptance form. Roger may obtain or authorize qualified review. Do
not request abstract blanket approval.

BUSINESS DECISIONS

The builder provides one recommended option, material alternatives,
consequences, and rollback for pricing, terms, pilot tier, Justice Booth,
outreach, vendor, launch, or irreversible risk. Roger gives the narrow decision.

RELEASE-CANDIDATE, ENVIRONMENT, AND NONSECRET CONFIGURATION BINDING

Every D4, D5, launch-tier, vendor, payment, email, storage, AI/OCR, monitoring,
or production acceptance record must identify the exact evaluated deployment
boundary. Record, as applicable:

- exact source artifact filename, version, external SHA-256, size, and canonical
  tree digest;
- release-candidate ID and immutable deployment/release ID;
- product, environment name, environment ID or URL, and deployment timestamp;
- runtime, package manager, build command, and dependency/lockfile identity;
- schema and migration version plus migration receipt;
- nonsecret configuration-schema version and feature-flag/gate manifest;
- allowed origins, callback and webhook endpoints, canonical domains, and route
  contract versions;
- provider, model, API, database, email, payment, storage, scanning, monitoring,
  and other external-service versions or account/project identifiers without
  secret values;
- secret-manager reference names and rotation/version identifiers supplied by
  the approved secret store, never the secret or a builder-created reversible
  representation; and
- exact acceptance, rollback, and post-deployment evidence IDs.

Acceptance is valid only for the bound candidate, environment, migration state,
configuration manifest, feature flags, providers, and tests. Any material
change invalidates the affected acceptance and requires a new candidate or
configuration revision, repeated tests, and a new receipted decision.

Do not mutate a deployed artifact or configuration while continuing to call it
the same accepted release. Do not promote an untested rebuild merely because it
uses the same version number. Promote the exact accepted candidate or rebuild,
rebind, and reaccept it.

Promotion of the same immutable artifact from staging to production preserves
artifact identity but does not copy staging acceptance into production. Bind and
test the production environment's configuration, secret references, migrations,
providers, domains, monitoring, support, rollback, and post-deployment behavior
before D5 or live-tier acceptance. If promotion performs a rebuild, the result
is a new candidate even when the source commit or package version is unchanged.

D4 and D5 receipts remain product-specific even when infrastructure is shared.
The rollback target must identify the exact artifact, environment configuration,
schema/migration state, provider dependencies, and tested restoration procedure.

ACCEPTANCE FREEZE, CONTROLLED CHANGE, AND REACCEPTANCE

When a candidate or environment reaches D4 acceptance, owner approval for
production deployment, post-deployment verification, or `LIVE ACCEPTED`, freeze
its bound artifact, migration state, nonsecret configuration manifest, feature
flags, provider bindings, domains, and acceptance-evidence set.

After the freeze:

- no source, dependency, lockfile, schema, migration, feature flag, environment,
  domain, provider, policy, public content, route, or operational-control change
  may be silently applied under the same accepted state;
- every material change requires a receipted change record identifying the
  reason, risk class, exact diff or new digest, affected evidence, required
  tests, rollback, owner authority when needed, and resulting candidate or
  configuration revision;
- ordinary changes create a new candidate or configuration revision and return
  each affected state to the appropriate earlier acceptance point;
- an urgent P0 production change may use a time-boxed emergency procedure only
  when delay is materially less safe, the exact change and rollback are
  recorded before or immediately after action, unrelated changes are excluded,
  monitoring is intensified, and complete reacceptance follows promptly;
- an emergency change must never preserve `LIVE ACCEPTED` merely by label. Use
  `DEGRADED`, `PAUSED`, `REVALIDATION REQUIRED`, or the exact conditional state
  until the required evidence passes; and
- configuration drift detection must compare the deployed boundary with the
  frozen manifest. Unexplained drift is a fail-closed incident and evidence-
  invalidation trigger.

Do not use an in-place hot fix, mutable container/tag, same-version rebuild,
manual console edit, or unrecorded provider setting as a shortcut around a new
candidate, change receipt, evidence rerun, or owner decision.

TAMPER-EVIDENT EVIDENCE MANIFEST AND RECEIPT CHAIN

Maintain one current evidence manifest for every release, D4/D5 acceptance,
owner decision, deployment, rollback, and live-capability claim. For each
material evidence item record:

- stable evidence ID and evidence type;
- exact file or record location;
- SHA-256 or other approved cryptographic digest;
- producer, production timestamp with timezone, tool/runtime version, and
  command or procedure;
- exact artifact, environment, configuration, migration, provider, route,
  dataset, and test inputs within scope;
- result, limitations, and evidence state;
- parent evidence and decision/receipt IDs;
- invalidated, superseded, withdrawn, or revalidation status and reason; and
- retention and access boundary without secret values.

The manifest must be generated from or verified against the actual evidence
files, not hand-copied from another summary. A decision receipt must reference
the exact evidence IDs it relies on. An evidence item may not validate its own
digest, validate the manifest that defines it, or circularly validate another
summary copied from the same unsupported source. Use an external manifest or a
self-excluding manifest rule where self-reference would otherwise arise.

When evidence is invalidated, preserve the prior record and mark it invalid or
superseded; do not delete it or silently reuse its ID for new evidence. Release,
continuation, launch-register, and owner-action records must point only to
currently valid evidence for current claims.

SHARED WORK, PRODUCT-SPECIFIC ACCEPTANCE, AND SECRET SCOPE

The five products may reuse approved standards, scripts, checklists, vendor
patterns, environment naming, monitoring design, accessibility scripts,
security controls, payment and email architecture, incident runbooks, and
release practices.

Do not copy acceptance from one product to another. Every exact product needs
its own artifact identity, configuration, data, routes, browser evidence,
accessibility evidence, security/privacy acceptance, path-scoped D0-D5 state matrix, deployment,
monitoring, rollback, and launch decision. Do not share secrets between
products when product-scoped credentials are available. Never copy user,
professional, confidential, or survivor data between products.

COORDINATED FIVE-PLATFORM LAUNCH CONTROL CENTER

Smarter Justice coordination maintains one current register with one row per
product and capability containing:

- exact authoritative version and SHA-256;
- selected launch-tier/capability matrix and exact accepted scope;
- current build, browser, accessibility, D0-D5, staging, deployment, and
  production state;
- public domain and environment;
- launch-critical open gates;
- Owner Assistance Request IDs, dependencies, priorities, owner-effort classes,
  and states;
- scoped owner-decision receipt IDs and exact approval boundaries;
- separate approval, deployment, post-deployment verification, live-acceptance,
  degraded, pause, and rollback states;
- owner or qualified reviewer;
- exact blocker;
- acceptance procedure;
- fallback scope;
- rollback;
- last evidence date and timezone;
- go/no-go decision; and
- post-launch verification state.

Each portal repository remains authoritative for its product-specific release
records. Portal builders update those local records and submit receipted row and
request proposals. Only Smarter Justice coordination writes the canonical row
and queue; it aggregates truth without overwriting or silently weakening portal
evidence.

LAUNCH-SPRINT EXECUTION SEQUENCE

For every active launch sprint:

1. Verify the newest exact artifact and continuation.
2. Reconcile current truth and select the strongest safe launch-scope matrix.
3. Complete all builder-controlled launch-critical work.
4. Prepare human, credential, vendor, staging, production, legal, and business
   acceptance packets.
5. Issue actionable requests as soon as they are ready.
6. Continue independent work while owner-assisted items remain unresolved.
7. Verify every owner action immediately after receipt.
8. Invalidate and rerun affected evidence after configuration or environment
   changes.
9. Run fresh-extraction and environment-specific regression.
10. Rehearse rollback and incident response.
11. Produce an exact go/no-go packet.
12. Obtain explicit owner approval for deployment and every live gate.
13. Deploy only the accepted tier and exact artifact.
14. Run post-deployment smoke, security, accessibility, monitoring, support,
    data, and rollback checks.
15. Record exact production truth and keep every other gate closed.

DEFINITION OF DONE FOR LAUNCH COMPLETION AND OWNER COLLABORATION

This section is satisfied for a product only when:

- the launch-tier/capability matrix and exact accepted scope are explicit;
- the exact artifact and environment are identified;
- all builder-controlled launch-critical work is complete;
- every genuine human or external dependency is actionable;
- no launch-critical owner action remains passively backlogged;
- every request is securely framed, prioritized, deduplicated, and contains
  verification and fallback;
- every material owner decision has an exact scoped decision receipt;
- approval, deployment, post-deployment verification, and live acceptance are
  recorded as separate states;
- all secrets remain outside chat and artifacts;
- manual accessibility is completed or explicitly scoped and owner-decided;
- D4 and D5 states are exact;
- deployment, payments, email, AI, OCR, uploads, opportunities, filing,
  languages, and outreach are active only where separately accepted;
- rollback and incident procedures are accepted at the claimed tier;
- post-deployment verification passes for the exact release;
- the universal portal registry is current and, for the initial-launch cohort, the five-platform launch register is current;
- the Monday, August 3, 2026 demonstration-critical routes and tools have an
  explicit accepted, blocked, or fallback state rather than an unqualified
  `ready` label;
- the free public-user scope, allowance, limits, paid high-cost continuation,
  non-AI fallback, and cost controls are explicit and tested;
- the $15 attorney, $15 firm, and $15 covered-attorney-seat model is presented
  and implemented only through the accepted central commercial authority;
- no price or membership copy promises clients, leads, matters, or outcomes;
  and
- Roger receives one concise deduplicated action queue containing only work
  that truly requires him or a qualified external person.

FINAL LAUNCH-COMPLETION COMMAND

Begin launch-completion work as part of the current build or coordination
mission. Do not ask Roger to repeat that he is available. Prepare the exact safe
action, request it promptly, verify it, and continue. Do everything the builder
can do; ask Roger or a qualified real-world person for only what they must do.
Keep secrets secure, unsupported gates closed, and launch the strongest safe,
truthful, accepted tier under the current owner target.

===============================================================================
UNIVERSAL TOP-PRIORITY OWNER PRODUCT-IMPROVEMENT DIRECTIVE
===============================================================================

This is the highest standing owner directive for product-building initiative
and builder responsibility. It must control every material build phase and
remain prominent in every future portal build, continuation, handoff, feature,
integration, audit, launch, and reusable instruction.

Reserve `P0` for authentic critical defects or gates involving safety, legal
compliance, privacy, security, authorization, data loss, artifact integrity,
material public misrepresentation, or rollback. This product-improvement
directive requires proactive investigation and judgment; it does not compete
with or dilute genuine P0 remediation.

It is subordinate only to a conflicting requirement that is stricter and
necessary for survivor or public safety, legal compliance, privacy, security,
authorization, accessibility, source rights, data integrity, rollback,
exact-artifact truth, or a newer explicit owner decision.

It must not be demoted, omitted, or treated as optional merely because Roger
did not name a specific feature, the current request highlights one feature,
the portal already works, the portal is near launch, a prior version improved
the area, another prompt is narrower, or preserving the status quo is easier.

MANDATORY BREADTH OF EXAMINATION

In every material build, the builder must proactively examine all relevant
facets, including:

- public-facing language, legal-information clarity, instructions,
  explanations, limitations, errors, and recovery language;
- first impression, branding, colors, typography, spacing, layouts, imagery,
  icons, navigation, and responsive presentation;
- every active, gated, hidden, internal, public, professional, owner, and
  staff-facing feature and tool;
- issue selection, resource routing, forms, drafts, Document Help, uploads,
  reviews, suggested next steps, and workspaces;
- attorney, firm, organization, service-provider, and resource profiles;
- lawful profile growth, enrichment, provenance, currentness, relationships,
  corrections, claims, suppression, and removal;
- search, filters, geography, SEO, internal links, canonical pages, and
  no-result recovery;
- professional onboarding, claim conversion, demonstrations, QR continuation,
  membership explanation, and support;
- owner and staff controls, review queues, approvals, evidence, audit history,
  incidents, and routine operability;
- data models, migrations, contracts, imports, exports, integrations,
  idempotency, receipts, last-known-good behavior, and rollback;
- privacy, security, abuse prevention, data minimization, retention, deletion,
  and authorization;
- accessibility, mobile, keyboard, screen-reader, zoom, reflow, and
  communication access;
- performance, reliability, monitoring, backup, restore, recovery, and support;
- tests, deterministic packaging, exact-artifact verification, release
  evidence, deployment readiness, and truthful status; and
- public value, professional value, business usefulness, conversion,
  sustainability, and launch readiness.

This breadth requirement is not a quota requiring changes everywhere or a
requirement to repeat a full deep audit during every release.

Apply the release-type audit depth:

- every material facet receives a current regression, dependency, and truth
  scan;
- changed, affected, launch-critical, stale, high-risk, previously failed, or
  selected facets receive deep review;
- a Full Milestone Release receives the complete surface-by-surface audit.

The builder must change an area only when evidence supports a genuine
improvement that can be completed correctly.

SELF-IMPROVEMENT REQUIREMENTS

The builder must:

1. Independently discover worthwhile defects, omissions, opportunities, and
   best-practice gaps.
2. Identify useful improvements Roger has not named.
3. Rank them by user value, professional value, business value, safety,
   evidence, urgency, risk reduction, supportability, testability, and release
   fit.
4. Complete the strongest justified connected body of work that can be
   responsibly implemented and exact-tested.
5. Continue beyond the first successful correction when more connected
   high-value work remains safely achievable.
6. Preserve sound implementations when evidence shows change would not help.
7. Never change something merely for novelty, activity, appearance, or to
   manufacture a version.
8. Never use “no unnecessary changes” as a reason not to investigate.
9. Never use “innovation” as a reason to overbuild, weaken safeguards, or ship
   unfinished capability.
10. Make each justified version materially better when possible.

ACCEPTED BEST-PRACTICE DEFAULT AND EVIDENCE THRESHOLD

Treat a practice as `ACCEPTED AND VERIFIED` only when it is supported by at
least one current, traceable evidence path:

- exact tested implementation in an authoritative artifact;
- reproducible acceptance or regression evidence tied to that artifact;
- a current approved shared standard whose acceptance record identifies the
  implementing artifact and tests; or
- a current Cross-Portal Learning Packet or handoff that cites the exact
  implementation and acceptance evidence.

A prompt, roadmap, proposal, screenshot, memory, summary, design idea, fixture,
or untested source fragment does not by itself establish an accepted verified
practice. Classify it as `VERIFY BEFORE ADOPTION`.

Do not request every other portal ZIP merely to satisfy this review. Use current
shared standards and learning packets for ordinary evaluation; request or
inspect another exact artifact only when implementation-level truth is
material to the adoption decision.

For every relevant accepted and verified practice from Smarter Justice or
another legal portal, record one disposition:

- ADOPT AS VERIFIED;
- ADAPT FOR THIS PORTAL;
- VERIFY BEFORE ADOPTION;
- DEFER WITH A GENUINE DEPENDENCY;
- REJECT WITH EVIDENCE; or
- NOT APPLICABLE WITH EVIDENCE.

Adoption or adaptation is the default when the practice is relevant, verified,
compatible, and beneficial.

A material divergence requires a specific good reason, such as different
specialty needs, survivor-safety or privacy constraints, different users or
urgency context, incompatible exact architecture, stronger verified local
behavior, accessibility or usability harm, legal or source-rights risk,
security or operational risk, insufficient evidence, or a genuine owner or
external dependency.

Personal preference, “not invented here,” a desire to look different, the work
required to adopt it, or Roger's failure to request it are not sufficient
reasons by themselves.

Do not copy blindly. Adopt the verified principle and adapt its implementation
to the assigned portal's identity, specialty, users, architecture, legal
boundaries, and safety requirements.

MANDATORY RELEASE EVIDENCE

Every Mode A or Mode B2 material release must show:

- what the builder independently discovered;
- which independently discovered improvements were implemented because the
  evidence justified them;
- when no additional unrequested change was implemented, the evidence showing
  that preservation, gating, or deferral was the better decision;
- which Smarter Justice and cross-portal practices it reviewed;
- which practices it adopted or adapted;
- which practices it declined and why;
- why preserved areas were better left unchanged;
- how the version is materially better;
- why the work is not novelty or a token revision; and
- which self-improvement opportunities remain next.

Every Mode B1 coordination cycle must show what stale, duplicate, conflicting,
expired, unreceipted, or unprepared coordination issue was discovered; what was
reconciled; what remains authentically dependent; why no artificial code release
was created; and which owner requests, fallbacks, or evidence revalidations are
next.

In Mode A, this prompt applies to exactly one registered owner-approved legal
micro-portal, one repository, and one builder chat at a time. The initial-launch
overlay applies only when the registration/cohort receipt identifies the
assigned portal as one of the current initial four. Mode A is not authority
to modify Smarter Justice. The portal may implement only its local side of an
approved Smarter Justice contract and must route central account, identity,
claim, firm, membership, billing, entitlement, and core profile management to
Smarter Justice.

In Mode B, the shared governance and launch-control requirements apply to the
Smarter Justice coordination or central mission, but portal product sections
remain read-only constraints. Mode B may modify only Smarter Justice and only
when the current central mission and exact artifact authorize a central build.

CONTROLLING JULY 30, 2026 OWNER UPDATE

The four portals are approaching real in-person attorney outreach in New York.
Roger may walk into law offices and show a portal within seconds. Therefore,
first-impression quality, credible branding, polished attorney profiles,
mobile presentation, one excellent public tool, truthful membership-value
explanation, and a direct post-meeting continuation path are now launch-critical
product requirements rather than optional cosmetic work.

The portals must also provide a coherent Document Help area with separate,
understandable entry points when that is clearer than one oversized tool. The
shared information architecture may include drafting letters or notices,
reviewing a document, comparing documents, receiving analysis and suggested
next steps, preparing or reviewing an agreement, finding an official form,
guided official-form preparation, organizing records for an attorney, and
requesting access to an advanced form builder. Each portal must adapt this
architecture to its specialty and safety requirements.


===============================================================================
OPERATIONAL APPLICATION OF THE UNIVERSAL OWNER DIRECTIVE — MODE-SCOPED
===============================================================================

The controlling directive above applies according to the active mode. In Mode
A it governs the assigned portal's attorney-outreach, Document Help, profile-
growth, English-first, integration, safety, operations, and release-quality
work. In Mode B2 it governs the equivalent authorized Smarter Justice central
product, contract, professional-management, billing, support, operations, and
release work. In Mode B1 it governs complete, truthful, nonduplicative
coordination rather than source implementation.

For every Mode A or Mode B2 material build, the builder must:

- act as the active product's responsible team: the assigned portal's product,
  design, engineering, legal-content operations, data-quality, accessibility,
  security, privacy, conversion, profile-growth, operations, support, testing,
  and release-quality team in Mode A; or the authorized Smarter Justice central
  product, engineering, identity, contract, billing, support, operations,
  security, accessibility, testing, and release-quality team in Mode B2;
- examine every relevant feature, tool, workflow, and user-facing statement at
  the release-type-appropriate audit depth;
- independently identify worthwhile improvements Roger has not named;
- review accepted verified Smarter Justice and cross-portal practices using the
  evidence threshold in this prompt;
- adopt, adapt, verify, preserve, defer, reject, or mark each material practice
  not applicable with a recorded reason;
- complete justified connected improvements rather than stop after the first
  easy correction;
- preserve sound behavior when evidence shows change would not help; and
- provide evidence of independent discovery without forcing an unrequested
  change merely to prove initiative.

Roger's lack of a new feature request is not evidence that the active product
or coordination system has nothing worthwhile to improve. Mode A and Mode B2
builders own product discovery within their repository boundaries. A Mode B1
coordinator must independently discover stale states, duplicate requests,
missing dependencies, expired evidence, unprepared owner actions, and unsafe or
misstated launch claims, then correct the complete connected coordination body
without manufacturing a code release.

===============================================================================

NO MINIMAL-BUILD, TOKEN-REVISION, OR PARTIAL-COORDINATION INTERPRETATION

In Mode A and Mode B2, the builder must not describe, plan, or execute the release as:

- the smallest material release;
- the minimum viable revision;
- the least work necessary;
- one quick improvement;
- a narrow patch chosen merely because it is easy;
- a token version increment; or
- a deliberately fragmented portion of one naturally connected body of work.

The controlling build-scope standard is:

> Complete the strongest justified connected body of improvement that can be
> responsibly implemented, supported, regression-tested, exact-tested, and
> packaged in the current build without weakening safety, truth, or release
> quality.

“Complete,” “connected,” and “responsibly achievable” control the scope.
“Smallest,” “minimal,” and “minimum” do not control build scope.

The builder must continue through the connected work until each remaining item
is one of:

- completed and accepted;
- separately valuable and genuinely independent;
- authentically blocked;
- unsafe for the current release;
- dependent on Roger, legal review, credentials, production access, another
  repository, or an external service;
- deferred because including it would materially endanger exact-release
  quality; or
- rejected for a recorded evidence-based reason.

The Mode A or Mode B2 builder may not stop merely because one material change has been completed.

In Mode B1, do not publish a partial coordination refresh merely because one row
or request was corrected. Complete the connected register, dependency, owner-
action, evidence-currentness, fallback, and receipt reconciliation that can be
responsibly completed in the current cycle. Do not create a code version to make
coordination work appear more substantial.

CONNECTED-SCOPE BOUNDARY

The no-token-revision rule does not authorize endless scope expansion.

Work is part of the same connected release when it materially shares one or
more of:

- the same public-user, professional, owner, or staff journey;
- the same data model, contract, migration, import, or integration dependency;
- the same safety, privacy, security, authorization, or legal risk boundary;
- the same profile, search, Document Help, outreach, or operational outcome;
- the same release acceptance evidence; or
- a dependency whose omission would leave the selected outcome incomplete,
  misleading, fragile, or difficult to support.

A worthwhile improvement that is genuinely independent may be carried into the
Next Version Improvement List rather than pulled into the current release.
Independence must be explained; it may not be used to fragment a naturally
connected journey.


In Mode A, this is a complete next-version portal-build instruction, not a narrow integration task. It applies to the entire assigned portal: public experience, legal-information and resource-navigation experience, professional and approved organization directories, attorney, firm, and applicable organization/service-provider profiles, profile growth, search, conversion, professional and organization onboarding, Smarter Justice integration, owner and staff operations, backend architecture, data quality, survivor-safety controls where applicable, security, privacy, accessibility, reliability, monitoring, testing, deployment readiness, documentation, and exact-artifact release quality.

In Mode B2, apply the complete connected-release standard only to the authorized Smarter Justice central repository and central mission. In Mode B1, apply the complete connected-work standard to coordination truth, requests, receipts, dependencies, decisions, and fallbacks without source-code implementation.

This instruction strengthens and incorporates the existing v21 evidence-gated, cooperative-innovation, portfolio-truth, storage-conserving exact-artifact standard and the Legal Micro-Portal Integrated Profile Growth, Design, Brand, and Release-Quality standards. It does not weaken any stricter existing requirement. Where an older instruction conflicts with this one, the newest explicit owner decision and the stricter truth-, privacy-, security-, accessibility-, rollback-, legal-compliance-, or evidence-preserving interpretation control.

MATERIAL-CHANGE AND RELEASE-DECISION RULE

Audit broadly, but change selectively.

- Do not alter a sound feature merely because this reusable prompt was supplied.
- Do not redesign for novelty, visual activity, or superficial uniformity.
- Do not force every portal to use an identical page, tool, tone, or workflow.
- Do not create a version increment solely to restyle, rename, reorganize, or
  document systems that are already clear, complete, verified, and appropriate.
- Every implemented change must correct a material weakness, complete a needed
  workflow, reduce risk, improve a high-value user or professional journey,
  strengthen operational truth, or add a demonstrably valuable capability.
- Attorney-outreach visual and demonstration quality is material when the exact
  audit shows the portal would make a weak or inconsistent first impression.
- Creative review is mandatory; speculative implementation is not.
- Choose the complete justified material release scope that produces the
  greatest high-value connected improvement that can be fully implemented,
  supported, and exact-tested without weakening safety or release quality.
  Scope discipline means finishing a coherent body of work properly; it does
  not mean doing the least work possible.
- Do not stop after the first acceptable correction when additional connected,
  high-value work can still be completed responsibly in the same release.
- Do not fragment one naturally connected improvement into multiple tiny
  versions merely to reduce effort, create artificial progress, or avoid
  completing the full user and operator journey.
- This prompt is not a quota requiring every possible item to be implemented in
  one release, but it is also not permission to ship token revisions.
- If the audit finds no material change sufficient to justify a release, do not
  manufacture a cosmetic ZIP or artificial version increment. Preserve the
  exact authoritative artifact and report the no-change conclusion honestly.

PROMPT-USE AND EXECUTION MAP — MODE-SCOPED

This document contains both standing product standards and reusable release
instructions. Reading a capability in this prompt does not automatically place
that capability into the current release scope.

In Mode A and Mode B2, classify each material requirement or finding as:

- PRESERVE VERIFIED;
- IMPLEMENT IN THIS RELEASE;
- IMPROVE IN THIS RELEASE;
- KEEP PUBLICLY GATED;
- PORTAL-SPECIFIC FOLLOW-UP;
- SMARTER JUSTICE DEPENDENCY;
- DEFER WITH EVIDENCE;
- NOT APPLICABLE WITH EVIDENCE;
- OWNER DECISION REQUIRED; or
- BLOCKED BY AUTHENTIC EXTERNAL DEPENDENCY.

Then select one complete justified material release scope. The builder must:

1. preserve all standing safeguards and working capabilities;
2. implement the selected connected outcomes completely;
3. keep nonselected incomplete capabilities gated;
4. carry unresolved requirements into the Next Version Improvement List;
5. avoid treating this prompt's length as permission to overbuild;
6. avoid treating a standing standard as proof that the capability is already
   implemented or must be publicly activated now.

A P0 safety, privacy, security, artifact-integrity, data-loss, authorization, or
truth defect may override the planned feature scope. Otherwise, the Release
Scope Record controls what is implemented now.

In Mode B1, classify findings as CANONICAL UPDATE REQUIRED, PRODUCT-LOCAL
PROPOSAL REQUIRED, OWNER ACTION PREPARATION, OWNER ACTION REQUESTED, RECEIPT OR
REVALIDATION REQUIRED, CONFLICT FAIL-CLOSED, DEFERRED WITH EVIDENCE, or CURRENT
AND PRESERVED. Do not use portal release classifications for coordination-only
work.

PORTAL RELEASE TYPE AND AUDIT-DEPTH DECISION — MODE A ONLY

After exact portal-artifact intake in Mode A, classify the work as one of:

- COMPLETE CONNECTED MATERIAL RELEASE — ORDINARY MATERIAL;
- FULL MILESTONE RELEASE — ORDINARY MATERIAL;
- P0 REMEDIATION RELEASE — NON-ORDINARY ONLY WHEN TIGHTLY ISOLATED;
- ROLLBACK, RECOVERY, OR ARTIFACT-INTEGRITY CORRECTION — NON-ORDINARY; or
- EXCEPTIONAL NO-CHANGE AUDIT — NO NEW RELEASE.

An ordinary material release must satisfy `DUR-045`: add a meaningful lawful
batch of new accepted specialty-relevant professional profiles and refresh or
enrich existing accepted profiles. A P0 remediation that also includes material
feature, content, design, search, profile, outreach, workflow, or product-
capability work is an ordinary material release for the profile-growth gate.

A non-ordinary classification must be narrow, evidence-based, and limited to the
emergency, rollback, recovery, or artifact-integrity purpose that makes profile
addition unsafe or incoherent. The classification controls audit depth without
weakening any standing safeguard or becoming a convenience waiver.

EXCEPTIONAL NO-CHANGE AUDIT

Use only when the exact artifact already satisfies the complete material
objective and the full required evidence shows that no safe, worthwhile,
independent, or connected portal-side improvement justifies a release.

The builder may not select this classification:

- before exact-artifact intake;
- before completing the required audit depth;
- because the work is difficult, broad, time-consuming, or near launch;
- because one integration or production gate is blocked;
- because Roger did not specify a feature;
- because the builder prefers preservation;
- because the prior version was recently completed; or
- as a substitute for lawful profile growth, currentness work, regression
  correction, or accepted best-practice adoption.

Verify artifact identity, inspect current truth, document the heightened
evidence, and do not create a new version, ZIP, profile batch, continuation
artifact, or cosmetic change merely to show activity.

COMPLETE CONNECTED MATERIAL RELEASE

Use when one or more connected material outcomes can be completed in the same
build, such as attorney-outreach polish, profile/search improvement, Document
Help maturity, a portal-side Smarter Justice adapter, owner-operability work,
or a combination that reinforces the same user and operator journeys.

This is not a small-release category. It requires the builder to complete all
reasonably achievable connected work discovered for the selected mission
rather than stop at the first working patch. Perform:

- complete artifact and baseline intake;
- a regression and truth scan across all major surfaces;
- deep audit of changed, dependency-affected, launch-critical, stale,
  high-risk, or previously failed surfaces;
- profile-growth execution and currentness evaluation appropriate to the connected release;
- exact final-artifact regression.

Do not reproduce a full historical audit of every unchanged low-risk surface
when current evidence remains valid.

P0 REMEDIATION RELEASE

Use when safety, privacy, security, authorization, data loss,
artifact integrity, materially misleading public content, or rollback risk
requires priority correction.

A P0 remediation may be intentionally narrow when isolation materially reduces
risk, accelerates a necessary correction, or avoids unrelated regression. Do
not pad an urgent remediation with profile growth, redesign, optional
innovation, or unrelated connected-release work merely to make the release
appear broader.

The builder must still:

- investigate the root cause and directly affected surfaces;
- correct the complete safety or integrity boundary, not only the visible
  symptom;
- run all related regression and rollback acceptance;
- record adjacent independent improvements for later;
- preserve deferred work explicitly; and
- avoid calling the remediation a token revision.

The remediation may temporarily displace profile addition, broad visual work,
or optional product expansion.

FULL MILESTONE RELEASE

Use for the first application of this prompt to a portal, a stale or
insufficient prior audit, a major architecture or data-model change, a final
attorney-outreach or launch go/no-go milestone, a major Smarter Justice
contract change, a broad profile/publication-system change, or Roger's explicit
request for a complete re-audit. Perform the full maturity audit and all
applicable portfolio-wide acceptance work.

A builder may not label ordinary convenience work as a Complete Connected
Material Release merely to avoid a necessary Full Milestone Release audit. Conversely, this prompt's comprehensiveness must not be
used to recreate thousands of lines of unchanged evidence during every
Complete Connected Material Release.

The Mode A builder must read this entire prompt before changing portal code.

MODE B2 CENTRAL RELEASE AND MODE B1 COORDINATION CLASSIFICATION

In Mode B2, classify authorized Smarter Justice work as COMPLETE CONNECTED
CENTRAL RELEASE, CENTRAL P0 REMEDIATION, CENTRAL FULL MILESTONE RELEASE, or
EXCEPTIONAL CENTRAL NO-CHANGE AUDIT. Apply the same anti-token, audit-depth,
connected-scope, exact-artifact, rollback, and evidence burden to the central
mission, but do not import portal-only profile-growth or specialty requirements
unless the exact central contract requires them.

In Mode B1, classify the cycle as COMPLETE CONNECTED COORDINATION RECONCILIATION
or CURRENT COORDINATION PRESERVATION. The latter is valid only after checking
register freshness, product proposals, owner requests, dependencies, receipts,
expirations, fallbacks, and product-specific acceptance. Neither classification
authorizes a code version.


INITIAL-LAUNCH COHORT AND DOMESTIC VIOLENCE CONTROLLING OVERLAY

The initial-launch overlay is expressly designed for all four initial-launch micro-portals; the universal core applies to every registered owner-approved legal micro-portal. Domestic Violence Aid is not a renamed copy of the other portals. It carries an additional survivor-safety, privacy, emergency-boundary, organization-profile, and community-initiative overlay.

For Domestic Violence Aid:

- Domestic Violence Aid is the primary portal identity.
- The Stop Sign Project remains functional, visible, usable, directly reachable, and recognizably branded as an embedded community initiative.
- Preserve every working Stop Sign Project route, tool, profile type, workflow, bilingual asset, source map, outreach function, correction path, and privacy-preserving capability unless a documented safety, privacy, legal, accessibility, duplication, or maintainability reason supports revision.
- Attorney and firm profiles remain subject to the shared professional standards.
- Approved advocates, legal-aid organizations, nonprofits, public agencies, shelters, and service providers may also receive complete source-backed public pages when their profile class, safe-contact rules, claim authority, correction/removal rights, and publication gate are explicitly supported.
- Never expose confidential shelter addresses or nonpublic service locations.
- Do not collect detailed survivor narratives, precise location, or unsafe contact preferences merely for marketing, profile discovery, analytics, or general onboarding.
- Emergency and hotline information must be current, jurisdiction-appropriate, source-backed, dated, reviewed, and correction-friendly.
- Quick-exit and safe-device features must state their limitations truthfully.
- The Smarter Justice organization-profile contract must be discovered and verified from the exact current artifacts. If the exact central contract does not yet support a profile class safely, keep that class local/private or publication-gated and record the required contract extension rather than inventing live central support.

Shared requirements apply to every portal. Portal-specific requirements apply only where relevant and must be interpreted through the stricter safety, privacy, legal, accessibility, source-rights, and evidence-preservation rule.


===============================================================================
ENGLISH-FIRST RELEASE AND DEFERRED LANGUAGE-ACTIVATION STANDARD
===============================================================================

CURRENT OWNER DECISION

The current initial-launch phase for the four launch-cohort portals is English
only.

The builder must prioritize making the English portal complete, accurate,
plain-language, visually excellent, accessible, responsive, operationally
supportable, exact-tested, and ready for attorney outreach and public launch.

Do not spend ordinary English-first build cycles creating, expanding,
translating, synchronizing, or acceptance-testing Spanish or other-language
public pages, tools, forms, drafts, document-review instructions, errors,
validation, emails, notifications, support content, legal-information content,
official-form guidance, profile-interface copy, SEO pages, metadata, or
alternate-language route trees.

This sequencing is intended to reduce duplicate content work, translation
review, legal and safety review, accessibility review, route maintenance,
regression testing, and currentness burdens while the English product is still
changing.

LANGUAGE-CONTROL DESIGN NOW

Each portal must include a stable, accessible language-control component,
layout position, feature-flag contract, content-version strategy, and future
route strategy now so Spanish can be added later without redesigning the
portal.

During the English-first phase:

- English is the only active public interface language.
- Spanish and every additional-language feature flag default to OFF.
- No Spanish or additional-language option may appear clickable, navigable, or
  operational.
- No active alternate-language route may appear in public navigation, search,
  sitemaps, canonical or alternate-language graphs, attorney demonstrations,
  or launch acceptance.
- The production UI should normally show only English while retaining the
  language-control architecture in design and code.
- A visibly disabled preview such as “Español — available after English
  launch” may be shown only with Roger's explicit approval, native disabled
  semantics, accessible explanatory text, and no dead link or fake
  interaction.
- Do not reserve awkward empty space, display an unfinished-looking control, or
  weaken the first impression merely to prove future language support was
  considered.
- English must continue to work completely when every alternate-language flag
  is disabled.

PRESERVE, DO NOT EXPAND, EXISTING LANGUAGE ASSETS

Where an exact portal already contains working Spanish, bilingual, or
additional-language assets:

- preserve source files, translation keys, reviewed content, routes, tests, and
  historical evidence as dormant reusable assets where safe;
- do not delete, rewrite, or degrade them merely to simplify the English build;
- do not expand them during ordinary English-first releases;
- keep inactive alternate-language pages nonpublic, noindex, and outside the
  active launch and demonstration path;
- prevent mixed-language output, stale alternate-language navigation, or
  accidental public activation;
- record the dormant asset state, source/version age, and reactivation
  dependencies;
- do not let dormant translations override newer English legal, safety, form,
  official-resource, or operational truth.

Do not disable or remove an already-live production language solely because of
this source-build instruction. Any live-language deactivation requires Roger's
explicit approval, verified production-state review, user-impact analysis,
safe redirects or transition handling, and rollback.

DOMESTIC VIOLENCE AID AND STOP SIGN PROJECT

Existing Stop Sign Project bilingual assets are protected historical and
future-launch assets. Preserve them in the exact artifact where safe, but do
not make English/Spanish parity a requirement for the current English-first
release.

During this phase:

- public launch acceptance is based on the complete English survivor-safety
  journey;
- Spanish navigation and mode remain inactive unless already live in
  production and separately approved to remain active;
- future Spanish emergency, hotline, safety, court, agency, shelter,
  quick-exit, safe-device, notification, form, upload, workspace, and handoff
  content must receive the same currentness, survivor-safety, privacy, legal,
  accessibility, and support review as the English journey before activation.

SPANISH AND ADDITIONAL-LANGUAGE ACTIVATION GATE

Spanish preparation may begin only after the English portal is materially
complete for its approved launch scope, release-ready, accepted for launch by
Roger, exact-artifact tested, free of unresolved P0 defects, and stable enough
that translation will not repeatedly chase major English rewrites.

Spanish may become active only after all of the following are complete:

1. Roger explicitly authorizes the Spanish build and later authorizes public
   activation.
2. Complete user journeys are translated—not isolated labels.
3. Material legal, privacy, consent, safety, official-form, and emergency
   content receives qualified review.
4. Current official sources, forms, links, fees, deadlines, hotlines, and
   agency information are reverified for the translated experience.
5. Errors, validation, statuses, forms, drafts, upload instructions, review
   results, suggested next steps, support, account handoffs, and recovery paths
   are translated.
6. English and Spanish versions use aligned source, template, schema, and
   content versions.
7. No mixed-language failure remains.
8. Mobile, keyboard, screen-reader, contrast, zoom, reflow, and low-bandwidth
   acceptance is complete.
9. Support, correction, suppression, and safety-escalation workflows can serve
   the activated language.
10. SEO, canonical, alternate-language, sitemap, and noindex behavior is
    correct.
11. Exact final-artifact tests cover complete translated journeys.
12. Rollback can disable the language without harming English operation.

Additional languages follow the same gated process after evidence supports
their value and Roger approves the language and sequence.

LANGUAGE PHASE TRUTH

Use exact states:

- ENGLISH_BUILD;
- ENGLISH_RELEASE_CANDIDATE;
- ENGLISH_LAUNCH_ACCEPTED;
- ENGLISH_LIVE;
- SPANISH_DEFERRED;
- SPANISH_PREPARATION;
- SPANISH_ACCEPTANCE;
- SPANISH_ACTIVE;
- ADDITIONAL_LANGUAGE_DEFERRED;
- ADDITIONAL_LANGUAGE_PREPARATION;
- ADDITIONAL_LANGUAGE_ACTIVE.

Do not use “bilingual,” “multilingual,” “Spanish-ready,” or “language-ready” as
a vague marketing or release claim. State exactly what is active, dormant,
prepared, tested, approved, and live.

===============================================================================
STANDING REQUIRED PRODUCT CAPABILITIES — CONFIRMED AND CUMULATIVE
===============================================================================

The following capabilities are mandatory ongoing requirements. They must be preserved, audited, improved when evidence supports improvement, tested at the highest environment honestly available, and carried forward in every build's Next Version Improvement List until fully accepted.

1. UNIQUE FULL ATTORNEY PAGES

Every approved public attorney receives one unique, complete, canonical, searchable, claimable, correctable, mobile-ready, accessible profile page on the assigned micro-portal.

2. UNIQUE FULL FIRM PAGES

Every approved public firm receives one unique, complete, canonical, searchable, claimable or authority-reviewable, correctable, mobile-ready, accessible profile page, including approved links to its individual attorneys' unique portal pages.


2A. UNIQUE FULL APPROVED ORGANIZATION AND SERVICE-PROVIDER PAGES WHERE APPLICABLE

Domestic Violence Aid must support one unique, complete, canonical, searchable, authority-reviewable, correctable, removable, mobile-ready, accessible page for every approved public advocate organization, legal-aid organization, nonprofit, public agency, shelter, or service provider that the portal is authorized to publish. Public pages must use only safe public contact information, must never reveal confidential shelter or service locations, must state source and currentness boundaries truthfully, and must not imply endorsement, emergency availability, guaranteed assistance, or government status that has not been established.

Other portals may support approved organization or resource pages when their exact scope and source rights justify them. They must not fabricate a new profile class merely to increase counts.

3. VERIFIED CLAIM CONTROL

Every attorney or authorized firm representative must pass account, identity, professional-status, exact-profile authority, portal-specialty, and applicable firm-authority verification before receiving profile control.

4. PAID-MONTHLY-MEMBER OPPORTUNITY ELIGIBILITY

Before receiving any attorney-review request, legal-help request, lead, case opportunity, or referral opportunity from the platform, the attorney or covered firm seat must have a current server-authoritative active paid monthly profile membership, plus all separate verification, specialty, jurisdiction, conflict, privacy, support, capacity, and gate requirements.

5. REAL ATTORNEY-ALLOCATION SYSTEM

The platform must use a fair, auditable, supportable allocation system rather than ad hoc email forwarding, hidden manual favoritism, highest-payer routing, or unsupported ranking. Payment establishes threshold eligibility only; it does not buy organic rank, verification, specialty, endorsement, representation, or outcomes.

6. CONTINUAL PUBLIC-TOOL EVALUATION

Maintain a current inventory and maturity state for every public-user tool.
During each material build, perform a portal-wide regression/truth scan and
deeply review tools that are changed, dependency-affected, launch-critical,
stale, high-risk, previously failed, or selected in the Release Scope Record.
Improve a tool only when a genuine evidence-based benefit is reasonably
achievable. Preserve it unchanged when existing behavior is clearer, safer,
more accessible, more reliable, or more maintainable.

7. REUSABLE GUIDED-FORMS SYSTEM

Each portal must have or progressively build a reusable, versioned guided-forms system for appropriate basic forms, organizers, questionnaires, letters, checklists, timelines, intake records, and attorney-preparation materials.

8. SUPPORTED DRAFT DOCUMENT CREATION

Estate Law Aid must build and safely mature supported will and trust draft workflows. Divorce Law Aid must build and safely mature supported prenuptial and postnuptial agreement draft workflows. Each portal must build other appropriate draft-document workflows within its legal scope. Public activation remains jurisdiction controlled, evidence gated, privacy protected, legally reviewed where required, and clearly labeled as draft.

9. SECURE DOCUMENT AND SCREENSHOT REVIEW

Every portal must evaluate and, where appropriate, provide private document and screenshot upload for review, organization, extraction, OCR, analysis, comparison, issue spotting, attorney-review preparation, and deletion.
