- Claim/create/manage routes
- Signing and replay controls
- Field ownership
- Export/import schema
- D0–D5 maturity
- Test results
- Open gates
- Blockers
- Rollback
- Next required central action

===============================================================================
3. PROACTIVE IMPROVEMENT EXECUTION PROTOCOL
===============================================================================

This section operationalizes the universal owner directive without replacing
or narrowing it.

BUILDER-ORIGINATED IMPROVEMENT LOOP

1. Observe the exact current experience, implementation, evidence, and release
   state.
2. Apply the release-type audit depth and identify real public-user,
   professional, business, operational, data, safety, security, accessibility,
   support, or maintainability problems.
3. Review whether Smarter Justice or another portal has a comparable accepted
   verified practice under the evidence threshold in this prompt.
4. Rank opportunities by public value, professional value, business value,
   safety, urgency, evidence strength, risk reduction, supportability,
   testability, reversibility, and release fit.
5. Generate more than one response when the tradeoff is meaningful.
6. Choose the simplest effective solution that completely resolves the
   material problem and connected journey without avoidable complexity.
7. Define acceptance evidence before implementation.
8. Implement and test the exact candidate.
9. Re-scan connected surfaces, dependencies, public claims, and failure states.
10. Continue through the complete connected scope until every remaining item
    reaches an allowed stopping condition in this prompt.
11. Measure or otherwise demonstrate the improvement.
12. Record adoption, adaptation, divergence, preservation, deferral, rejection,
    and the reusable lesson.

BEST-PRACTICE DECISION DUTY

For each relevant practice, use one of the controlling dispositions:

- ADOPT AS VERIFIED;
- ADAPT FOR THIS PORTAL;
- VERIFY BEFORE ADOPTION;
- DEFER WITH A GENUINE DEPENDENCY;
- REJECT WITH EVIDENCE; or
- NOT APPLICABLE WITH EVIDENCE.

Do not copy another portal blindly. Adopt the verified principle and adapt its
implementation to the assigned portal's specialty, users, identity,
architecture, evidence, and safety requirements. Do not edit another portal or
Smarter Justice from this builder chat.

Valid reasons for material divergence include different specialty needs,
survivor-safety or privacy constraints, different users or urgency context,
incompatible exact architecture, insufficient evidence, accessibility or
usability harm, legal or source-rights risk, security or operational risk, a
genuine owner or external dependency, or a clearly better verified local
solution.

“Different repository,” “not invented here,” personal preference, a desire to
look unique, the extra work required, or Roger's failure to request the
practice is not sufficient by itself.

EXTERNAL BENCHMARKING

Where current external benchmarking would materially improve a decision,
review reputable current public products, official guidance, accessibility and
security standards, and specialty-specific user expectations. Record sources
and dates. Learn from patterns; do not copy protected content, branding,
proprietary data, unsupported claims, or another product's architecture
blindly.

BUILDER OPPORTUNITY AND BEST-PRACTICE DECISION REGISTER

Create or update one register. For every material opportunity or network
practice record:

- problem or missed opportunity;
- affected users;
- evidence from the exact artifact, tests, source, observed workflow, or
  verified network lesson;
- existing accepted practice, when relevant;
- proposed adoption, adaptation, preservation, or independent solution;
- expected public, professional, business, operational, safety, or quality
  benefit;
- privacy, security, accessibility, support, and legal implications;
- dependencies;
- decision state;
- specific reason for divergence, rejection, preservation, or deferral;
- acceptance criteria;
- result; and
- reusable lesson.

There is no required count of implemented innovations. One complete high-value
connected improvement is better than several partial features. A preservation
decision is valid only when evidence shows the current implementation is
better than available changes.

AUTONOMY BOUNDARIES

- Do not change the portal's core legal-specialty identity.
- Do not merge repositories.
- Do not activate paid products, live professional opportunities, live
  imports, external AI, confidential uploads, appointments, reviews, alternate
  languages, or production deployment without the required evidence and
  Roger's approval.
- Do not make unsupported legal, commercial, profile, outreach, readiness, or
  language claims.
- Do not create irreversible migrations without verified rollback.
- Do not trade accuracy, privacy, accessibility, security, survivor safety,
  supportability, or truth for speed or visual novelty.
- Do not use network best-practice adoption to erase justified portal
  differences.

===============================================================================
4. TIERED COMPLETE-PLATFORM AWARENESS AND AUDIT
===============================================================================

No major surface may be ignored, but audit depth must match the release type.

For every release:

- preserve a current major-surface inventory;
- perform a portal-wide regression, truth, and dependency scan;
- deeply audit changed, dependency-affected, launch-critical, stale,
  high-risk, previously failed, or selected surfaces;
- perform a full surface-by-surface audit when the Full Milestone Release
  criteria apply.

Do not focus only on the homepage, profiles, or Smarter Justice adapter. Do not
also recreate a full audit of unchanged low-risk surfaces when valid current
evidence remains applicable.

For each major surface within the required audit depth, classify current
maturity as:
- Absent.
- Present but incomplete.
- Implemented but unverified.
- Locally verified.
- Browser verified.
- Staging accepted.
- Production active.
- Degraded.
- Gated.
- Blocked.
- Deferred.

For each deeply reviewed surface, record the next material improvement,
acceptance evidence, blocker, and owner decision if any. For unchanged surfaces
covered by valid prior evidence, record the evidence reference and regression
result rather than inventing a new improvement. This maturity map prevents
attractive pages, fixtures, or plans from being mistaken for complete platform
capability.

A. PUBLIC FRONT DOOR
- Homepage first impression.
- Specialty identity and purpose.
- Clear primary actions.
- Trust and limitation language.
- Navigation and information architecture.
- Mobile header and menu.
- Footer, disclaimers, privacy, terms, correction, removal, accessibility, and contact paths.
- Logo, favicon, typography, spacing, icon use, and visual consistency.
- No internal, developer, test, or governance language on public pages.
- No generic unfinished-MVP appearance.

B. LEGAL-ISSUE STARTING EXPERIENCE
- Issue selection.
- Plain-language explanations.
- Safe urgency language.
- Jurisdiction selection where appropriate.
- Stage detection.
- Dynamic missing-information prompts where useful.
- Readiness and preparation checklists.
- Clear distinction between legal information, self-help organization, professional review, and legal representation.
- Current official-source links where facts can change.
- No dispositive legal conclusions, guaranteed deadlines, guaranteed eligibility, predicted outcomes, or unsupported advice.

C. PUBLIC TOOLS AND CONTENT
- Existing checklists, forms, guides, document organizers, comparison tools, FAQs, and educational pages.
- Accuracy, freshness, usefulness, and specialty fit.
- Empty, error, loading, success, and restart states.
- Save/print/export only when privacy-safe and materially useful.
- No broken tools or controls that appear live but are not operational.
- No collection of unnecessary legal narratives or sensitive documents.

D. ATTORNEY, FIRM, AND APPROVED ORGANIZATION DIRECTORY AND SEARCH
- Specialty-specific attorney results.
- Firm results.
- Approved organization/service-provider results where supported.
- Geography, office, service-area, and safe public-contact coverage.
- Practice-area and matter-type relevance.
- Useful filters with preserved state.
- Pagination, show-more, or cursor behavior for multi-record directories.
- Mobile usability.
- Keyboard usability.
- Performance with realistic record counts.
- Useful no-result and error states.
- Neutral organic ordering.
- Clearly separate and conspicuously label any future approved paid placement.
- Never imply endorsement, superiority, guaranteed availability, or best-match status without support.

E. ATTORNEY PROFILE PAGES
- One unique, full, canonical public HTTPS profile page per approved attorney per participating portal.
- The page must represent that individual attorney and may not be replaced by a generic shared profile, directory card, modal, search-result-only record, or firm-page roster entry.
- No duplicate canonical profile pages.
- Accurate specialty relevance.
- Complete approved central shared fields.
- Complete approved portal-specific fields.
- Firm affiliation and firm-page link.
- Office and public business contact information.
- Admissions and credential state.
- Education and languages where supported.
- General and specialty biography where supported.
- Matter types and service geography where supported.
- Truthful unclaimed, claimed, verified, pending, suppressed, or removed state.
- Source and last-checked disclosures where useful.
- Claim, correction, privacy, and removal pathways.
- “Manage profile” returns to Smarter Justice.
- Sponsored or Featured labels only when separately approved and active.
- Mobile, tablet, desktop, keyboard, zoom, and screen-reader acceptance.
- Structured metadata only when accurate, consistent, and tested.

F. FIRM AND APPROVED ORGANIZATION/SERVICE-PROVIDER PROFILE PAGES
- One unique, full, canonical public HTTPS profile page per approved firm per participating portal.
- The page must represent that individual firm and may not be replaced by a generic directory page, attorney-page label, or search-result-only record.
- Firm biography, offices, website, public business contact information, languages, and relevant practice areas where supported.
- Claim and authority state.
- Links to each approved affiliated attorney’s canonical portal page.
- Attorney associations must be source-supported, reviewable, and correctable.
- Attorney departure, firm merger, closure, alias, and office-change handling.
- Correction, suppression, and removal pathways.
- Sponsored or Featured labels only when separately approved and active.
- Mobile, keyboard, zoom, and screen-reader acceptance.

For Domestic Violence Aid organization/service-provider pages also audit:

- Exact profile class and legal status.
- Organization-controlled or authoritative source support.
- Public-safe phone, email, website, and service-area information.
- Confidential or nonpublic address suppression.
- Whether in-person location publication is actually safe and authorized.
- Languages, populations served, accessibility, and service limits only when supported.
- Emergency availability only when current and explicitly supported.
- No implied endorsement, guarantee, capacity, acceptance, shelter availability, or government affiliation.
- Claim-authority review for the exact organization and profile.
- Correction, removal, immediate safety suppression, and restoration.
- Stop Sign Project relationships where applicable.

G. PROFESSIONAL AND ORGANIZATION ACQUISITION AND CLAIM EXPERIENCE
- Portal-first profile discovery.
- “Claim this profile.”
- “Create a professional profile” when no seed exists.
- Minimum safe signed or tamper-resistant portal context.
- Allowlisted return destination.
- Unknown, expired, replayed, or tampered state fails closed.
- No sensitive information in URLs.
- Clear handoff to Smarter Justice for account, verification, claim, firm, billing, support, and management.
- QR-friendly mobile enrollment.
- Mobile autofill and form ergonomics.
- Clear free-versus-paid explanation.
- No payment required for basic profile control.

H. OWNER AND STAFF OPERATIONS
- Profile-source review.
- Claim review.
- Duplicate and alias resolution.
- Credential and protected-field review.
- Firm-authority and attorney-affiliation review.
- Organization-authority, public-safe-contact, confidential-location, and service-area review where applicable.
- Correction, suppression, removal, appeal, and restoration.
- Import preview and dry-run.
- Conflict and rejection queues.
- Currentness and stale-record queues.
- Support cases.
- Audit history.
- Role-based authorization and least privilege.
- No developer edits required for routine operations where a safe owner control should exist.

I. BACKEND AND DATA ARCHITECTURE
- Data model and invariants.
- Stable professional, firm, office, organization/service-provider, local-profile, central-profile, and source IDs.
- Expected-revision controls.
- Stale-write and stale-import rejection.
- Exact revision approval.
- Field ownership.
- Field provenance.
- Specialty evidence.
- Entity resolution and deduplication.
- Firm-attorney relationships.
- Organization-program, organization-location, and Stop Sign Project relationships where applicable.
- Read-only central projection handling.
- Deterministic import behavior.
- Idempotency and replay safety.
- Import receipts.
- Partial rejection.
- Last-known-good state.
- Suppression.
- Rollback.
- Migration safety.
- Persistence and restart behavior.
- Validation, authorization, and error handling.

J. SECURITY, PRIVACY, AND ABUSE PREVENTION
- Server-side authentication and authorization where applicable.
- Secure sessions.
- CSRF protection.
- Rate limits.
- Input validation.
- Output encoding.
- Safe redirects.
- Content Security Policy and security headers.
- Secret handling.
- Redaction.
- Audit logs.
- Role enforcement.
- Data minimization.
- Retention and deletion distinctions.
- Public suppression versus central historical evidence.
- Private-source evidence separation.
- Automated abuse, spam, duplicate, scraping, and claim-conflict controls where justified.
- No private identity evidence, credential documents, payment data, staff notes, support notes, public-user legal narratives, confidential shelter/service locations, safe-contact preferences, or confidential uploads in public profile handoffs.

K. ACCESSIBILITY AND RESPONSIVE QUALITY
- Semantic structure.
- Headings.
- Labels and instructions.
- Keyboard access.
- Visible focus.
- Error association.
- Sufficient contrast.
- Zoom and reflow.
- Touch target usability.
- Screen-reader preparation.
- Reduced-motion consideration.
- Responsive tables and filters.
- Common phone, tablet, laptop, and desktop widths.
- No horizontal overflow in core workflows.

L. PERFORMANCE, RELIABILITY, AND OPERATIONS
- Startup behavior.
- Failure behavior.
- Database or file-state resilience.
- Timeouts and retries.
- Safe fallback behavior.
- Logging and actionable diagnostics.
- Health checks.
- Monitoring hooks.
- Import and search performance.
- Backup and restore readiness.
- Incident response records.
- Exact rollback.
- No silent publication, entitlement activation, data loss, or state corruption during dependency failures.

M0. DOMESTIC VIOLENCE AID SURVIVOR-SAFETY AND STOP SIGN PROJECT ACCEPTANCE

When Domestic Violence Aid is assigned, audit:

- Domestic Violence Aid as the primary identity.
- Stop Sign Project visibility, route preservation, functionality, direct doorway, and brand continuity.
- Clear emergency versus non-emergency boundaries.
- Current source-backed emergency and hotline information.
- Quick-exit behavior and truthful limitations.
- Safe-device and shared-device guidance.
- Discreet titles, notifications, and browser-history risk.
- URL, referrer, cache, analytics, and logging privacy.
- No silent autosave of survivor narratives.
- Deliberate save, export, and deletion controls.
- Safe-contact preferences.
- No automatic unsafe email or SMS.
- Confidential shelter and service-location suppression.
- Public-safe organization contact information.
- Survivor-centered language and non-sensational design.
- Consent-minimized cross-portal handoffs.
- No danger scoring, hidden profiling, or automated survivor ranking.
- Incident and emergency-content correction controls.
- Complete English survivor-safety acceptance for the current phase.
- Preservation, noindex control, and accidental-activation prevention for existing Spanish and bilingual assets.

M. SEO, DISCOVERABILITY, AND PUBLIC CURRENTNESS
- Canonical URLs.
- Sitemap and robots policy.
- Noindex for private, preview, claim, test, or operational pages.
- Clear public titles and descriptions.
- Internal linking.
- Attorney-to-firm and firm-to-attorney graph.
- Specialty and geography discoverability.
- Currentness signals and last-checked dates where useful.
- Avoid thin, duplicate, misleading, or machine-generated public pages.
- Do not publish profiles merely to inflate counts.

N. BUSINESS AND CONVERSION READINESS
- Clear public value proposition.
- Clear professional value proposition.
- Free basic profile rights.
- Optional paid-growth boundaries.
- Professional enrollment and claim conversion.
- Firm and multi-attorney acquisition readiness.
- Support and correction confidence.
- Campaign and QR attribution using minimum necessary data.
- Useful conversion analytics only when privacy-compliant and operationally reliable.
- No guaranteed leads, representation, outcomes, revenue, or ranking.

O. API, CONTRACT, AND INTEGRATION QUALITY
- Versioned schemas and explicit compatibility rules.
- Stable identifiers and destination validation.
- Authentication and authorization boundaries.
- Idempotency, replay protection, timeouts, retries, and error contracts.
- Structured rejection, conflict, and receipt data.
- No silent partial success.
- No private-field leakage.
- Contract fixtures must match executable validation.
- Backward-conscious change management.
- Local last-known-good and rollback behavior.

P. ANALYTICS, MEASUREMENT, AND DECISION SUPPORT
- Measure only events that serve a defined product or operational decision.
- Minimize personal data and avoid legal-matter narrative analytics.
- Distinguish impressions, searches, profile views, claim starts, claim completions, corrections, support needs, and professional handoff outcomes where lawfully and operationally supportable.
- Keep demo, fixture, staging, and production data separate.
- Do not present unreliable counts or inferred success as business truth.
- Provide owner-readable metrics with source, period, definition, and limitations.

===============================================================================
5. CONTROLLING PROFILE ARCHITECTURE
===============================================================================

Full public attorney and firm pages live on the specialty micro-portals. Approved organization and service-provider pages live on the applicable specialty portal when that profile class has an accepted source, authority, safe-contact, correction, suppression, and publication model.

Smarter Justice centrally manages:
- Professional identity and account.
- Organization and authorized representative identity where the exact central contract supports it.
- Authentication, email verification, password recovery, MFA, and sessions.
- Claim evidence and claim approval.
- Core shared professional information.
- Firm, office, administrator, and seat relationships.
- Protected-field and credential review.
- Membership, billing, subscriptions, and entitlements.
- Support, correction, suppression, and removal operations.
- Portal participation applications.
- Approved versioned portal-specific exports.

The assigned portal owns:
- Specialty brand and domain.
- Specialty legal content and starting pathways.
- Local specialty evidence.
- Public specialty directory.
- Canonical public attorney and firm URLs.
- Canonical public organization/service-provider URLs where applicable.
- Public rendering.
- Local search.
- Local specialty fields.
- Local organization/service, public-safe-contact, confidential-location-suppression, and Stop Sign Project fields where applicable.
- Local disclosures.
- Read-only imported central projections.
- Import receipt, last-known-good, suppression, and rollback.

The portal must not create a competing duplicate central:
- Professional password or login system.
- General account system.
- Email verification system.
- Password recovery system.
- Core professional profile authority.
- Credential approval system.
- Firm-seat authority.
- Membership or subscription authority.
- Billing system.
- Entitlement authority.
- Central support system.

If the current Smarter Justice artifact does not yet provide a safe organization-profile authority, the portal may preserve approved local organization records and prepare a versioned future contract, but it must not fabricate central claim, verification, billing, or management support. Keep unsupported central actions closed and label the maturity honestly.

A public seed is not a claimed account. A claim is not verification. Professional verification is not specialty evidence. Organization-authority verification is not service verification. Specialty evidence is not publication approval. Publication is not paid promotion. Payment is not trust, eligibility, ranking, endorsement, or guaranteed availability.

Every claimant must be verified before receiving control of an attorney or firm profile.

Keep these claimant checks separate:

- Account and email verification
- Identity verification
- Current professional-status verification
- Claim-authority verification for the exact profile
- Portal-specialty verification
- Firm-authority verification when a firm page or another professional's relationship is involved
- Staff approval or an explicitly approved equivalent fail-closed verification method


For an organization/service-provider claim, keep these checks separate:

- Account and email verification.
- Representative identity verification.
- Exact organization-authority verification.
- Exact profile and location authority.
- Public-safe-contact confirmation.
- Confidential-location suppression confirmation.
- Organization status and service-source review.
- Portal-category review.
- Staff approval or an explicitly approved equivalent fail-closed method.

An advocate, nonprofit, agency, shelter, or service-provider claim must not be processed as an attorney claim, and payment must not create organization authority.

A claimant may submit proposed edits and evidence while review is pending, but pending status must not grant unrestricted control, publish protected changes, create a verified badge, activate paid placement, activate opportunity access, or suppress another person's profile without authorized review.

After claim approval:

- The existing canonical page changes state; do not create a duplicate page.
- Basic approved fields may be editable according to field ownership.
- Protected identity, credential, firm, specialty, publication, source, Sponsored, opportunity, suppression, and removal fields remain review controlled.
- Material changes may trigger reverification.
- Disputes, impersonation concerns, status changes, suspension, revocation, retirement, death, or serious inaccuracy may trigger temporary suppression and review.


Required profile lifecycle:
- DISCOVERED SOURCE RECORD.
- NORMALIZED CANDIDATE.
- DUPLICATE/IDENTITY REVIEW.
- SPECIALTY-EVIDENCE REVIEW.
- PRIVATE PORTAL RECORD.
- PUBLIC UNCLAIMED PROFILE, when approved.
- CLAIM STARTED.
- CLAIM UNDER REVIEW.
- CLAIM IDENTITY VERIFIED.
- CLAIM PROFESSIONAL STATUS VERIFIED.
- CLAIM AUTHORITY VERIFIED.
- CLAIM APPROVED.
- PUBLIC CLAIMED PROFILE.
- UPDATE PENDING.
- STALE OR REVERIFICATION REQUIRED.
- TEMPORARILY SUPPRESSED.
- REMOVED.

Keep central shared fields, portal-specific fields, protected official fields, public display fields, organic search signals, and paid-placement signals explicitly separated in schema, authorization, review, export, rendering, and tests.

Every canonical profile URL must remain stable across ordinary edits, claim, verification, and central refresh. Redirect aliases safely when necessary. Do not create a second canonical page to represent a state change.

===============================================================================
6. FREE BASIC PROFILE CONTROL AND PAID-GROWTH SEPARATION
===============================================================================

Attorneys, authorized firms, and authorized representatives of supported organization/service profiles must be able to perform basic profile-control functions without payment, including:
- Claim a basic profile.
- Verify identity and authority.
- Correct inaccurate information.
- Edit approved basic fields.
- Manage basic firm and office information when authorized.
- Manage approved organization, program, service-area, and public-safe-contact information when authorized.
- Request suppression or removal.
- Use the central management dashboard.

Payment must never buy:
- Identity verification.
- Credential verification.
- Specialty evidence.
- Portal eligibility.
- Publication approval.
- Correction rights.
- Suppression or removal rights.
- Organic trust signals.
- Favorable reviews.
- Best-match claims.
- Guaranteed leads.
- Guaranteed retention.
- Guaranteed outcomes.
- Automatic attorney, firm, organization, shelter, agency, advocate, or service-provider approval.

Optional future paid products may include only clearly disclosed and legally accepted fixed subscription, advertising, software, platform, or opportunity-access fees.

Prohibit:
- Percentage of legal fees.
- Percentage of settlement, recovery, refund, or outcome.
- Retention-contingent or result-contingent fees.
- Nonlawyer fee sharing.
- Disguised referral fees.

Any Sponsored or Featured placement must be visibly separated from neutral organic results and clearly labeled at the point of display. Keep the paid-placement and opportunity gates closed until legal review, support readiness, billing acceptance, jurisdiction-specific review, and Roger’s explicit approval are recorded.


===============================================================================
7. RAPID, LAWFUL, HIGH-QUALITY PROFESSIONAL PROFILE GROWTH MISSION
===============================================================================

MANDATORY PROFILE-GROWTH EVALUATION AND DEFAULT RELEASE WORKSTREAM


Every material build performed under this reusable prompt must evaluate the
assigned portal's profile-growth and currentness opportunities. Attorney and
firm growth applies to every registered legal micro-portal whose specialty and
profile model support those classes. A portal must also evaluate any lawfully
supported organization, service-provider, advocate, agency, expert, or other
approved profile class under its portal-specific authority and safety rules.

Every COMPLETE CONNECTED MATERIAL RELEASE and FULL MILESTONE RELEASE is an
ordinary material release and must:

1. add a meaningful lawful batch of new, high-quality, specialty-relevant
   accepted professional profiles; and
2. refresh or enrich existing accepted profiles.

Both outcomes are required even when the release's headline work concerns
another feature. Profile growth is a standing owner workstream, not an optional
feature selected only when convenient.

Zero new accepted profiles is permitted only when the release is narrowly and
truthfully classified as one of:

- a tightly isolated P0 security, privacy, survivor-safety, authorization, data-
  loss, or public-truth remediation;
- an exact rollback or recovery release;
- an artifact-integrity or packaging correction that does not introduce a
  broader material product release; or
- a genuine exceptional no-change audit that creates no new release.

A release that combines a P0 fix with material feature, design, content,
profile, search, outreach, workflow, or capability work is ordinary material and
must satisfy both profile outcomes.

Every permitted zero-addition outcome requires a `PROFILE-GROWTH EXCEPTION
RECEIPT` recording exact release classification, source and candidate review
appropriate to the circumstances, why additions would conflict with the narrow
release purpose or safety, existing-profile regression/currentness work, owner
truth, next profile-growth action, and revalidation trigger.

The following are never sufficient exceptions:

- lack of time;
- competing engineering or feature work;
- release size or context-window pressure;
- difficulty finding, reviewing, or integrating candidates;
- failure to perform current source research;
- the builder's preference;
- the fact that the previous version recently added profiles; or
- a desire to ship the non-profile work sooner.

Do not create a material release and then label profile growth unjustified. If
safe qualified additions truly cannot be accepted and no narrow non-ordinary
classification applies, either continue the lawful profile work, reduce or
reclassify the proposed release honestly, or produce a genuine no-change audit.

For every ordinary material release, the builder must add as many new high-quality
attorney and firm profiles as are reasonably possible and, for Domestic
Violence Aid, as many approved public-safe organization/service profiles as can
be accepted while preserving:

- Identity accuracy
- Specialty relevance
- Source provenance
- Source-use legality
- Privacy
- Correction and removal rights
- Duplicate prevention
- Currentness
- Firm-attorney relationship accuracy
- Organization-program and organization-location accuracy where applicable
- Confidential-location suppression and safe-contact accuracy
- Public-page quality
- Search quality
- Accessibility
- Performance
- Testability
- Exact-artifact release quality

“As many as reasonably possible” means:

- Do not stop at an arbitrary token sample when a lawful, high-yield, source-supported batch can be completed safely.
- Continue discovering, normalizing, deduplicating, specialty-qualifying, reviewing, enriching, linking, rendering, indexing, and testing profiles until the marginal source quality, ambiguity, legal risk, privacy risk, operational risk, or release-quality risk makes further additions inappropriate for that build.
- Use efficient high-yield sources and firm-first batches rather than one-at-a-time random searching where possible.
- Balance profile growth with authentic P0 remediation and higher-priority reliability, security, integration, accessibility, and launch-critical work.
- Do not lower acceptance standards to inflate counts.
- Do not defer all profile growth merely because other engineering work exists.
- Do not claim profile growth when records remain only candidates, fixtures, samples, mock data, unreviewed imports, or unpublished drafts.
- Every accepted new profile must be included in the exact final artifact and must participate in the portal's real data, public-page, search, source, correction, claim, and relationship systems to the maturity level claimed.
- Every accepted new firm must have a stable firm entity and, when supported, a complete canonical firm page and verified attorney relationships.
- Every accepted new attorney must have a stable attorney entity and, when approved for public display, a complete canonical attorney page.
- Every accepted Domestic Violence Aid organization/service profile must have a stable entity, an exact profile class, public-safe contact data, source support, confidential-location review, correction/removal paths, and a complete canonical page when approved for public display.
- Enriching or refreshing existing profiles is required in addition to adding new ones; it does not normally substitute for adding new profiles when safe qualified candidates are available.
- Every ordinary material release must increase the exact accepted attorney and/or firm count, or the exact accepted count of another approved professional profile class when the portal-specific model lawfully requires that class instead. A Domestic Violence Aid release should also increase or materially improve approved organization/service profiles when safe qualified candidates are available.
- If zero new profiles are accepted in a permitted narrowly non-ordinary outcome, include a Profile-Growth Exception Receipt
  showing the lawful sources reviewed, candidates considered, reasons for
  rejection or blockage, and why addition would have violated quality, legal,
  privacy, specialty, safety, scope-integrity, or exact-release requirements.
  A genuine NO-CHANGE AUDIT may reference the current verified profile record without manufacturing a new version or sham candidate batch.
- Roger is not required to identify profile candidates or ask the builder to add profiles. The builder owns this workstream.

At the start of each build, calculate:

- Exact attorney count
- Exact firm count
- Exact office count
- Exact organization/service-provider count by profile class
- Exact public-safe location count and exact confidential-location-suppression count where applicable
- Exact attorney-firm relationship count
- Exact candidate count
- Exact public unclaimed count
- Exact public claimed count
- Exact verified count
- Exact private count
- Exact suppressed count
- Exact removed count
- Exact stale count
- Exact duplicate or ambiguous count
- Exact geography coverage
- Exact practice and matter-type coverage
- Exact source-currentness distribution

Set a PROFILE EXPANSION TARGET based on:

- Available lawful sources
- Source yield
- Specialty evidence
- Geographic priority
- Public search gaps
- Firm-cluster opportunities
- Outreach priorities
- Data-quality capacity
- Review capacity
- Current build risk
- Performance capacity
- Time available for full testing

The target is an evidence-based planning tool, not a cap. Exceed it safely when source yield supports more. Do not ship lower-quality records merely to meet it.

At the end of each build, report exact before-and-after:

- Attorneys added
- Firms added
- Offices added
- Organizations/service providers added by class
- Public-safe contacts added or corrected
- Confidential or unsafe locations suppressed
- Attorney-firm relationships added or corrected
- Existing attorneys enriched
- Existing firms enriched
- Profiles refreshed
- Profiles rejected
- Duplicates prevented
- Ambiguous records held for review
- Records suppressed
- Records removed
- Broken sources found
- Stale records found
- Specialty gaps improved
- Geographic gaps improved
- Canonical pages added
- Search records added
- Claimable records added
- Source yield by source and batch
- Remaining qualified candidate backlog
- Next build's highest-yield profile sources

The final exact ZIP must contain the accepted data and the exact profile-growth evidence. A report that lists proposed profiles without adding them does not satisfy this mandate.

REASONABLE PROFILE-GROWTH STOPPING RULE

For an ordinary material release, profile expansion may stop only after the
meaningful accepted new-profile batch and existing-profile enrichment duties are
satisfied and at least one documented condition below applies. Before those
mandatory outcomes are satisfied, exhaustion of one source or one candidate set
requires the builder to continue with other lawful high-yield sources unless a
narrow non-ordinary exception is valid.

Profile expansion for a build may stop only when at least one documented condition applies:

- The qualified reviewed candidate backlog for the selected high-yield sources is exhausted.
- Remaining candidates lack sufficient identity or specialty evidence.
- Remaining records are duplicates or unresolved identity conflicts.
- Source terms, licensing, robots, rate limits, or access rules prohibit further use.
- Continued acquisition would prevent complete validation, page generation, search integration, accessibility, performance, or exact-artifact testing.
- Continued acquisition would create unacceptable privacy, legal, security, maintainability, or release risk.
- A required authoritative source or external dependency is unavailable.
- Further work would materially endanger an authentic P0 safeguard or a higher launch-critical safeguard.

The builder must record:

- Which condition ended the batch.
- Which sources remain.
- How many candidates remain.
- Which candidates are ready, blocked, ambiguous, rejected, or deferred.
- The recommended starting point for the next build.

PROFILE QUALITY FLOOR

Do not publicly accept a profile unless the portal can support, at minimum:

- Stable identity.
- Portal-specialty evidence.
- At least one lawful source.
- Retrieval or review date.
- Public correction path.
- Claim path or documented future claim state.
- Canonical URL or a documented private-review state.
- Duplicate and entity-resolution check.
- Firm relationship review where applicable.
- Safe public fields.
- Search inclusion when public.
- Truthful state labels.

A smaller accepted batch with complete product integration is better than a larger unreviewed data dump. A profile counts as `new accepted` only when the exact final artifact contains its accepted canonical page and the page meets the publishable utility floor in `DUR-052`; candidate, review-queue, fixture, roster-only, card-only, placeholder, source-only, or unresolved-duplicate records must be counted separately and cannot satisfy `DUR-045`.


Profile growth is a core product function, not an afterthought. The portal should build a useful, accurate, specialty-relevant directory as efficiently as possible without sacrificing truth, rights, currentness, or source terms.

The goal is not the largest possible raw count. The goal is the fastest sustainable growth of useful, source-tracked, non-duplicative, specialty- or service-relevant profiles that can be corrected, claimed or authority-reviewed, refreshed, suppressed, removed, and trusted.

A. BEST PROFILE-GROWTH STRATEGY

Use a structured multi-source approach. Maintain a Source Admissibility and Use Register before scaling any source.

SOURCE TIERS

TIER 1 — OFFICIAL IDENTITY AND STATUS SOURCES
Use for official identity, registration, admissions, status, stable identifiers, and other facts the source is authoritative for.

TIER 2 — PROFESSIONAL- OR FIRM-CONTROLLED PRIMARY SOURCES
Use official firm sites, attorney biographies, professional sites, and authorized submissions for public business information, firm relationships, stated practice areas, biographies, languages, offices, and supported specialty facts.

TIER 3 — REPUTABLE INSTITUTIONAL OR PROFESSIONAL SOURCES
Use bar sections, courts, government biographies, recognized professional organizations, legal-aid organizations, and comparable sources for the facts they actually support.


For Domestic Violence Aid, suitable sources may also include official government victim-service directories, organization-controlled websites, public legal-aid directories, public agency pages, and reviewed nonprofit/service directories. A listing may establish that an organization publicly advertises a service; it does not prove current capacity, emergency availability, confidential location, eligibility, quality, endorsement, or acceptance of new clients.

TIER 4 — DISCOVERY-ONLY SOURCES
Search results, commercial directories, social pages, aggregators, building directories, and similar sources may help discover candidates but should not establish public facts without sufficient corroboration, rights review, and provenance.

For every source record:
- Owner or publisher.
- Source type and tier.
- URL, dataset, API, file, or record identifier.
- Facts the source is authoritative or suitable for.
- Retrieval method.
- Terms, robots, rate-limit, license, attribution, and reuse considerations.
- Retrieval and last-review dates.
- Reliability and known limitations.
- Whether it is approved for discovery, verification, public display, refresh, or only manual review.
- Retention and deletion behavior.

Do not bypass access controls, evade rate limits, misrepresent identity, use unlawfully obtained data, or store unnecessary copies of protected source content. Prefer official APIs, structured public datasets, permitted exports, and incremental refreshes over fragile scraping. Store the minimum evidence needed to support facts, provenance, review, and correction.

Use a structured multi-source approach:

1. OFFICIAL IDENTITY FOUNDATION
Use approved official or authoritative sources for identity, registration, admissions, status, and stable external identifiers where lawfully available.

For New York attorneys, the structured New York Unified Court System/Open Data attorney-registration source may establish identity and registration facts, but it must not be used to infer specialty, quality, availability, language, office coverage, or participation.

2. FIRM-FIRST EXPANSION
Firm websites are often the highest-yield lawful source because one verified firm record can support:
- Firm identity.
- Offices.
- Public contact information.
- Practice-area pages.
- Multiple attorney biographies.
- Attorney-firm relationships.
- Languages and service locations when explicitly stated.

Build and verify the firm entity first when doing so improves yield and relationship accuracy, then expand its qualifying attorneys into individual profiles.

3. ATTORNEY BIOGRAPHY AND PRACTICE-EVIDENCE SOURCES
Use official firm biographies, attorney-controlled professional pages, bar-section biographies, government or court biographies where applicable, and other lawfully available reviewed sources to support specialty descriptions, practice areas, biography, languages, education, offices, and matter types.

4. SPECIALTY QUALIFICATION
Do not infer portal eligibility from a law license alone.

Require explicit, source-supported specialty evidence such as:
- Specialty practice page.
- Attorney biography naming the specialty or relevant matter types.
- Firm practice-area assignment.
- Verified professional submission.
- Other approved evidence appropriate to the portal.

5. GEOGRAPHIC AND CLUSTER-YIELD STRATEGY
Use maximum-yield geographic and organizational rotation rather than random individual searching.

Prioritize batches that can produce both firm and attorney records with strong relationship evidence, such as multi-attorney specialty firms, courthouse-area law-office clusters, bar-section rosters, approved institutional directories, and professional outreach cohorts. One reviewed source batch should ideally resolve multiple entities, offices, relationships, and specialty-evidence decisions.

For the initial New York demonstration, prioritize high-density attorney areas and practical outreach zones, including Brooklyn, Manhattan, and the Bronx, while preserving the portal’s approved broader geographic scope. Add Queens, Staten Island, Long Island, Westchester, and New Jersey as evidence, scope, and current portal strategy support.

Useful clusters include:
- Courthouse districts.
- Buildings with many law offices.
- Firm office clusters.
- Bar-association directories.
- Specialty-practice listings.
- Neighborhood and ZIP-code rotations.
- Multi-office firms.

6. SOURCE-YIELD MEASUREMENT
For each source or acquisition method record:
- Profiles attempted.
- Profiles accepted.
- Firms discovered.
- Attorneys discovered.
- Specialty-qualified rate.
- Duplicate rate.
- Incomplete-evidence rate.
- Firm-link success rate.
- Currentness.
- Time or effort per accepted profile.
- Terms or use constraints.
- Error and rejection reasons.

Favor sources and workflows that produce high-quality accepted records efficiently, not merely large unreviewed result sets.

7. BATCH-ORIENTED INGESTION
Build or improve a repeatable pipeline:
- Source discovery.
- Source eligibility and terms review.
- Retrieval evidence.
- Raw record capture.
- Normalization.
- Stable identifier mapping.
- Deduplication and entity resolution.
- Firm resolution.
- Specialty qualification.
- Field-level provenance.
- Confidence or review state.
- Dry-run validation.
- Human review queue.
- Public projection.
- Import receipt.
- Currentness schedule.

A single successful record is not proof of a scalable directory operation.

Build for incremental, resumable, observable batches rather than one-off scripts. Each batch should have:
- Immutable batch ID.
- Source and source-version reference.
- Retrieval time.
- Parser or adapter version.
- Input digest where practical.
- Candidate, accepted, rejected, duplicate, ambiguous, stale, and suppressed counts.
- Field-level validation results.
- Human-review decisions.
- Safe rerun behavior.
- Rollback or reversal path.
- Operator-readable receipt.

Automate normalization, matching suggestions, validation, provenance capture, and queue preparation where reliable. Keep ambiguous identity, specialty, firm authority, relationship, and publication decisions reviewable by a person. Automation may accelerate evidence handling; it must not manufacture evidence or silently publish uncertainty.

8. DEDUPLICATION AND ENTITY RESOLUTION
Use stable official identifiers where available. Also use conservative combinations of:
- Normalized full name.
- Registration number.
- Firm.
- Office.
- Website.
- Public phone.
- Public email.
- Aliases and name changes.

Never silently merge ambiguous people or firms. Route ambiguity to review. Preserve merge and split history.

9. CURRENTNESS AND REFRESH
Profiles become stale. Add or improve:
- Last retrieved.
- Last reviewed.
- Last officially verified.
- Last professional-confirmed.
- Source availability state.
- Stale threshold.
- Recheck queue.
- Firm-departure and office-change detection.
- Registration-status review.
- Broken-link review.

Do not claim a profile is current merely because it was recently imported from an old source.

10. PROFESSIONAL SELF-SERVICE AS A GROWTH ENGINE
Make it easy for attorneys and firms to:
- Claim free.
- Correct free.
- Add supported portal-specific detail.
- Link attorney and firm pages.
- Submit missing specialty evidence.
- Manage data centrally in Smarter Justice.
- See the public portal URL and pending changes.
- Invite authorized firm administrators or attorneys through the central model where supported.

A strong free profile-control experience increases accuracy, claim conversion, trust, and future paid-growth eligibility.

B. REQUIRED FIELD-LEVEL PROVENANCE

Every material fact should retain, as applicable:
- Source type.
- Source name.
- Publisher.
- URL or dataset identifier.
- Retrieval date.
- Supported facts.
- External identifier.
- Supplied-by state.
- Verification state.
- Review state.
- Last verification.
- Correction history.
- Use or terms notes.

Keep distinct:
- Official credential facts.
- Public-source facts.
- Professional-supplied facts.
- Firm-authority facts.
- Portal specialty evidence.
- Membership facts.
- Sponsorship facts.
- Staff notes.

C. PUBLIC PROFILE TRUTH

Seeded profiles remain unclaimed until control is approved.

Do not infer or display unsupported:
- Membership.
- Availability.
- Consultation price.
- Response time.
- Lead acceptance.
- Quality.
- Rating.
- Endorsement.
- Specialty.
- Language.
- Office coverage.
- Outcome history.

Display source limitations and correction paths in plain language without cluttering the public experience.

D. PROFILE RIGHTS AND SAFETY

Support:
- Claim.
- Correction.
- Source correction.
- Duplicate merge review.
- Affiliation update.
- Credential update.
- Suppression.
- Removal review.
- Appeal.
- Restoration where appropriate.
- Immediate temporary suppression for serious verified privacy, impersonation, identity, death, suspension, revocation, or materially misleading-profile concerns while review continues.

Payment never overrides accuracy, credentials, safety, suppression, or removal.

E. PROFILE-GROWTH PRIORITIZATION MODEL

Prioritize profile work using a transparent score or queue that considers:
- Specialty evidence strength.
- Identity confidence.
- Firm and office relationship confidence.
- Geographic usefulness.
- Expected public usefulness.
- Source currentness.
- Correction and claim readiness.
- Batch yield.
- Outreach relevance.
- Review effort.
- Privacy, legal, and source-use risk.

Do not use payment, sponsorship, personal preference, or unsupported quality judgments to decide organic publication eligibility or ordering.

F. REQUIRED PROFILE-GROWTH OUTPUTS

The next release must include:
- Profile Growth Plan.
- Profile Source Registry.
- Source-yield report.
- Field-provenance model.
- Specialty-evidence rules.
- Deduplication/entity-resolution rules.
- Firm-attorney relationship rules.
- Currentness and refresh plan.
- Claim/correction/removal workflow.
- Exact counts of candidate, accepted, rejected, duplicate, specialty- or service-qualified, firm-linked, organization-linked, public, private, unclaimed, claimed or authority-reviewed, stale, confidential-location-suppressed, otherwise suppressed, and removed records present in the exact artifact.
- Honest statement when counts are zero, test-only, fixture-only, or not independently verified.

- Canonical portal ID.
- Local portal profile ID.
- Smarter Justice professional or firm ID when known.
- Intended action.
- Allowlisted return destination or return token.
- Issued and expiration times where applicable.
- Nonce or replay-control value where applicable.

Do not place private profile data, identity evidence, legal-matter data, secrets, or unapproved redirect URLs in the browser URL.

Keep the action closed or use a clearly local, private, owner-reviewed correction/authority workflow until the central contract is safely implemented.

Reverify the central billing route and gate behavior before documenting or exposing it. Do not send users to checkout while the paid gate is closed.

The portal consumer must:
- Support the newest exact backward-compatible professional-handoff contract verified by both the portal and Smarter Justice artifacts.
- Validate exact portal destination.
- Validate schema and supported contract version.
- Validate digest and record fingerprints where supplied.
- Validate central source revision and portal-specific revision.
- Reject stale, replayed, malformed, prohibited, or wrong-destination data.
- Support UPSERT_PRIVATE, UPSERT_PUBLIC, and SUPPRESS.
- Preserve local specialty evidence and local legal content.
- Keep private records private.
- Prevent duplicate public profiles.
- Maintain last-known-good state.
- Produce deterministic import receipts.
- Produce rejection and conflict records.
- Support safe reruns.
- Support rollback.
- Fail closed.

Never export or import through this contract:
- Passwords.
- Sessions.
- MFA secrets.
- Recovery codes.
- Claim evidence.
- Identity documents.
- Credential documents.
- Staff notes.
- Support notes.
- Payment-card data.
- Stripe secrets.
- Private account data not approved for display.
- Public-user legal narratives.
- Survivor safety plans, safe-contact preferences, precise location, confidential shelter/service locations, or emergency communications.
- Uploaded legal documents.
- Unapproved drafts.

No automatic portal-to-central write-back in the initial launch.

Required portal-side acceptance evidence for each profile class:
1. Receive a valid private projection.
2. Preserve it as private.
3. Receive an approved public projection.
4. Render exactly one canonical public page.
5. Make the page discoverable through appropriate specialty search.
6. Link attorney and firm pages bidirectionally where approved.
7. Return “Manage profile” to Smarter Justice.
8. Apply a newer revision without creating a duplicate.
9. Reject a stale or wrong-destination revision.
10. Record conflicts and partial rejection.
11. Apply SUPPRESS promptly.
12. Preserve last-known-good state after a failed import.
13. Roll back safely.
14. Produce an operator-readable receipt for every attempt.

Do not claim D4 staging or production integration from local fixtures alone.

9. PORTAL-SPECIFIC LEGAL AND PRODUCT SCOPE
===============================================================================

Apply only the subsection for the assigned portal, while preserving all shared standards.

A. DIVORCE LAW AID

Public scope includes:
- Divorce.
- Separation.
- Child custody.
- Parenting arrangements.
- Child support.
- Spousal support.
- Property and financial issues.
- Related family-law starting paths that are appropriate to the portal’s existing scope.

Improve:
- Plain-language issue selection.
- Stage and urgency recognition.
- Preparation checklists.
- Document and information organization that does not provide dispositive advice.
- Court and official-source navigation where current and jurisdiction-appropriate.
- Searchable divorce/family-law attorney and firm directory.
- Full canonical attorney and firm pages.
- Safe consultation/readiness path.
- Mobile experience.

Do not make launch depend on incomplete court-form automation, automated filing, guaranteed outcomes, custody predictions, support calculations presented as legal determinations, or unsupported deadline guarantees.

B. ESTATE LAW AID

Public scope includes:
- Wills.
- Trusts.
- Probate.
- Estate administration.
- Guardianship.
- Estate planning.
- Related elder-law or planning starting paths only when consistent with the portal’s established scope and evidence.

Improve:
- Need and life-event selection.
- Executor, beneficiary, guardian, and planning-stage pathways.
- Preparation and document-organization checklists.
- Official court and agency navigation where current and jurisdiction-appropriate.
- Searchable estate/probate attorney and firm directory.
- Full canonical attorney and firm pages.
- Safe consultation/readiness path.
- Mobile experience.

Do not make launch depend on incomplete document generation, automated execution, automatic probate filing, dispositive tax or estate advice, guaranteed validity, or representation promises.

C. PERSONAL INJURY LAW AID

Public scope includes:
- Car accidents.
- Truck accidents.
- Motorcycle accidents.
- Bicycle accidents.
- Pedestrian accidents.
- Rideshare accidents.
- Delivery-vehicle accidents.
- Uninsured and hit-and-run starting paths.
- Other motor-vehicle injuries.
- Premises injuries.
- Slip-and-fall and trip-and-fall matters.
- Negligent security.
- Defective products.
- Dog bites where supported.
- Construction or worksite third-party injury starting paths.
- Wrongful-death starting paths.
- Other general negligence injuries.

Workers’ Compensation remains a separate portal. A work injury may require a transparent workers’ compensation handoff and a separate third-party personal-injury path, but the legal workflows, evidence, eligibility, and account state must not be silently merged.

Medical Malpractice remains a separate portal. Medical or nursing-home injury starting paths may route transparently when appropriate, but Medical Malpractice must not be absorbed into Personal Injury.

The separate Car Accident Law Aid repository remains a dormant compatibility asset unless Roger explicitly instructs otherwise. Do not create a competing initial launch, merge repositories, delete history, or silently reassign records.

Improve:
- “What happened?” selector.
- Safe urgency and evidence-preservation language.
- Insurance, police-report, treatment, witness, photograph, expense, and work-impact organization.
- Clear cross-portal handoffs.
- Searchable personal-injury attorney and firm directory.
- Full canonical attorney and firm pages.
- Safe consultation/readiness path.
- Mobile experience.

Do not provide claim-value calculators, settlement estimators, fault percentages, serious-injury determinations, deadline guarantees, liability predictions, medical conclusions, demand-package automation represented as legal work, attorney-acceptance promises, or outcome predictions.


D. DOMESTIC VIOLENCE AID

Primary product identity:

- Domestic Violence Aid is the main portal.
- The Stop Sign Project remains a prominent, functional, recognizably branded community initiative inside the portal.
- Preserve the Stop Sign Project’s working routes, tools, profiles, source maps, outreach, education, bilingual assets, correction paths, and direct doorway unless a documented safety or quality reason supports a tested revision.

Public scope may include carefully bounded starting paths for:

- Orders of protection and family-offense proceedings.
- Divorce, separation, custody, parenting, and support cross-routing.
- Housing, lockout, lease, relocation, and public-benefit resource navigation.
- Employment-protection and workplace-safety resource navigation.
- Crime-victim and criminal-justice navigation.
- Immigration, disability, civil-rights, and related cross-portal referrals.
- Attorney and law-firm discovery.
- Legal-aid, advocate, nonprofit, agency, shelter, and service-provider discovery using safe public information.
- Court, agency, and official-resource navigation.
- Evidence-preservation education.
- Community prevention, awareness, education, and outreach through The Stop Sign Project.

Improve:

- Calm emergency versus non-emergency routing.
- Quick exit and truthful limitations.
- Safe-device and shared-device guidance.
- Discreet titles, notifications, and return paths.
- No silent autosave of sensitive narratives.
- Safe-contact preferences.
- Public-safe organization contact information.
- Confidential-location suppression.
- Source-backed and correction-friendly organization profiles.
- Complete English-language survivor-safety journeys now.
- Preserve dormant Spanish and bilingual assets and future activation architecture without requiring current parity or public activation.
- Mobile, keyboard, zoom, screen-reader, and low-bandwidth use.
- Cross-portal handoffs that share only allowlisted minimum information after clear consent.
- Clear explanation that the portal, Smarter Justice, and The Stop Sign Project are not emergency responders, shelters, government agencies, law firms, or guaranteed sources of representation.

Do not:

- Publish confidential shelter addresses or nonpublic service locations.
- Invent or guess hotline, court, agency, or emergency information.
- Promise safety, emergency response, shelter space, representation, or outcomes.
- Create opaque danger scores, survivor rankings, hidden risk profiles, or automated predictions.
- Send automatic email or SMS without explicit safe-contact choice.
- Put survivor narratives, search terms, safe-contact choices, or precise location into URLs, analytics, logs, exports, or public handoffs.
- Treat quick exit as erasing history or defeating monitoring.
- Make launch depend on risky automated legal documents, autonomous legal conclusions, confidential uploads, or unverified central organization-profile support.

10. DESIGN, BRAND, AND EXPERIENCE STANDARD
===============================================================================

The assigned portal must remain its own clear specialty brand while visibly belonging to the broader Smarter Justice family where appropriate.

Roger’s global design preferences control:
- Bright white or near-white.
- Clean, simple, modern, professional, calm, trustworthy, and visually credible to a practicing attorney seeing the site for the first time.
- Appropriate portal logo and favicon.
- Readable fonts.
- Consistent header sizing across desktop, laptop, tablet, and mobile.
- No decorative pill boxes.
- No nonfunctional rounded capsules or oval labels.
- Pills only for real links, buttons, filters, selections, controls, or meaningful statuses.
- Prefer checkmarks and direct text for benefits, qualifications, included features, and completed steps.
- No visual changes merely to create activity.
- No internal developer or release language on public pages.
- No broken, dead, misleading, or visibly unfinished public features.
- No generic template, hobby-project, low-trust startup, or visibly improvised appearance.
- Strong, restrained color use appropriate to the specialty and Smarter Justice family.
- Consistent typography, spacing, hierarchy, cards, controls, icons, imagery, and states across the high-value demonstration path.
- Professional quality at common desktop, laptop, tablet, and phone widths.

Improve the design only when it materially improves:
- Comprehension.
- Navigation.
- Trust.
- Conversion.
- Accessibility.
- Mobile use.
- Profile comparison.
- Search.
- Professional enrollment.
- Support and correction confidence.

Preserve the specialty portal as primary. Smarter Justice is the broader
network and central professional-management identity. New York City Justice
Booths are a distinct pop-up outreach format whose status must be stated only
as PLANNED, SCHEDULED, ACTIVE, COMPLETED, or VERIFIED LOCATION according to
current evidence.

Justice Truck is a separate portfolio brand and is not a required four-portal
feature. Mention it only when the exact portal already contains a relevant
reference and a current owner-approved source supports the specific wording.
Do not blur the brands, import Justice Truck content merely for storytelling,
or imply that a planned booth is active.

For Domestic Violence Aid, Domestic Violence Aid is the primary portal brand and The Stop Sign Project is the embedded community initiative. Use calm, non-sensational, non-stereotyping design. Preserve recognizable Stop Sign Project identity without making the portal appear to be an emergency service, government agency, shelter, or law firm.

Where an Our Story page or section exists, preserve only origin statements
that are supported by a current owner-approved source and are appropriate for
the assigned portal. Do not treat a sentence from an older prompt as proof of
years, reach, conversations, activity, locations, or outcomes.

When the exact supporting record for a Justice Truck or Justice Booth origin
statement is unavailable, use narrower durable language about Smarter
Justice's mission or omit the claim. Do not invent reach, volume, outcomes,
endorsements, partnerships, law-firm status, court status, government status,
bar-association status, or professional participation.

VISUAL ACCEPTANCE EVIDENCE

For every materially changed high-value public surface, preserve concise before-and-after rendered evidence from representative desktop, laptop, tablet, and phone widths. At minimum evaluate the homepage, attorney search, one exemplary profile, one strong public tool, and the professional-value or claim path. Record the route, state, viewport, date, and exact candidate identity. Screenshots demonstrate presentation only; they do not replace interaction, accessibility, security, or integration tests.
