10D. PUBLIC-FACING LANGUAGE, FIRST-SECONDS HOMEPAGE, AND EVERY-VERSION VISUAL-SYSTEM ACCEPTANCE
===============================================================================

PUBLIC-FACING SURFACE INVENTORY

Every release must discover and review all applicable:

- public pages, sections, navigation, forms, fields, labels, help text,
  validation, errors, empty states, loading states, confirmations, banners,
  modals, tooltips, and recovery paths;
- emails, receipts, notices, reminders, transactional notifications, support
  replies, complaint acknowledgments, cancellation/refund communications, and
  provider-failure messages;
- downloads, exports, print, QR, short-link, document, metadata, title,
  description, canonical, accessibility-label, and social-preview output; and
- public API error or status text that a normal user or professional can see.

No internal, technical, developer, builder, test, fixture, staging, schema, API,
migration, feature-flag, debug, placeholder, artifact, backlog, sprint, mock,
seed, database, environment-variable, route-name, component-name, or other
inappropriate operational language may appear publicly. Necessary legal,
safety, privacy, billing, AI, processing, and service-limit concepts must remain
accurate but be explained in clear user language.

FIRST-FEW-SECONDS HOMEPAGE ACCEPTANCE

A new visitor must understand within the first few seconds:

- what the assigned specialty portal is and is not;
- who it helps;
- that useful public starting services are free subject to clear fair-use
  limits;
- the one obvious primary action;
- how to find legal information, a tool, Document Help, an attorney, a firm,
  or the appropriate Smarter Justice management path; and
- what happens next.

Avoid walls of text, competing primary calls to action, unexplained legal or
technical jargon, premature payment demands, hidden essential actions, fake
urgency, ambiguous buttons, decorative non-links, or dead-end gates.

EVERY-VERSION VISUAL-SYSTEM REVIEW

Review:

- portal logo, wordmark, favicon, brand consistency, and the appropriate
  specialty/Smarter Justice family relationship;
- color palette, contrast, state colors, focus, hover, visited, disabled,
  warning, error, success, and safety signals;
- font family, font files and licensing, font sizes, line height, line length,
  heading hierarchy, weight, legibility, and zoom/reflow;
- layout, whitespace, density, alignment, grouping, cards, borders, shadows,
  buttons, links, icons, imagery, forms, tables, navigation, footer, and print;
- responsive behavior, touch targets, keyboard, screen reader, reduced motion,
  forced colors, mobile browser chrome, email client behavior, notifications,
  QR/print, and slow/network-failure states; and
- consistency across public, professional, owner, staff, email, notification,
  support, and exported experiences while keeping internal controls private.

Proactively identify visual improvements, but implement only changes that
materially improve clarity, trust, accessibility, safety, completion,
conversion, consistency, supportability, performance, or user outcomes. A
review does not require a redesign. Preserve a strong design when change would
only create cosmetic activity, inconsistency, risk, or version churn.

DOMESTIC VIOLENCE SAFETY

For Domestic Violence Aid and embedded Stop Sign Project surfaces, preserve neutral labels,
safe-device and quick-exit truth, deliberate save/share, safe notifications,
neutral download names, confidential-location protection, safe URLs/referrers,
privacy-minimized analytics, and no required narrative before basic help. Never
sacrifice survivor safety for visual consistency or conversion.

REQUIRED EVIDENCE

Record the complete portal surface inventory, terminology scan, human plain-language
review, first-seconds script and result, before/after screenshots where changed,
responsive/device/accessibility evidence, email and notification review,
preserved areas and reasons, changed areas and expected benefit, defects and
retests, rule IDs, exact artifact, and rollback. Automated scans assist but do
not replace human public-language, first-impression, visual, and accessibility
acceptance.


===============================================================================
10A. ATTORNEY-OUTREACH FIRST-IMPRESSION AND DEMONSTRATION STANDARD
===============================================================================

Each assigned portal must support a truthful, practice-relevant demonstration that Roger can use during a brief office visit, on a phone or laptop, by screen share, or through self-guided follow-up.

TARGET EXPERIENCE

- FIRST 15 SECONDS: the portal must communicate specialty, credibility, public usefulness, and professional quality without requiring a technical explanation.
- FIRST 2 MINUTES: Roger must be able to show the homepage, a relevant search or starting path, one exemplary canonical attorney profile, and one strong public tool without encountering a broken, gated, private, or visibly unfinished state.
- FIRST 10 MINUTES: the portal must be able to explain the professional profile, free claim and correction rights, central Smarter Justice management, qualified membership value, and the next step.

These are presentation-design targets, not promises that every meeting will follow a fixed script. Do not add filler merely to occupy time.

MONDAY, AUGUST 3, 2026 DEMONSTRATION ACCEPTANCE

For the immediate launch objective, the demonstration path is launch-critical,
not optional polish. Before representing the assigned portal as ready for
attorney outreach, verify that:

- the homepage, attorney search, one exemplary attorney page, one exemplary
  firm page, one selected public tool, Document Help entry, professional-value
  page, and claim/enrollment continuation are visually coherent and complete;
- the demonstrated tool reaches a meaningful result without broken controls,
  placeholder copy, internal/developer language, false live data, or an
  unexplained dead end;
- desktop, laptop, tablet, and phone presentation is sharp, fast, readable, and
  consistent, with accessible keyboard, focus, zoom/reflow, and screen-reader
  behavior at the evidence level honestly available;
- public-user free access, fair-use limits, and any paid high-cost continuation
  are explained plainly and do not create a bait-and-switch impression;
- the attorney and firm membership presentation states the current $15 pricing
  accurately, distinguishes free claim/correction rights from paid membership,
  and makes no guarantee of clients or outcomes;
- QR codes, short links, forms, enrollment links, support paths, and fallback
  routes are tested from the exact candidate;
- the demonstration can be reset and repeated safely with no private data; and
- Roger receives an actionable rehearsal or human-acceptance request with the
  exact URL/build, script, expected result, safe data, defect form, and retest
  path whenever his real-world review would materially improve Monday
  readiness.

HUMAN ATTORNEY-DEMONSTRATION REHEARSAL AND ACCEPTANCE GATE

Before a portal is labeled `ATTORNEY-DEMONSTRATION ACCEPTED`, Roger or an
owner-authorized human delegate must rehearse the exact candidate through the
15-second, 2-minute, and 10-minute paths on at least one real phone and one real
laptop or desktop environment. The acceptance record must identify:

- exact artifact, deployment or preview URL, and nonsecret configuration;
- tester, date, timezone, device, browser, and relevant assistive technology;
- the attorney and firm profiles, public tool, Document Help path, pricing and
  enrollment presentation, QR/short link, support, and fallback tested;
- first-impression, clarity, speed, visual quality, trust, and operational
  findings;
- every defect, severity, owner, correction, retest, and accepted residual
  limitation; and
- the exact scoped owner decision authorizing outreach use.

When a human rehearsal is ready but unavailable, the builder must issue an
Owner Assistance Request immediately. It may continue all independent work and
may report automated or local readiness truthfully, but it must not silently
upgrade that evidence to human demonstration acceptance. Roger may explicitly
accept a narrow residual risk for a named artifact and outreach scope; such a
decision does not waive unresolved safety, privacy, security, accessibility,
legal, broken-journey, or false-claim defects.

A visually attractive page is not accepted when the journey behind it is
broken. A working journey is not accepted for attorney demonstration when its
presentation is visibly unfinished or untrustworthy.

DEFAULT PORTAL-SIDE DEMONSTRATION PATH

1. Portal homepage and specialty identity.
2. One practice-relevant public starting path or search.
3. One polished, complete, canonical attorney profile.
4. One strong specialty-appropriate public tool.
5. A concise For Attorneys or professional-value page.
6. Claim or create-profile continuation through the verified Smarter Justice path.
7. QR code or short link the attorney can use after the meeting to revisit the demonstration, review membership information, claim or create a profile, or request follow-up.

The QR code or short link is a direct continuation path, not a claim of
confidential communication. It must not expose private client data, unrestricted
staff pages, payment secrets, or owner controls. Use allowlisted destinations,
stable links, minimum necessary attribution, and safe expiration or revocation
when a campaign-specific token is used.

DEMONSTRATION PROFILE TRUTH

The canonical attorney profile shown on a public portal should be a real,
approved, source-supported public profile with truthful claimed or unclaimed
status. Do not publish a fictional attorney merely to create a polished example.

Synthetic profile data may be used only in a clearly labeled, nonindexed,
access-controlled demonstration or test environment that cannot be confused
with a real public listing. A synthetic demonstration profile is not a
canonical public profile and does not satisfy the public-profile acceptance
requirement.

An initial-launch five-platform presentation should normally begin with Smarter
Justice and then show the attorney's most relevant initial-launch portal. A broader
network presentation may instead show the most relevant registered legal micro-
portal. Do
not force every attorney through every portal. The portal builder is
responsible only for making the assigned portal's portion polished, truthful,
mobile-ready, and compatible with the current Smarter Justice tour.

ALL-FOUR-PORTAL READINESS AND OUTREACH SEQUENCING

All four initial-launch cohort portals are in the current Monday readiness, demonstration-quality,
public-freemium, owner-assistance, and exact-release scope. An outreach sequence
may begin with Divorce Law Aid and Estate Law Aid when current coordination
evidence supports that order, but older first-two-pilot language must not be
used to defer the product quality, Tier A preparation, human demonstration
rehearsal, profile/search readiness, public free core, or actionable owner
requests of Personal Injury Law Aid or Domestic Violence Aid.

Readiness does not mean identical activation. Each portal retains its own exact
artifact, launch-capability matrix, D0-D5 path states, product-specific
acceptance, rollback, and owner decision. Domestic Violence Aid remains subject
to the stricter survivor-safety gate and may require a narrower or differently
sequenced accepted scope. No portal may claim production or live status from
family inclusion alone.

DEMONSTRATION TOOL SELECTION

Select the demonstrated tool by evidence, not novelty. It should be relevant to the attorney's practice or understandable across practices, complete enough for a short demonstration, visually clear, safe with synthetic resettable inputs, free of confidential data, honest about retention and limitations, and supported by a fallback when a dependency is unavailable.

A document-upload or review-preparation tool may be selected when the exact portal safely supports the demonstrated formats and workflow. Do not expose a confidential upload, external AI, or attorney-review path merely because it would look impressive.

DEMONSTRATION DATA AND FALLBACK

- Use clearly labeled synthetic or owner-approved demonstration data.
- Do not present a fictional attorney, testimonial, transaction, appointment, review, membership, lead, case, or result as real.
- Do not expose private user, claimant, payment, owner, staff, or support information.
- Provide a tested offline or dependency-failure fallback that remains truthful.
- Preserve a self-guided path from the public portal without requiring Roger to narrate every step.

===============================================================================
10B. PROFESSIONAL MEMBERSHIP VALUE AND NEW YORK CITY OUTREACH TRUTH
===============================================================================

The portal may explain why an attorney or firm may find Smarter Justice membership economically attractive, but it must do so with current owner-approved pricing, qualified language, and clear separation between facts, comparisons, and outcomes.

PERMITTED QUALIFIED VALUE CONCEPTS

Where accurate and owner approved, professional-facing copy may explain that:

- the monthly membership may cost less than what some firms pay for one purchased lead or advertising response;
- one appropriate retained matter may cover a year or more of membership depending on the practice, matter, fee arrangement, and actual outcome;
- setup and ordinary ongoing administration are intended to be minimal, not literally zero;
- where the opportunity and intake systems are operational, inquiries may enter the firm's ordinary intake process and remain subject to conflict, capacity, eligibility, engagement, and professional judgment;
- Smarter Justice seeks public users through legal micro-portals and may also use verified New York City Justice Booth outreach to direct people toward available legal and community resources.

PROHIBITED COMMERCIAL CLAIMS

Do not promise or imply:

- a lead, client, consultation, retained matter, revenue, case value, ranking, exclusivity, territory, appointment, response rate, or return on investment;
- that one client will actually pay for a year or many years of membership;
- that participation requires absolutely no work;
- that every inquiry will be appropriate, conflict-free, reachable, retained, or valuable;
- that payment establishes credentials, specialty, publication, ranking, endorsement, availability, or opportunity acceptance.

PUBLIC COPY, SALES TALKING POINTS, AND INTERNAL GUIDANCE

Track these as separate controlled artifacts. A statement approved as Roger's
spoken talking point must not silently become public website copy. Comparative
cost language on a public page is OWNER DECISION REQUIRED unless the exact
artifact already contains current approved language. Prefer durable factual
descriptions of membership benefits when a market comparison cannot be
substantiated and maintained.

CENTRAL COMMERCIAL-TRUTH AUTHORITY

The portal must not become an independent source of current Smarter Justice
pricing, membership terms, entitlement status, Justice Booth operations, or
portfolio-wide commercial claims.

- Prefer a stable link to the current owner-approved Smarter Justice page or a
  controlled central projection over duplicated portal copy.
- When local portal copy is necessary, record the central source, approval date,
  effective date, expiration or review date, and responsible owner.
- If the central record is missing, stale, inconsistent, gated, or unverified,
  omit the specific price, comparison, booth-status claim, or entitlement
  statement and use narrower durable wording.
- A portal release must not silently update portfolio-wide pricing or sales
  policy.
- A Smarter Justice link or status record does not prove that billing,
  opportunities, or Justice Booth operations are live.

JUSTICE BOOTH TRUTH CONTROL

Every Justice Booth statement must record:

- status: PLANNED, SCHEDULED, ACTIVE, COMPLETED, or VERIFIED LOCATION;
- New York City limitation;
- location only when safe, public, authorized, and verified;
- date or applicable period;
- source of the status;
- owner approval;
- correction and expiration behavior.

A Justice Booth may be described as providing free direction to available legal
and community resources only when that description matches the approved
operating plan or verified activity. Do not imply legal representation,
emergency dispatch, government operation, guaranteed service availability,
permanent citywide coverage, or completed activity when only planned.

Where a portal displays Justice Booth status, it must use the newest approved
central status record or omit the status. Do not maintain multiple drifting local
copies. Expired SCHEDULED or ACTIVE statements must automatically become
unpublishable or enter a correction queue until reverified.

===============================================================================
10C. ONE CENTRAL SMARTER JUSTICE ACCOUNT, STRIPE, COMMERCIAL, SUBSCRIPTION, SEAT, AND ENTITLEMENT RELATIONSHIP
===============================================================================

Every applicable legal micro-portal must preserve one central professional identity and commercial
relationship through Smarter Justice wherever the exact current architecture
supports the capability.

CONTROLLING SHARED COMMERCIAL ARCHITECTURE

- One professional or firm must not need unrelated portal accounts, duplicate
  claimant identities, duplicate subscriptions, or independent payment
  relationships merely to manage approved participation across the network.
- Smarter Justice is the server-authoritative source for central professional
  accounts, authentication, firm/office/seat authority, membership,
  subscription, billing, payment-provider integration, entitlements,
  reconciliation, refunds, cancellations, chargebacks, receipts, support, and
  commercial audit.
- Use one central Stripe integration or other exact owner-approved central
  payment architecture across applicable portals. A portal must not create an
  independent Stripe account, checkout source of truth, subscription state,
  payment webhook authority, entitlement ledger, or commercial support system
  merely because the portal needs a local membership link or display.
- Each portal may render only its approved local presentation, portal-specific
  eligibility, specialty context, claim/create/manage continuation, and
  receipted read-only entitlement or status projection under the exact current
  Smarter Justice contract.
- Keep payment separate from identity, professional status, profile authority,
  publication, credential truth, specialty qualification, organic ranking,
  endorsement, availability, opportunity acceptance, representation, and
  outcome.
- No percentage of legal fees, settlement, recovery, refund, or case outcome is
  authorized by this prompt.
- Do not require duplicate portal subscriptions or charge a professional twice
  for the same centrally covered identity, seat, entitlement, or approved
  network benefit.

CURRENT OWNER-APPROVED USD MONTHLY PILOT PRICING AND TERMS — JULY 31, 2026

The current pilot price is no longer a broad $10-$20 placeholder. Use the
following owner-approved structure for preparation, test acceptance, attorney
demonstration, and production activation once the applicable Tier B/C and owner
gates pass:

INDIVIDUAL ATTORNEY

- $15 USD per month for each covered attorney-profile membership.
- The membership may support enhanced claimed-profile participation and
  threshold eligibility to receive appropriately routed public-user assistance,
  document or case review, consultation, or full legal-work opportunities.
- Every opportunity remains subject to identity, professional status,
  specialty, jurisdiction, conflict, consent, capacity, allocation, support,
  complaint, and attorney-acceptance controls.

LAW FIRM

- $15 USD per month for the claimed firm-profile membership.
- Plus $15 USD per month for each attorney profile the firm covers and enrolls in
  the network.
- The firm base membership does not automatically cover unlimited attorneys.
- Use one active payer and entitlement source for each covered attorney seat.
  Do not double-charge the same attorney merely because the attorney also has
  an individual login or the firm has more than one office.
- Define firm administrator, office, roster, seat assignment, invitation,
  transfer, removal, suspension, offboarding, billing, audit, and dispute
  behavior before live activation.

FREE CLAIM AND FACTUAL CONTROL REMAIN SEPARATE

Basic profile claiming or claimant preparation, identity and authority review,
factual correction, suppression, removal, and approved factual editing remain
available without requiring payment. Payment activates the approved membership
benefits and opportunity-eligibility threshold; it does not create identity,
credentials, authority, publication, specialty, organic rank, endorsement,
availability, or factual truth.

CLAIMED STATUS AND PAID MEMBER STATUS MUST REMAIN DISTINCT

Use separate server-authoritative states and public language for:

- `CLAIMED — BASIC FACTUAL CONTROL`, meaning the authorized claimant may manage
  approved factual profile fields without a paid membership; and
- `MEMBER — ACTIVE PAID PROFESSIONAL MEMBERSHIP`, meaning the exact attorney or
  firm membership is paid, current, entitled, supported, and eligible for only
  the separately accepted paid benefits.

Do not label every claimed profile a paid member. Do not remove factual control,
correction, suppression, or removal rights when membership lapses. A lapse,
cancellation, failed payment, dispute, or suspension must remove or pause only
the exact paid benefits and opportunity-eligibility state, subject to current
support, notice, grace, reconciliation, and rollback rules.

FIRM BASE MEMBERSHIP AND COVERED-ATTORNEY SEATS

The $15 USD firm-profile base and each $15 USD covered-attorney seat are
distinct approved entitlements, not duplicate charges for the same benefit. For
clarity:

- one firm profile plus one covered attorney is $30 USD per month before any
  applicable tax;
- one firm profile plus three covered attorneys is $60 USD per month before any
  applicable tax;
- an attorney participating without a paid firm-profile membership may use one
  $15 USD individual covered-attorney membership when otherwise eligible; and
- the same attorney seat may have only one active payer and one active seat
  charge at a time, whether paid individually or by a firm.

Before live activation, define and test payer transfer, firm invitation,
attorney consent, effective date, proration or credit, failed transfer,
removal, firm departure, office transfer, cancellation, delinquency, dispute,
refund, reactivation, audit, and support. Moving an attorney from individual
payment to firm coverage, or back again, must not create overlapping attorney-
seat charges, loss of factual profile control, or unreceipted entitlement gaps.

CANONICAL OWNER COMMERCIAL AND PUBLIC-VALUE MODEL — ALL LEGAL MICRO-PORTALS

This is the controlling plain-language summary unless Roger issues a newer
explicit scoped owner decision. Detailed sections remain controlling and may be
stricter; this summary resolves ambiguity rather than replacing safeguards.

PUBLIC LEGAL USERS

- Every portal provides a genuinely useful free core: legal starting help,
  specialty routing, official resources, basic preparation, profile discovery,
  correction/removal/privacy/support, safety where applicable, and a meaningful
  non-AI path.
- The free core may use transparent fair-use and abuse controls but may not be
  quietly crippled, moved behind payment, or conditioned on unnecessary identity
  collection.
- After a reasonable disclosed free allowance, optional high-cost AI, OCR,
  document processing, storage, or similar usage may use owner-approved
  cost-recovery pricing with advance disclosure, explicit consent, metering,
  spending limits, receipts, failed-service credit/refund behavior, privacy and
  security controls, and a useful free or lower-cost fallback.
- Public-user high-cost-service payments are separate from professional
  membership, attorney fees, representation, filing fees, case value, recovery,
  settlement, or legal outcome.

PROFESSIONAL MEMBERSHIP

- One covered individual attorney profile: `$15 USD per month` before applicable
  tax.
- One claimed firm-profile membership: `$15 USD per month` before applicable tax.
- Each attorney profile covered by a firm: an additional `$15 USD per month`
  before applicable tax.
- Examples: one individual attorney with no firm-paid seat is `$15/month`; one
  claimed firm plus one firm-covered attorney is `$30/month`; one claimed firm
  plus three firm-covered attorneys is `$60/month`, all before applicable tax.
- One attorney seat has one payer and one active charge. Transfers, credits,
  proration, cancellations, delinquency, disputes, refunds, departures, and
  reactivation must be centrally reconciled.
- Annual plans, setup fees, paid-trial conversion, hidden add-ons, portal-specific
  competing prices, or another billing cadence require a newer explicit owner
  decision.

FREE CONTROL, PAID STATE, AND VALUE TRUTH

- Basic factual claiming, correction, suppression, removal, verification
  preparation, and approved profile control remain free.
- `CLAIMED — BASIC FACTUAL CONTROL` is not `MEMBER — ACTIVE PAID PROFESSIONAL
  MEMBERSHIP`. Payment does not prove identity, credentials, specialty,
  authority, publication, rank, availability, or participation.
- Membership may establish eligibility for approved network functions and
  separately gated opportunities; it never guarantees clients, leads, calls,
  inquiries, matters, consultations, document reviews, revenue, ranking,
  exclusivity, representation, or outcomes.
- Smarter Justice remains the one central commercial, account, identity, firm,
  seat, payment, subscription, entitlement, receipt, reconciliation, and support
  authority. A portal may display an approved projection or link but may not
  create a competing source of truth.

Every material portal release must create an `OWNER COMMERCIAL AND PUBLIC-VALUE
MODEL NONREGRESSION RECEIPT` comparing the exact portal copy, controls, profile
states, prices, examples, claim routes, membership routes, checkout links,
terms, entitlements, fallbacks, and tests against this model. Conflict or unknown
central authority is fail-closed for the affected payment or entitlement path,
not permission to invent a portal-local substitute.

CENTRAL COMMERCIAL TERMS AND ENTITLEMENT MANIFEST

Smarter Justice must maintain one versioned server-authoritative manifest for
the current commercial policy. It must identify, without exposing secrets:

- stable owner-approved product and entitlement IDs;
- $15 USD monthly firm-base and covered-attorney-seat prices;
- currency, billing cadence, effective date, review date, and supersession;
- exact benefits, exclusions, launch tiers, opportunity thresholds, and portal
  applicability;
- payer, seat, firm, office, roster, transfer, cancellation, delinquency,
  grace, refund, credit, chargeback, tax, receipt, and support behavior;
- test and live payment-provider product/price bindings;
- public terms and portal projection version; and
- owner approval, rollback, and revalidation triggers.

Monthly billing is the current approved cadence. Do not introduce an annual
plan, setup fee, hidden add-on, automatic paid trial conversion, or different
portal price without a newer scoped owner decision. Taxes may be added only
where legally required and must be disclosed before payment. Portals may render
or link to the approved manifest projection but must not hard-code a competing
commercial source of truth or expose raw provider identifiers unnecessarily.

CENTRAL PRICING AND BILLING AUTHORITY

- Smarter Justice remains the server-authoritative source for price, products,
  firm and attorney seats, checkout, subscriptions, entitlements, receipts,
  cancellation, refunds, chargebacks, reconciliation, support, and audit.
- Portals may display an approved controlled projection or link; they must not
  create a separate pricing or billing source of truth.
- Prepare and accept exact public terms, taxes, cancellation, refund,
  proration, delinquency, failed-payment, seat-transfer, and support behavior
  before production activation.
- A price approved for demonstration or test mode does not authorize live
  checkout until the exact Tier C, D4/D5 where applicable, deployment, and
  owner-activation gates pass.

VALUE AND OUTCOME TRUTH

The $15 price may be described as a low-cost professional membership when that
wording is current and approved. Never promise or imply a client, lead, matter,
consultation, document review, revenue, ranking, exclusivity, territory, case
value, return on investment, or outcome. Membership provides eligibility and
access to approved network functions, not guaranteed business.

PUBLIC LEGAL-USER FREEMIUM, FAIR-USE, AND COST-RECOVERY MODEL

CORE PUBLIC VALUE MUST REMAIN FREE

The portals must provide useful free legal starting value to public users,
including as applicable:

- issue and specialty routing;
- plain-language legal information and limitations;
- current official-resource and form discovery;
- attorney and firm search and profile viewing;
- basic Document Help entry and memory-first question/unknown preparation;
- basic organizers, checklists, timelines, and consultation preparation;
- correction, suppression, removal, privacy, support, and recovery pathways;
- a deterministic non-AI fallback for every launch-critical journey; and
- Domestic Violence Aid emergency, safety, quick-exit, public-safe resource,
  and confidential-location protections.

Do not charge merely to understand a starting point, locate an official
resource, find a professional profile, correct or remove a profile, delete user
data, obtain safety information, or use the approved basic non-AI path.

PUBLIC FREE-CORE FLOOR AND NONREGRESSION

The free public core is a standing owner decision that continues after the
Monday target unless Roger expressly supersedes it. A later build may improve,
expand, meter costly extensions, or add optional paid services, but it may not
silently move the free core behind payment, make it unusably small, require
unnecessary identity data, or substitute a payment prompt for a meaningful
non-AI starting path.

Any proposed reduction of the free core requires a scoped owner decision,
public-user and safety impact review, clear migration and notice, updated terms,
accessible alternatives, exact tests, rollback, and a record of why ordinary
rate limits, abuse controls, provider budgets, or operational improvements were
insufficient. Emergency, survivor-safety, official-resource, profile search,
correction/removal, privacy/deletion, support, and basic non-AI starting paths
must not be conditioned on high-cost-service payment.

REASONABLE FREE ALLOWANCE AND FAIR-USE CONTROLS

Costly capabilities may use transparent limits based on actual service burden,
such as number or size of documents, OCR pages, AI tokens or tasks, generated
exports, storage, retention period, repeated retries, or other measurable
resource use. Before public activation:

- define the free allowance and reset period;
- provide a clear usage meter or understandable remaining-allowance notice;
- use account, device, session, rate, size, and abuse controls proportionate to
  risk without requiring unnecessary identity data;
- establish provider budgets, quotas, timeouts, retries, circuit breakers,
  pause thresholds, and kill switches;
- preserve a useful free non-AI or lower-cost continuation when the allowance
  is exhausted; and
- test denial, degradation, provider outage, quota exhaustion, and recovery.

PAID HIGH-COST CONTINUATION

After a reasonable free allowance, the platform may offer an optional payment
for high-cost AI, OCR, document processing, storage, or comparable services.
The charge must:

- be shown before the costly operation begins;
- require explicit user consent;
- identify what will be processed, the expected output, price, material limits,
  privacy/data handling, retention, deletion, and fallback;
- be designed approximately to recover direct provider, processing, payment,
  storage, support, and safety-control costs rather than exploit urgent legal
  need;
- use an owner-approved exact price or pricing formula tied to current vendor
  economics;
- provide a receipt and clear failed-operation credit/refund behavior;
- avoid surprise charges, hidden auto-renewal, negative-option enrollment, or
  charging for a failed or unusable result;
- include spending caps and an accessible way to stop, delete, or continue with
  a free alternative; and
- remain separate from attorney fees, representation, filing fees, outcomes,
  settlements, recoveries, or a percentage of legal fees.

COST-RECOVERY FORMULA CURRENTNESS AND USER PROTECTION

The cost-recovery formula must be versioned and revalidated whenever provider
pricing, payment fees, storage, model choice, quotas, retention, support burden,
or safety controls materially change. A reasonable operational reserve may be
included only when transparent and owner approved; the high-cost continuation
must not become hidden urgency pricing, vulnerability-based pricing, or a
penalty for reaching a legal deadline. Do not vary price based on the user's
perceived desperation, protected characteristic, matter value, likely recovery,
or legal urgency.

A paid AI vendor key or connected payment account is not acceptance. Complete
vendor, privacy, security, untrusted-content, cost, monitoring, support,
incident, fallback, rollback, test, deployment, and scoped owner-activation
gates first.

OPTIONAL FUTURE LEAD OR OPPORTUNITY INTEGRATIONS

Nolo and 4LegalLeads are optional future integration candidates only. They are
not required for Tier A, the initial portal launch, the attorney demonstration,
or basic professional membership. Any future connection must be managed through
Smarter Justice unless a newer exact architecture says otherwise and must pass
separate vendor, source-rights, consent, privacy, professional eligibility,
conflict, allocation, support, complaint, economics, monitoring, pause,
rollback, D4/D5, and owner-approval gates. Do not promise availability, volume,
exclusivity, leads, clients, or outcomes.

PORTAL ACCEPTANCE

For each portal, test that:

- claim, create, manage, membership, and support continuations reach the exact
  approved Smarter Justice destination;
- tampered, stale, replayed, unknown, or wrong-portal state fails safely;
- central and portal field ownership cannot become last-write-wins;
- a central entitlement projection cannot alter organic rank or factual profile
  truth;
- local unavailability does not create a second account, subscription, payment,
  or entitlement system; and
- rollback or suppression cannot reactivate an expired, cancelled, disputed,
  or unauthorized commercial state.

===============================================================================
11. CROSS-PORTAL COOPERATIVE LEARNING AND CONTINUOUS IMPROVEMENT
===============================================================================

The four micro-portals and Smarter Justice must improve as a coordinated learning network while remaining separate products, repositories, brands, domains, versions, and deployments.

PROACTIVE CREATIVITY AND INNOVATION EXECUTION GATE

Before fixing the release scope, perform a complete opportunity scan across the
facets named in `DUR-046`. Do not limit discovery to Roger's explicit requests,
the last continuation's list, or features another portal already has. Use
current research, lawful competitor learning, user and professional journeys,
operational evidence, support and failure patterns, cross-portal learning, and
the exact assigned artifact to identify meaningful candidates.

Create a `PROACTIVE CREATIVITY AND INNOVATION OPPORTUNITY RECORD` containing:

- each material opportunity, origin, evidence, target users/surfaces, expected
  benefit, risk, cost, dependencies, and rollback;
- disposition: IMPLEMENT, ADAPT, VERIFY FIRST, DEFER, REJECT, WITHDRAW, PRESERVE
  CURRENT IMPLEMENTATION, or NOT APPLICABLE WITH EVIDENCE;
- why the chosen connected set is the strongest responsible scope;
- exact tests and acceptance; and
- reusable learning for Smarter Justice and other portals.

There is no mandatory count of implemented innovations. There is a mandatory
quality of examination. A material release may preserve the current solution
when evidence shows change would not help, but it may not pass by ignoring
obvious opportunities or treating a checklist-only implementation as proactive
product work.

Every assigned portal builder has two duties:

1. INBOUND LEARNING DUTY
Review useful verified patterns, standards, tests, data models, UX decisions, profile-growth methods, security controls, accessibility improvements, operational practices, and release methods developed in Smarter Justice and the other legal micro-portals when those exact materials are available.

2. OUTBOUND LEARNING DUTY
Document every reusable lesson discovered or proven in the assigned portal so Smarter Justice and the other portals can evaluate and benefit from it.

Do not wait for Roger to ask for cross-sharing.

Use Smarter Justice as the coordination hub for shared standards and portfolio truth, not as permission to create runtime coupling between independent portals.

Every reusable lesson must be expressed as a Candidate Shared Standard containing:
- Standard ID and title.
- Origin portal and exact version.
- Problem addressed.
- Evidence before and after the change.
- Reusable rule, schema, test, UX pattern, data method, or operational method.
- Specialty-specific assumptions.
- Security, privacy, accessibility, legal, and operational implications.
- Adoption dependencies.
- Backward-compatibility and migration considerations.
- Acceptance tests.
- Recommended classification for Smarter Justice and each other portal: adopt, adapt, verify first, defer, reject, or not applicable.

Shared learning is not limited to successful features. Failed experiments, unreliable sources, weak conversion patterns, accessibility failures, brittle integrations, false assumptions, and expensive operational methods should also be shared so the network does not repeat them.

A. WHAT SHOULD BE SHARED

Share reusable, non-confidential knowledge such as:
- Profile-source discoveries and yield.
- Source terms or reliability concerns.
- Better entity-resolution methods.
- Firm-first profile-growth methods.
- Specialty-evidence patterns.
- Claim-conversion improvements.
- Search and filter patterns.
- Canonical profile URL patterns.
- Attorney-firm relationship handling.
- Currentness and stale-record methods.
- Import, idempotency, receipt, suppression, last-known-good, and rollback patterns.
- Accessibility fixes.
- Mobile enrollment improvements.
- Performance improvements.
- Security controls.
- Abuse and duplicate protections.
- Support workflows.
- Testing strategies.
- Release-quality automation.
- Bugs, failed approaches, and pitfalls.
- Useful design components that remain specialty-adaptable.
- Owner-operation improvements.
- Conversion and professional-acquisition insights.

B. WHAT MUST NOT BE SHARED OR CENTRALIZED

Do not share or centralize:
- Public-user legal narratives.
- Privileged or confidential communications.
- Uploaded legal documents.
- Private identity or credential evidence.
- Passwords, sessions, MFA secrets, recovery data, credentials, or API secrets.
- Payment-card data.
- Private support notes.
- Production personal data.
- Portal-specific confidential information.

C. NO BLIND COPYING

Every inbound idea must be classified:
- Adopt now.
- Adapt for this specialty.
- Verify first.
- Defer.
- Reject.
- Not applicable.

Record:
- Exact source portal and version.
- Evidence status.
- What was learned.
- Why it fits or does not fit.
- Specialty adaptation.
- Dependencies.
- Risks.
- Tests.
- Owner-approval needs.

The newest exact uploaded artifact in the assigned portal remains implementation truth. A roadmap, conversation, prior prompt, or another portal’s code does not prove implementation or compatibility.

D. REQUIRED CROSS-PORTAL LEARNING PACKET

Include in every final release:
- Registry snapshot reviewed.
- Exact source artifacts reviewed and their evidence status.
- Inbound ideas evaluated.
- Inbound decisions and adaptations.
- Outbound reusable lessons.
- Reusable specifications, schemas, tests, or patterns.
- Profile-source and yield findings.
- UX and conversion findings.
- Security, privacy, accessibility, and reliability findings.
- Failed or rejected approaches and reasons.
- Recommended changes for Smarter Justice.
- Recommended changes for each of the other three initial portals.
- Items requiring owner approval.

The packet is a handoff recommendation. The assigned portal chat must not directly edit another repository.

A reusable code component may be proposed, but do not copy code blindly across repositories. Confirm license/provenance, dependency compatibility, security impact, design fit, portal-specific legal scope, tests, and maintenance ownership. Prefer shared specifications and contract tests over hidden cross-repository coupling.

At the start of each material build, review the newest available Cross-Portal
Learning Packets that are actually available and relevant. At the end, update
the existing packet and inbound/outbound adoption matrix with the material
lesson, or record that no new reusable lesson was found. Do not create a
duplicate packet merely to satisfy a deliverable count.

A portal builder may implement only the assigned portal's local adoption. It
must not edit another portal or Smarter Justice. Any proposed network adoption
must be expressed as a concise candidate standard or builder instruction for
the appropriate separate repository.

===============================================================================
11A. NEWEST EXACT ARTIFACT LEARNING, REVALIDATION, LINEAGE, AND PROPAGATION CONTROL
===============================================================================

The builder must learn from the newest exact Smarter Justice and legal
micro-portal artifacts actually available and relevant to the assigned portal.
Learning is not limited to counts, blockers, or continuation facts. Review
verified features, functions, workflows, safeguards, design patterns, operating
controls, tests, failed approaches, and exact-release practices that may improve
the target portal or prevent a repeated defect.

Learning may operate in every useful direction:

- Smarter Justice to one or more micro-portals;
- one micro-portal to Smarter Justice;
- one micro-portal to another micro-portal;
- a specialty-safe implementation to a reusable family practice;
- a defect or rejected experiment to a network anti-pattern; and
- a stronger later implementation to supersession of an older shared pattern.

Learning does not require uniform feature parity. Transfer a verified beneficial
principle only when it fits the target. Do not make every portal contain the
same features, routes, data, wording, design, dependencies, or operating burden.

PROMPT-DEVELOPMENT AUDITED SMARTER JUSTICE v1.7.61 LEARNING — HISTORICAL CENTRAL SNAPSHOT, NOT A FIXED FUTURE BASE

The exact central artifact used for this prompt refinement is:

- artifact: `smarter-justice-v1.7.61(1).zip`;
- SHA-256: `a8ef3578f4a899e69dfe5388082ba556b6f1facb7b63e6c31f1d3e84e7ae391e`;
- exact size: 5,398,198 bytes;
- ZIP entries: 1,648;
- packaged files: 1,630;
- explicit directories: 18;
- uncompressed packaged file bytes: 15,261,638;
- compressed packaged file bytes: 5,156,624;
- self-excluding `FILE_INVENTORY_SHA256.txt`: 1,629 records, SHA-256
  `852b3e7088194781dc95782882c6a75dd7c9001f2515ae7f4ae9cd1a66c2449d`;
- current prompt-refinement inspection: exact archive identity, entry structure,
  CRC/path-safety boundary, and complete inventory validation passed; and
- prior prompt-development evidence recorded 115 dependency-independent
  package test scripts reproduced across two command invocations. Do not treat
  that prior record as a new current portal test reproduction or as proof of
  PostgreSQL, staging, production, D4, D5, or live acceptance.

Relevant central learning to evaluate for each assigned portal includes:

- version-neutral portal authority discovery rather than fixed portal versions
  inside reusable masters;
- one central professional account, commercial terms, firm/seat, entitlement,
  and $15 membership truth with portal-specific presentation and acceptance;
- useful public freemium and non-AI fallback controls;
- Matter Path, memory-first preparation, explainability, and specialty-safe
  portal handoffs;
- scoped owner actions, launch matrices, path-scoped D0-D5, exact receipts,
  acceptance freeze, continuation, and rollback; and
- dual-master and repository boundaries preventing central or portal drift.

The exact v1.7.61 archive does not contain the complete stable durable-rule ID
ledger, `DUR-040` non-idempotent-evidence system, `DUR-041` recurring current-
internet/competitive-research system, `DUR-042` closed-inheritance baseline
system, `DUR-043` append-only baseline-evolution system, or the expanded every-
surface public-language and first-seconds visual acceptance terminology. Those are prompt-
level propagation requirements for later exact central and portal releases;
their absence from v1.7.61 is not evidence that an individual portal lacks all
underlying behavior.

Use v1.7.61 only as dated central implementation learning. On every reuse,
discover the newest exact completed Smarter Justice artifact and current master,
revalidate the learning, and preserve portal-specific implementation and
acceptance. Do not fix v1.7.61 as the future central base.

NEWEST-ARTIFACT REVALIDATION

When a newer exact source artifact becomes authoritative, do not continue to
teach an older learning item without checking its relationship to the newer
artifact. Classify each material older item as:

- REVALIDATED UNCHANGED;
- REVALIDATED AND IMPROVED;
- CHANGED — NEW RULE OR IMPLEMENTATION;
- SUPERSEDED;
- REMOVED INTENTIONALLY;
- REMOVED WITH UNCLEAR REASON;
- REGRESSED OR BROKEN;
- NOT RECHECKED; or
- NOT APPLICABLE TO THE NEW VERSION.

Only current, revalidated learning may drive a new target build as verified
learning. Preserve older packets as historical lineage, but do not allow a more
detailed older packet to override the newest artifact's current truth. Do not
discard a still-valid principle merely because its original learning ID came
from an older version.

VERIFIED SOURCE AND TARGET THRESHOLDS

A source learning candidate may be called verified only when evidence supports
that it:

- exists in the newest authoritative source artifact;
- is accurately classified as active, gated, fixture-only, planned, blocked,
  or deprecated;
- has reproducible tests or acceptance evidence appropriate to maturity;
- has no unresolved critical defect that invalidates the claimed benefit;
- provides a concrete public-user, professional, owner, staff, safety,
  operational, business, quality, or release benefit;
- has known dependencies, field ownership, failure behavior, support burden,
  and rollback boundaries; and
- can be described without overstating deployment, adoption, outcomes, or
  production maturity.

Code presence, a route, schema, fixture, mock, screenshot, release note, prompt,
or passing unit test alone is not proof that the practice works well.

Before requiring target adoption, evaluate target need, specialty, maturity,
existing behavior, public-language and design fit, data ownership, privacy,
security, authorization, accessibility, survivor safety, dependency and
supply-chain burden, migration risk, owner/staff burden, support and incident
burden, launch timing, testing cost, rollback, repository independence, and
whether the target already has an equal or stronger accepted implementation.

LEARNING CLASSIFICATION AND DISPOSITION

Classify each material learning candidate as one of:

- UNIVERSAL SHARED PRACTICE;
- SMARTER JUSTICE CENTRAL-ONLY PRACTICE;
- PORTAL-SHARED PRACTICE;
- SPECIALTY-SPECIFIC PRACTICE;
- SAFETY-RESTRICTED PRACTICE;
- PRODUCT-IDENTITY-SPECIFIC PRACTICE;
- EXPERIMENTAL OR NOT YET VERIFIED;
- BLOCKED OR INCOMPLETE;
- DEPRECATED;
- REJECTED ANTI-PATTERN; or
- NOT PORTABLE.

Then record one target disposition:

- ADOPT AS VERIFIED;
- ADAPT FOR THIS PORTAL;
- VERIFY BEFORE ADOPTION;
- PRESERVE EQUAL OR STRONGER LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION;
- DEFER WITH A GENUINE DEPENDENCY;
- REJECT WITH EVIDENCE;
- NOT APPLICABLE WITH EVIDENCE; or
- SUPERSEDE AN OLDER PRACTICE.

LEARNING LIFECYCLE AND PROPAGATION STATES

Use the following lifecycle:

1. SOURCE ARTIFACT IDENTIFIED.
2. SOURCE EVIDENCE CLASSIFIED.
3. LEARNING CANDIDATE EXTRACTED.
4. SOURCE BENEFIT AND LIMITS EVALUATED.
5. TARGET SUITABILITY EVALUATED.
6. TARGET PROMPT OR RELEASE DISPOSITION RECORDED.
7. TARGET IMPLEMENTATION COMPLETED.
8. TARGET-SPECIFIC TESTS AND ACCEPTANCE COMPLETED.
9. TARGET RELEASE EXACTLY VERIFIED.
10. TARGET LEARNING ELIGIBLE FOR FURTHER PROPAGATION.

Use these propagation states:

- SOURCE CANDIDATE;
- SOURCE VERIFIED;
- TARGET PROMPTED;
- TARGET IMPLEMENTED;
- TARGET ACCEPTED;
- ELIGIBLE FOR FURTHER PROPAGATION;
- VERIFY AGAINST NEW SOURCE VERSION;
- PAUSED;
- REGRESSED;
- DEPRECATED;
- WITHDRAWN; or
- REJECTED.

Only `SOURCE VERIFIED`, `TARGET ACCEPTED`, or `ELIGIBLE FOR FURTHER
PROPAGATION` may be described as verified learning, subject to the exact scope
of the evidence. A source-verified pattern remains unverified in the target
until target-specific acceptance passes.

NO CIRCULAR VERIFICATION

Do not validate a learning claim merely because:

- a prompt required it;
- another learning packet repeated it;
- the target copied it;
- the source later cited the target;
- multiple summaries repeat the same unsupported statement;
- two products inherit the same unverified fixture, mock, or standard; or
- release notes, current-truth files, tests, and owner summaries merely copy the
  same original count or status.

Trace every verified claim back to exact implementation and independent
acceptance evidence. When Product A teaches Product B and Product B later
improves the pattern, preserve Product A as the original lineage and identify
Product B as the stronger accepted implementation only after target acceptance.
Do not retroactively claim Product A contained Product B's improvement.

LEARNING LINEAGE RECORD

For each material candidate that affects a build or future prompt, record:

- learning ID and parent learning ID;
- source product, artifact filename, version, SHA-256 when available, and source
  evidence state;
- source supersession and revalidation state;
- feature, workflow, safeguard, anti-pattern, or release practice;
- maturity and gate state;
- exact routes, modules, schemas, records, tests, or acceptance evidence where
  useful;
- verified benefit, known defects, dependencies, ownership, safety, privacy,
  security, accessibility, and specialty constraints;
- portability classification and candidate targets;
- target prompt or release, required adaptation, disposition, reason, tests,
  support implications, and rollback implications;
- target implementation artifact and SHA-256;
- target acceptance evidence and release state;
- propagation eligibility, descendant learning IDs, later regression,
  withdrawal or deprecation reason, and last revalidated date.

NEGATIVE LEARNING, REGRESSION, AND WITHDRAWAL

If a later artifact shows that a shared practice is unsafe, broken, misleading,
unsupported, or materially weaker than believed:

1. mark it `REGRESSED`, `DEPRECATED`, or `WITHDRAWN`;
2. identify every prompt, standard, and target that inherited it;
3. stop further propagation;
4. create corrective instructions for affected future builds;
5. preserve historical lineage rather than silently deleting it; and
6. require target-specific remediation where the affected implementation
   remains active.

BOUNDED LEARNING REVIEW AND DOCUMENTATION DISCIPLINE

The review depth must match materiality. Perform deeper exact-artifact review
when a learning claim affects safety, legal truth, privacy, security,
accessibility, source rights, identity, billing, publication, opportunity
allocation, data ownership, central/portal contracts, release truth, or a
shared standard. A full audit of unrelated historical files is not required
merely because a ZIP was supplied.

Do not create formal learning records for trivial formatting, generated
timestamps, equivalent refactoring without a portable principle, one-off
fixture values, private debugging details, cosmetic novelty, or version-number
changes alone. Use one current learning register with historical lineage when
sufficient. Do not create duplicate packets, empty tables, artificial IDs, or a
cross-platform standard before the practice is mature enough.

MANDATORY `NEWEST ARTIFACT LEARNING APPLIED` SECTION

Every material release summary and updated continuation handoff must include a
concise section titled:

`NEWEST ARTIFACT LEARNING APPLIED`

Identify, as applicable:

- source product and exact artifact version;
- source evidence and revalidation state;
- learning ID or practice;
- target disposition and adaptation;
- preserved specialty or survivor-safety constraint;
- required target tests and rollback;
- target acceptance and propagation state;
- stale learning superseded; and
- exact source or target verification still required.

When no learning candidate materially changes the assigned portal after review,
state: `No additional cross-platform change is required after review of the
newest relevant learning.` Then give the evidence-based preservation reason.
Do not add boilerplate merely to satisfy the heading.

===============================================================================
13. SAFE INNOVATION AND EXPERIMENT STANDARD
===============================================================================

The builder should identify and test new ideas when they are likely to produce meaningful value and fit the selected coherent release. The builder must not force an experiment or feature count.

A good innovation is:
- Relevant to the assigned portal.
- Useful to a real user, attorney, firm, staff member, or owner.
- Truthful.
- Privacy-minimizing.
- Accessible.
- Reversible.
- Testable.
- Maintainable.
- Compatible with the central architecture.
- Honest about its maturity.

Examples of promising innovation areas:
- Better issue-to-specialty routing.
- Better readiness checklists.
- Better professional profile comparison without ranking claims.
- Firm-first directory expansion.
- Smarter source-quality scoring.
- Currentness dashboards.
- Duplicate and affiliation review assistance.
- Mobile-first claim flows.
- QR campaign continuity.
- More useful empty states that guide users safely.
- Privacy-preserving analytics.
- Batch profile review tools.
- Import preview and conflict explanations.
- Faster owner review without lowering standards.
- Public page performance and structured discoverability.

Do not ship:
- Gimmicks.
- Unsupported AI claims.
- Automated legal conclusions.
- Fake personalization.
- Manipulative conversion patterns.
- Dark patterns.
- Unreviewed public data.
- Features that collect sensitive data without a clear need.
- Novelty that increases operational burden without measurable value.

Every experimental feature must be clearly classified as implemented, verified, simulated, staging-only, gated, deferred, blocked, or owner-dependent.

For every material experiment, create a concise Experiment Card:
- User or operational problem.
- Hypothesis.
- Existing evidence.
- Intended users.
- Minimum safe implementation.
- Success and failure criteria.
- Privacy, security, accessibility, legal, and support risks.
- Measurement method.
- Rollback or removal method.
- Result and disposition: IMPLEMENT_NOW, PRESERVE, PORTAL_FUTURE, SHARED_STANDARD_CANDIDATE, DEFER, REJECT, or OWNER_DECISION_REQUIRED.

Stop, revise, or remove an experiment when it adds confusion, risk, maintenance cost, sensitive-data collection, misleading claims, poor accessibility, or operational burden without demonstrated value.

===============================================================================
14A. GENERATED-EVIDENCE REPRODUCIBILITY, NON-IDEMPOTENT OUTCOMES, FLAKY TESTS, AND LEGAL-USE TRUTH
===============================================================================

DO NOT CONFUSE TWO DIFFERENT FORMS OF IDEMPOTENCY

State-changing idempotency means safely repeating an operation does not create
duplicate or inconsistent state. Evidence-generation repeatability means an
accepted input and execution boundary produces the same or an explicitly
bounded equivalent result. A workflow may satisfy one and fail the other. Test
and record both where applicable.

REQUIRED EVIDENCE REPEATABILITY CLASS

Classify each material evidence generator as exactly one of:

- BYTE-DETERMINISTIC — independent clean executions produce byte-identical
  output;
- CANONICALLY EQUIVALENT — volatile nonmeaningful fields are removed under a
  documented canonicalization rule and remaining evidence is identical;
- SEMANTICALLY BOUNDED STOCHASTIC — wording or ordering may vary, but required
  facts, citations, constraints, schemas, and invariants stay inside a defined
  tested boundary;
- EXTERNAL-STATE DEPENDENT — results depend on a named current source,
  provider, registry, network, clock, law, or environment and are bound to
  retrieval and observation evidence;
- HUMAN-JUDGMENT DEPENDENT — a qualified human decision is part of the evidence
  and the reviewer, criteria, inputs, conflicts, limits, and receipt are
  recorded; or
- NOT ACCEPTABLE AS SOLE EVIDENCE — the output may assist investigation or
  drafting but cannot independently prove the asserted fact, requirement,
  legal conclusion, implementation, acceptance, or live state.

Do not use `deterministic`, `reproducible`, `idempotent`, `verified`, or
`repeatable` without identifying the class, exact boundary, comparison method,
and evidence scope.

GENERATION-BINDING RECORD

For each material evidence-producing run, record as applicable:

- generator, command, script, test, workflow, model, template, prompt, schema,
  policy, and version;
- exact source artifact, tree, patch, inputs, data snapshot, and hashes;
- provider, model revision, endpoint, runtime, package, dependency, lockfile,
  OS, architecture, hardware, and container identity;
- temperature, top-p, seed, decoding mode, locale, timezone, clock source,
  concurrency, ordering, network, cache, retry, and external-state controls;
- source URLs or identifiers, retrieval time, response digest, effective date,
  jurisdiction, and currentness scope;
- nonsecret configuration and feature flags;
- canonicalization and permitted-variance rules;
- run ID, actor, environment, timestamps, output files and hashes; and
- acceptance invariants, observed variance, result, invalidation triggers,
  rollback, and revalidation date.

A fixed seed, temperature zero, cache hit, identical prompt, or provider claim
does not by itself prove reproducibility across provider updates, hardware,
libraries, platforms, or time.

NON-IDEMPOTENT OUTCOME TEST

For every claimed deterministic or repeatable evidence path:

1. reset mutable state, caches, temporary files, databases, queues, clocks, and
   environment variables to the documented clean boundary;
2. run the identical accepted command and inputs independently at least twice,
   and more often when risk or known variability requires it;
3. compare bytes or the documented canonical form;
4. compare all evidence-relevant facts, statuses, counts, citations, decisions,
   and invariants;
5. record `PASS IDENTICAL`, `PASS CANONICAL EQUIVALENCE`, `PASS BOUNDED
   VARIANCE`, or `NON-IDEMPOTENT OUTCOME DETECTED`;
6. preserve every run and invalidate the affected evidence and gate when a
   material difference occurs; and
7. diagnose, remove, or explicitly bound the variance before acceptance.

Never keep only a favorable run, rerun until success, hide a conflicting
result, silently alter seeds, reuse an unrecorded cache, suppress failure,
weaken assertions, widen tolerances without evidence, or use majority voting to
manufacture acceptance.

FLAKY-TEST AND VARIABLE-PIPELINE QUARANTINE

Detect order, state pollution, randomness, clock, timezone, locale, network,
concurrency, port, filesystem, resource, retry, and external-service dependence.
Use isolated state, deterministic fixtures, controlled clocks, explicit seeds
where supported, collision-safe resources, and authenticated mocks or recorded
responses where appropriate. Mark unresolved variable tests or pipelines
`QUARANTINED — NOT ACCEPTANCE EVIDENCE`; keep dependent gates closed or at the
last exact accepted state.

AI AND GENERATED OUTPUT

AI output may support research leads, brainstorming, summaries, organization,
drafts, comparison, missing-information detection, and human review. It does
not prove an underlying fact, law, source currentness, professional status,
product behavior, test result, artifact identity, acceptance, or admissibility.
Preserve exact prompts and available provider/model/sampling state, verify
sources independently, validate structured invariants, compare repeated outputs
for material contradiction or omission, require deterministic or human-review
fallbacks for launch-critical claims, and preserve original records.

Do not promise that machine-generated, AI-assisted, or system-produced material
is admissible, authentic, expert, or sufficient for a court, agency, insurer,
professional, or adversary. Recheck any proposed or enacted evidence rule before
relying on it. Provide chain-of-custody, original-preservation, metadata,
annotation-separation, export, and qualified-review guidance without making an
admissibility conclusion.

MANDATORY OUTPUTS

Create, when applicable:

- `GENERATED EVIDENCE REPRODUCIBILITY MANIFEST`;
- `NON-IDEMPOTENT OUTCOME REGISTER`;
- `FLAKY TEST AND VARIABLE PIPELINE QUARANTINE REGISTER`;
- exact comparison reports and run hashes;
- evidence invalidation and reacceptance receipts; and
- continuation entries for unresolved variance and the next exact test.

===============================================================================
14B. CURRENT INTERNET RESEARCH, LAWFUL COMPETITIVE LEARNING, AND CONTINUOUS EVIDENCE-BASED INNOVATION
===============================================================================

EVERY-MATERIAL-VERSION RESEARCH DUTY

Every material assigned-portal version and every authorized Mode B2 central version must include a current targeted internet
research workstream at the depth appropriate to the release. Builders own this
work and must not wait for Roger to supply ideas, competitor names, articles, or
feature requests.

Research is mandatory even when the evidence supports preservation. It is not a
quota for changes. An urgent P0 correction requires the current research needed
to understand the critical boundary, but unrelated broad research must not
delay a safe urgent fix. Record deferred broad research as explicit debt. A
Full Milestone Release requires the complete applicable research breadth.

MINIMUM RESEARCH BREADTH

Review current information relevant to the selected release, including as
applicable:

- official statutes, rules, pending rules, forms, courts, agencies, bar and
  licensing authorities, filing systems, deadlines, fees, and public resources
  for the assigned specialty and jurisdiction;
- accessibility, privacy, security, identity, payments, email, storage, AI,
  OCR, malware, incident, monitoring, backup, restore, and software-supply-
  chain standards and guidance;
- legal-aid, court self-help, nonprofit, public-benefit, guided-help, document-
  help, readiness, and official-form systems;
- attorney directories, profile claiming, firm and seat management,
  professional verification, Q&A, consultation, document review, matter
  preparation, opportunity, referral, and support workflows;
- public competitor and adjacent-platform first impression, navigation,
  onboarding, mobile, design, accessibility, pricing, freemium, conversion,
  trust, retention, support, and cancellation patterns;
- current AI evaluation, provenance, hallucination, prompt-injection,
  reproducibility, human-oversight, cost, and data-governance practices; and
- user complaints, enforcement actions, accessibility barriers, deceptive
  practices, dark patterns, known failures, and stronger alternatives.

SOURCE HIERARCHY AND CURRENTNESS

Use:

1. official law, court, agency, standards-body, regulator, licensing, and
   government sources for authoritative facts;
2. the competitor or provider's current public product, help, pricing, terms,
   privacy, accessibility, and technical documentation for observed features;
3. peer-reviewed research, primary technical documentation, and recognized
   nonprofit or institutional guidance;
4. reputable independent reporting and reviews for context and market signals;
   and
5. search summaries, AI answers, snippets, forums, and social material only as
   leads requiring independent verification.

Record publication, effective, retrieval, and review dates; timezone; source
owner; URL or durable identifier; jurisdiction; version; evidence type;
currentness; and limitations. Do not present a search-engine or AI summary as
the source. Recheck current facts before implementation or public reliance.

COMPETITIVE LEARNING WITHOUT COPYING

Observe public user problems, workflows, features, information architecture,
pricing presentation, support models, and measurable patterns. Adapt a useful
underlying principle to the assigned portal's users, specialty, architecture,
brand, Smarter Justice relationship, legal boundaries, safety, public freemium
floor, and business model.

Do not copy source code, protected text, images, datasets, branding, logos,
trade dress, proprietary prompts, confidential materials, or nonpublic
workflows; bypass authentication, paywalls, robots, rate limits, terms, or
technical controls; impersonate users; interfere with another service; import
profiles, reviews, ratings, or claims without source rights; or adopt dark
patterns, hidden fees, forced continuity, deceptive urgency, pay-to-rank
ambiguity, fake scarcity, or unsupported outcome claims.

RESEARCH AND COMPETITIVE LEARNING RECORD

For each material finding, record:

- research ID and parent learning ID;
- research question and release connection;
- source title, owner, URL or identifier, publication/effective/retrieval/review
  dates, jurisdiction, evidence type, and currentness;
- public feature or principle observed without copying protected expression;
- user, professional, business, safety, accessibility, or operational problem;
- strengths, limits, complaints, conflicts, uncertainty, and anti-patterns;
- legal, ethical, privacy, security, accessibility, safety, source-rights,
  support, and operational implications;
- target-specific adaptation and disposition: ADOPT AS VERIFIED, ADAPT,
  EXPERIMENT, VERIFY FURTHER, PRESERVE STRONGER LOCAL PRACTICE, DEFER WITH
  DEPENDENCY, REJECT WITH EVIDENCE, or NOT APPLICABLE WITH EVIDENCE;
- hypothesis, expected benefit, measurement, failure signal, guardrails, kill
  switch, tests, human acceptance, rollback, and release evidence; and
- revalidation, supersession, regression, withdrawal, and propagation state.

Innovation is a paramount recurring responsibility, but implementation remains
subject to material benefit, safety, legality, ethics, source rights, support,
testability, release quality, and `DUR-001`. Do not change the platform merely
because a competitor does something differently or to make a release appear
innovative.

MANDATORY OUTPUTS

Create or update:

- `CURRENT INTERNET RESEARCH AND COMPETITIVE LEARNING REGISTER`;
- source/currentness and lawful-use evidence;
- adopted/adapted/rejected/deferred disposition records;
- experiments with metrics, guardrails, kill switches, and rollback;
- implementation and target-acceptance evidence; and
- continuation and learning-lineage propagation entries.

15. BASELINE, SCOPE, AND WORKING METHOD
===============================================================================

STALE PORTAL MODERNIZATION BASELINE — WHEN TRIGGERED

When `STALE-PORTAL REVALIDATION REQUIRED` is active, complete and evidence-bind
the following before treating prior acceptance as current:

1. Exact artifact lineage, package structure, inventory, rollback, and current
   portal identity.
2. Runtime, package manager, dependencies, lockfiles, build scripts, tests,
   deterministic/reproducible classification, and vulnerability posture.
3. Official law, rules, forms, agencies, deadlines, filing methods, fees,
   eligibility, safety resources, and public citations relevant to the specialty.
4. Attorney, firm, organization, service, and resource profile currentness,
   source admissibility, duplicates, suppression/removal, canonical pages, and
   search.
5. Smarter Justice account, claim, firm, seat, commercial, entitlement, support,
   contract, and D0-D5 compatibility without inventing central implementation.
6. Canonical public freemium and professional pricing/model nonregression.
7. Public language, brand, homepage first seconds, logo/favicon, navigation,
   mobile, forms, emails, errors, metadata, QR/print, and accessibility.
8. Privacy, security, authorization, untrusted content, AI/OCR isolation, data
   minimization, deletion, support, incident response, monitoring, backup/
   restore, rollback, and stale-restoration prevention.
9. Product capability maturity, abandoned or broken routes, fixtures, fake live
   data, dormant language assets, migrations, and obsolete operational claims.
10. Continuation, durable-rule baseline, current master authority, learning
    lineage, release records, owner-action queue, and truthful development/
    launch/deployment/live state.

Invalidate and rerun only the evidence materially affected by the modernization
work, but do not preserve an old `PASS` merely because the old test still exists.
Record every preserved implementation and every modernization change so the
release is evidence-based rather than a wholesale rewrite.

Before coding, create a baseline record containing:
- Exact artifact identity.
- Version.
- Repository layout.
- Production and deployment state.
- Test results.
- Blocked tests.
- Dependencies.
- Public routes.
- Issue pathways.
- Tools and content.
- Search behavior.
- Attorney profile behavior.
- Firm profile behavior.
- Claim and create behavior.
- Smarter Justice link behavior.
- Import/export behavior.
- Profile counts and evidence states.
- Source and currentness behavior.
- Owner/staff operations.
- Security and privacy controls.
- Accessibility and mobile state.
- Monitoring, backup, restore, incident, and rollback state.
- Live connections and gates.
- Known defects.
- Assumptions and uncertainties.

Then choose the complete justified material next-version scope that delivers
the strongest high-value connected improvement the builder can fully complete,
support, and exact-test. A release may concentrate on one or more connected outcomes, but it must
complete the entire justified connected journey comprehensively. It must not
stop at an early patch merely because that patch could independently be called
material.

Create one concise RELEASE SCOPE RECORD with the selected outcomes, preserved
strengths, portal work included, central Smarter Justice work excluded,
deferred or rejected ideas, closed gates, and evidence required for
completion.

The Release Scope Record must also state:

- which worthwhile improvements the builder independently discovered beyond
  Roger's named requests;
- which accepted Smarter Justice and cross-portal practices were reviewed;
- which were adopted or adapted;
- the specific evidence-based reason for every material divergence, rejection,
  or deferral;
- why the scope is complete rather than merely sufficient;
- which connected improvements were considered and included;
- why any connected improvement was excluded;
- why the work is not a token or artificially fragmented release;
- what evidence shows the builder continued beyond the first easy change; and
- the exact stopping condition for every remaining connected item.

The scope decision must explicitly explain:
- Why these improvements are the highest-value feasible work now.
- Which improvements were originated by the builder rather than requested by Roger.
- What user-facing and backend changes reinforce each other.
- What will be measurable or visibly better.
- What remains intentionally unchanged and why.
- What was deferred and why.
- How the scope benefits Smarter Justice and the other portals through reusable learning.

A release must not be called complete merely because it added profiles, redesigned pages, added tests, improved an adapter, or produced documentation. Visual refinement may be a material release component when it corrects a demonstrably weak attorney-outreach first impression, but appearance alone does not excuse broken workflows or open P0 defects. The chosen scope must address the weakest material constraints discovered across the whole product, within safe and evidence-supported limits.

The scope should normally include a balanced combination of:
- User-facing quality.
- Professional profiles and search.
- Profile-growth operations.
- Smarter Justice integration readiness.
- Backend reliability and data integrity.
- Owner/staff operability.
- Accessibility/mobile polish.
- Security/privacy.
- Tests and exact-release quality.

Use verifiable implementation increments within one Complete Connected
Material Release. Test continuously, keep the exact baseline available, and
update truth records as the implementation changes. Do not split one connected
journey into artificial releases merely because the implementation uses
increments.
