===============================================================================
33. REUSABLE FINAL RELEASE AND HANDOFF REQUIREMENTS
===============================================================================

Every reuse must end in exactly one truthful outcome:

- JUSTIFIED MATERIAL RELEASE; or
- LEGITIMATE NO-CHANGE AUDIT.

A material release is required when safe material work is justified and can be
completed. A new version or ZIP is prohibited when the evidence supports a
no-change outcome.

JUSTIFIED MATERIAL RELEASE PACKAGE

When a material release is produced, the package must include, as applicable:

- complete portal ZIP;
- coherent derived version;
- exact SHA-256 and exact size;
- ZIP metrics;
- self-excluding inventory and inventory hash;
- canonical tree digest;
- two independent fresh extractions;
- final tests from both extractions where practical;
- dependency install and vulnerability audit;
- honest blockers;
- release notes, audit report, change map, and Release Decision Record;
- Current Build Intake Record;
- product maturity map and feature classification;
- Profile Expansion Gate result and any approved exception;
- exact before-and-after profile metrics;
- Source Admissibility and Use Register and Profile Source Registry;
- applicable batch receipts and candidate, accepted, rejected, duplicate,
  ambiguous, stale, suppressed, and removed counts;
- applicable source-yield and currentness reports;
- attorney-page, firm-page, search, claim/create/manage, and integration
  acceptance;
- import receipts, last-known-good, suppression, and rollback evidence;
- security, privacy, accessibility, responsive, performance, reliability,
  owner/staff operations, support, communications, monitoring, incident,
  backup, restore, and rollback reviews;
- Cross-Portal Learning Packet or an honest no-new-lesson disposition;
- updated owner decisions and full Next Version Improvement List;
- updated version-neutral continuation prompt;
- exact current Smarter Justice coordination record;
- honest live, staging, local, simulated, gated, blocked, deferred, and
  owner-dependent statement; and
- concise owner-facing Release Decision Summary.

The Release Evidence Index must map every required category to the exact file,
test, receipt, screenshot set, register, or documented not-applicable,
no-material-change, blocked, or deferred disposition. Do not create empty or
duplicate records merely to satisfy this list.

EXCEPTIONAL NO-CHANGE AUDIT DELIVERABLE

Only when the heightened no-change evidence burden is fully satisfied, do not
create a new ZIP, version, continuation artifact, profile batch, or cosmetic
evidence package. Provide:

- the verified authoritative artifact identity;
- the release-type and audit-depth decision;
- the evidence showing that every mandatory improvement category was examined
  at the required audit depth and no safe material portal-side change was
  justified;
- confirmation that no critical open defect was ignored;
- the current profile-growth/currentness state without a synthetic candidate
  search;
- any real independent or blocked opportunity carried forward; and
- the reason a version increment was not warranted.

The updated continuation prompt inside a material release must remain
version-neutral and instruct the next builder to use the newest exact completed
artifact in the dedicated chat.

The final ZIP must be the exact tested artifact.

Do not:

- test one tree and deliver another;
- deliver only a patch, report, proposed-profile list, or unreviewed data dump
  when a material release is justified;
- fabricate a ZIP for a no-change audit;
- include secrets, production personal data, `node_modules`, nested old ZIPs,
  duplicate source trees, or temporary runtime data.

===============================================================================
34. LEGAL-CONTENT FRESHNESS AND EXTERNAL VERIFICATION
===============================================================================

Legal content, court information, forms, filing procedures, fees, deadlines, thresholds, officeholders, agency links, and professional-registration information may change.

For every material build:

- Identify changed, dependency-affected, launch-critical, stale, high-risk, or
  previously disputed public claims that could have changed.
- Reverify every official form, filing instruction, deadline, fee, threshold,
  hotline, emergency resource, and government link touched by the release or
  shown in the attorney-demonstration path.
- For a Full Milestone Release, perform the broader launch-critical legal
  content freshness review.
- Verify material current facts through authoritative primary sources.
- Prefer courts, legislatures, agencies, official datasets, and professional-controlled primary sources.
- Record source, retrieval date, effective date where known, jurisdiction, supported fields, and limitations.
- Preserve exact citations or source records inside the package where appropriate.
- Do not rely on another portal’s content as legal authority.
- Do not silently copy another portal’s legal text.
- Do not state a deadline, fee, threshold, form, filing rule, or procedural requirement without current authoritative support.
- Mark uncertain or jurisdiction-dependent information plainly.
- Use safe escalation language when a user may face an urgent deadline, safety concern, contested matter, incapacity issue, serious injury, or other high-risk condition.
- Keep legal-information language separate from individualized legal advice.
- Never claim that a tool result, profile, checklist, organizer, or routing decision is a legal conclusion.

Every release must update a Legal Content Freshness Register for the launch-critical public content actually reviewed.

===============================================================================
35. RELEASE-SCOPE BALANCE AND MATERIALITY
===============================================================================

Profile-growth and currentness evaluation is mandatory. Profile addition and
refresh are the default when they fit the release, subject only to the narrow
documented exceptions in this prompt. Profile work must not crowd out an
authentic critical safety, reliability, accessibility, integration, data, or
operational defect.

A coherent material release should normally include a balanced subset of:

- Public user value
- Professional profile growth
- Existing-profile currentness
- Canonical profile and firm page quality
- Search and discoverability
- Claim and central-management integration
- Backend and data integrity
- Owner and staff operability
- Accessibility and responsive quality
- Security and privacy
- Reliability and operations
- Tests and exact-artifact evidence

Do not create dozens of superficial changes across unrelated areas.

Do not ship only cosmetic polish when a known P0 defect remains safely fixable.

Do not ship only new profiles when the portal cannot render, search, correct, claim, suppress, or maintain them reliably.

Do not hold a useful release indefinitely for optional perfection.

A material release must have:

- A clear problem statement.
- A coherent scope.
- Measurable or observable benefit.
- Defined non-goals.
- Acceptance criteria.
- Complete exact-artifact evidence.
- Honest blockers.

If the exact audit shows that the current artifact already satisfies the
material objectives and no safe improvement justifies release risk, the correct
outcome is an honest no-change audit result. Do not manufacture a version
solely because this prompt was reused. A no-change audit must confirm that the
current profile-growth record is not materially stale, but it need not conduct
a synthetic candidate search or create a Profile-Growth Exception Receipt solely to
force release activity.

===============================================================================
36. SOFTWARE SUPPLY CHAIN, LICENSES, MIGRATIONS, AND DATA REVERSIBILITY
===============================================================================

Every material release must review:

- Direct dependencies
- Transitive dependencies
- Lockfile consistency
- Runtime requirements
- Package-manager requirements
- Vulnerabilities
- Licenses
- SBOM or equivalent dependency record where practical
- Abandoned or unnecessary packages
- Build scripts
- Generated artifacts
- External assets
- Fonts, icons, images, logos, and profile photographs
- Data-source reuse rights

Do not:

- Add a dependency without a clear purpose.
- Use a fake package or unverified mirror.
- Disable integrity checks.
- alter the lockfile merely to manufacture a pass.
- Include `node_modules` in the release.
- Copy protected competitor text, reviews, ratings, photos, logos, or badges.
- Include a font file in a user-delivered artifact unless rights and necessity are explicitly established.

For every schema or data migration:

- Record the old and new format.
- Preserve stable IDs.
- Define forward migration.
- Define rollback or forward-fix.
- Test partial failure.
- Test restart.
- Test duplicate prevention.
- Test profile, firm, relationship, claim, source, and import state.
- Preserve last-known-good data.
- Back up high-risk production-like data before acceptance.
- Do not make an irreversible migration without an approved recovery path.

===============================================================================
37. OWNER COMMUNICATION, ACTIVE COLLABORATION, AND BUILD CONDUCT
===============================================================================

The canonical launch section controls Owner Assistance Requests, secret safety,
request states, vendor connections, and the coordinated action queue. Do not
create a second conflicting protocol here.

Roger's working preferences and the builder's conduct duties remain:

- Use concise factual progress updates during long builds.
- Surface material defects, blockers, improvements, and ready owner actions
  early rather than waiting for the final report.
- Do not announce completion before the final ZIP passes exact-artifact
  verification and any claimed environment acceptance passes.
- Do not promise background or asynchronous work.
- Do not ask Roger to repeat information already present in the chat or files.
- Resolve ordinary engineering, product, design, data, content, test, and
  implementation decisions from evidence.
- Escalate only a true owner decision, qualified review, secure credential or
  vendor connection, deployment approval, irreversible risk, or genuine
  source-of-truth conflict.
- Before asking for help, prepare the exact recommended action, prerequisites,
  verification, revocation/rollback, and fallback required by the canonical
  Owner Assistance Request protocol.
- Never request a raw secret in chat or an artifact.
- When Roger must perform a manual nonsecret step, use numbered instructions
  and one copy-and-paste block at a time, stating exactly what to replace and
  with what.
- Continue all independent safe work while an owner-assisted item is pending.
- Preserve storage by avoiding redundant artifacts and duplicate
  documentation.
- When Roger requests a text-link-only or ZIP-link-only response, put the
  complete evidence inside the artifact and provide only the requested link.
- Never conceal incomplete work behind polished language.
- Distinguish exact verified, locally verified, browser verified, staging
  accepted, production active, simulated, gated, blocked, deferred,
  owner-action-requested, and owner-action-verified states.

===============================================================================

Before final delivery, confirm all applicable statements are true:

- The newest exact completed portal base was used.

- No other repository was modified.

- Existing records were refreshed or corrected where reasonable.

- Basic profile control remains free.
- Paid placement remains separate and labeled.
- Organic ordering is not secretly paid.
- Smarter Justice central authority is preserved.
- The portal remains independently deployable.
- Imports fail closed.
- Stale revisions are rejected.
- Last-known-good and rollback are preserved.
- Security and privacy checks passed to the claimed level.
- Accessibility and responsive checks passed to the claimed level.
- Dependency and license results are recorded.
- The exact final ZIP contains no secrets, caches, production data, `node_modules`, or nested obsolete releases.
- The final ZIP was independently fresh-extracted and tested.
- Release notes match the final ZIP.
- Deployment truth is honest.
- All unresolved work remains in the Next Version Improvement List.
- The reusable continuation prompt was updated without freezing stale version numbers.
- Roger’s remaining approvals are listed clearly.

Before leaving final quality control, also confirm:

- all `DUR-001` through `DUR-043` rules are present in the active ledger and
  every applicable rule has a semantic implementation/test/evidence result;
- canonical baseline `DRB-2026-07-31-DUR001-DUR043-V2`, its exact parent,
  semantic fingerprint, inheritance receipt, transaction chain, exceptions,

  repeatability classifications where applicable;

- current internet and competitor research was performed lawfully, recorded

- v1.7.61 or any other historical Smarter Justice learning is not represented
  as the fixed future central base or as proof of portal implementation.

===============================================================================
39. VERIFIED CLAIMANT CONTROL — MANDATORY CENTRAL STANDARD
===============================================================================

Roger's controlling decision is:

Every attorney or authorized firm representative who claims a profile must be verified before receiving control.

This verification is free. Payment must not accelerate it, replace evidence, lower the standard, guarantee approval, or influence specialty eligibility.

A. CLAIMANT VERIFICATION LAYERS

1. ACCOUNT VERIFICATION

Confirm:

- Valid central Smarter Justice account
- Verified email
- Secure authentication
- Required MFA or step-up authentication for sensitive actions
- No known account takeover or high-risk session state

Email verification proves control of an email address. It does not by itself prove attorney identity or claim authority.

2. IDENTITY VERIFICATION

Confirm that the claimant is the person represented by the attorney profile, or the authorized representative for the firm profile.

Possible evidence, used proportionately and with data minimization, may include:

- Secure confirmation through a known official professional contact channel
- Confirmation through an official firm-domain address
- Confirmation through a public professional website controlled by the claimant
- Callback using an independently sourced official business number
- Professional registration or bar record
- Approved identity document review when necessary
- Signed attestation combined with corroborating evidence
- Existing verified firm-administrator relationship
- Other owner-approved reliable methods

Do not rely only on:

- A newly created free email address
- A matching name
- A social-media account
- Payment
- Possession of a public profile URL
- Knowledge of public facts
- A claim link alone

3. PROFESSIONAL-STATUS VERIFICATION

Confirm current professional status through the most authoritative current source reasonably available.

Record:

- Jurisdiction
- Registration or admission identifier where lawfully used
- Status
- Source
- Retrieval date
- Review date
- Limitations
- Reverification trigger

Current professional status does not by itself prove specialty or availability.

4. CLAIM-AUTHORITY VERIFICATION

Confirm that the claimant may control the exact profile.

For an attorney profile, verify that the claimant is the represented attorney or an expressly authorized representative under an approved policy.

For a firm profile, verify authority to act for the firm.

For firm-attorney relationships, verify the scope of the representative's authority. A firm administrator may not silently change protected personal, credential, specialty, or relationship facts for an attorney without the required review.

5. PORTAL-SPECIALTY VERIFICATION

Separately confirm that the attorney or firm has adequate evidence for the assigned portal's specialty.

A claimant may correct or claim an identity record even if specialty eligibility remains pending, disputed, or rejected.

Claim approval must not automatically create portal publication.

6. STAFF OR APPROVED FAIL-CLOSED DECISION

The initial launch model should use authorized staff review for claim approval.

Later automation may assist:

- Evidence collection
- Source matching
- Risk scoring
- Duplicate detection
- Official-status retrieval
- Firm-domain validation
- Review preparation

Automation must not silently approve ambiguous identity, authority, specialty, or firm-control claims.

B. CLAIM STATES

Use explicit states such as:

- NOT CLAIMED
- CLAIM STARTED
- ACCOUNT VERIFICATION PENDING
- IDENTITY EVIDENCE PENDING
- PROFESSIONAL STATUS PENDING
- AUTHORITY EVIDENCE PENDING
- SPECIALTY EVIDENCE PENDING
- STAFF REVIEW PENDING
- MORE INFORMATION REQUIRED
- IDENTITY VERIFIED
- PROFESSIONAL STATUS VERIFIED
- AUTHORITY VERIFIED
- SPECIALTY APPROVED
- CLAIM APPROVED
- CLAIM REJECTED
- CLAIM DISPUTED
- REVERIFICATION REQUIRED
- TEMPORARILY SUSPENDED
- PROFILE CONTROL REVOKED

Do not collapse these into one ambiguous “verified” state.

C. PENDING-CLAIM BEHAVIOR

While a claim is pending:

- The public profile may remain unclaimed if it independently satisfies publication requirements.
- The claimant may submit evidence and proposed changes.
- Proposed changes remain private or review pending.
- The claimant receives no unrestricted public-profile control.
- Protected fields remain unchanged.
- No verified claim indicator appears publicly.
- No paid placement or opportunity eligibility is activated.
- No competing duplicate profile is created.
- Support and appeal are available.

D. APPROVED-CLAIM BEHAVIOR

After approval:

- Grant only the permissions supported by the verified role.
- Preserve the same canonical portal URL.
- Display a truthful claimed state.
- Distinguish claimed from credential verified, specialty verified, Sponsored, available, and opportunity eligible.
- Apply field-level edit and review rules.
- Record approval evidence and revision.
- Record reviewer and time.
- Provide correction, suppression, removal, account security, and support controls.
- Require step-up authentication for sensitive actions.
- Reverify when required.

E. REVERIFICATION TRIGGERS

Reverification may be required after:

- Email or account-security change
- Legal name change
- Registration identifier change
- Professional-status change
- Jurisdiction change
- Firm change
- Firm-administrator change
- Material specialty change
- Public contact change that affects identity confidence
- Long inactivity
- Source conflict
- Claim dispute
- Impersonation report
- Suspension, revocation, retirement, or death information
- Security incident
- Owner-approved periodic review interval

F0. ORGANIZATION/SERVICE-PROVIDER CLAIM CONTROL — DOMESTIC VIOLENCE AID

When Domestic Violence Aid supports organization/service-profile claims, use a distinct authority model.

Verify:

- Central or approved local account.
- Representative identity.
- Exact organization and profile authority.
- Authority for the exact office, program, or service location.
- Organization-controlled or authoritative corroboration.
- Public-safe contact information.
- Confidential/nonpublic location suppression.
- Exact profile class.
- Supported services and service area.
- Staff or approved fail-closed decision.

Do not require professional bar-status verification for a non-attorney organization claimant, and do not treat a domain email, donation, sponsorship, or payment as sufficient authority.

Organization claim approval must not:

- Verify an attorney.
- Create attorney opportunity eligibility.
- Publish a confidential location.
- Guarantee availability or service acceptance.
- Create an endorsement.
- Activate paid prominence.
- Override correction, removal, or emergency safety suppression.

F. CLAIM DISPUTES AND APPEALS

Support:

- Duplicate claim review
- Competing firm authority
- Former employee or former firm administrator
- Attorney-firm relationship dispute
- Identity conflict
- Deceased or retired professional report
- Suspension or inactive status
- Incorrect specialty
- Unauthorized change
- Organization-authority dispute
- Unsafe or confidential location report
- Incorrect service, availability, or emergency-status report
- Appeal
- Temporary suppression
- Restoration

Every action must be auditable and reversible where practical.

===============================================================================
40. CONTROLLED SOURCE-BASED PUBLICATION OF UNCLAIMED PROFILES
===============================================================================

Source-based publication can be useful because it builds a searchable public directory before every attorney has joined.

It is permitted only as a controlled, evidence-gated process.

Publication and claiming are separate:

- Publication means the portal displays an approved source-supported profile.
- Claiming means a verified attorney, authorized firm representative, or authorized representative of a supported organization/service profile receives only the management rights approved for that exact profile and role.
- Outreach means the platform separately invites an attorney, firm, or approved organization representative to review, correct, remove, or claim the profile through the appropriate workflow.
- Payment means an optional central commercial product.
- None substitutes for another.

A. INITIAL PUBLICATION MODEL

The safer initial model is:

1. Automate candidate discovery, normalization, duplicate suggestions, source capture, field validation, and review preparation.
2. Require authorized human or staff approval before first public publication.
3. Publish the record as clearly unclaimed.
4. Provide claim, correction, suppression, and removal paths.
5. Preserve sources and currentness.
6. Keep unsupported fields absent.
7. Keep the same canonical page when later claimed.

B. LIMITED AUTOMATIC PUBLICATION MAY BE CONSIDERED LATER

Automatic first publication may be allowed only for a narrowly approved profile class or source workflow after all of the following are complete:

- Source admissibility and reuse rights accepted
- Reliable authoritative identity source
- Reliable specialty evidence
- Reliable duplicate and entity-resolution performance
- Reliable firm relationship logic
- Required field validation
- Public-page completeness
- Canonical URL stability
- Search integration
- Claim, correction, suppression, and removal
- Currentness and refresh schedule
- Legal and privacy review
- Error and false-positive measurement
- Random or risk-based quality sampling
- Emergency suppression
- Batch rollback
- Staff-visible receipts
- Monitoring and alerts
- Owner approval
- A defined publication threshold and pause threshold

Automatic publication must remain source-class specific. Approval for one source or profile type does not authorize all sources.

C. AUTOMATIC PUBLICATION MUST NOT MEAN

- Publishing immediately from raw scraping
- Publishing candidates without specialty evidence
- Publishing duplicates
- Publishing private data
- Publishing unsupported biographies
- Publishing personal email or phone information not intended for professional use
- Publishing copied competitor content
- Publishing photos or logos without rights
- Publishing suspended, inactive, deceased, or ambiguous attorneys as ordinary active profiles
- Publishing every attorney in a firm under every firm practice
- Publishing a profile merely to increase counts
- Bypassing suppression, correction, or removal
- Activating a claimed or verified state
- Activating paid prominence
- Activating opportunity access

D. PUBLICATION GATE

A profile may be approved for public unclaimed publication only when:

- Identity or organization entity is sufficiently resolved
- Professional status is reviewed to the required level for attorneys
- Organization status, profile class, and authority source are reviewed for organization/service profiles
- Portal specialty or service-category evidence is sufficient
- Public source use is lawful and recorded
- Required public fields are present
- Unsupported fields are excluded
- Duplicate checks pass
- Firm relationship is supported or omitted
- Organization program/location relationships are supported or omitted
- Public-safe contact is accepted and confidential/nonpublic location is suppressed where applicable
- Canonical URL is assigned
- Search inclusion is ready
- Source and currentness data exist
- Claim, correction, suppression, and removal paths work
- Mobile and accessibility requirements are met
- Public language does not imply participation, availability, emergency response, capacity, eligibility, endorsement, membership, government status, or shelter availability
- The final release can test and maintain the profile

E. PUBLICATION STATES

Use states such as:

- DISCOVERED
- NORMALIZED
- IDENTITY REVIEW
- SPECIALTY REVIEW
- DUPLICATE REVIEW
- PUBLICATION REVIEW
- PRIVATE APPROVED
- PUBLIC UNCLAIMED
- PUBLIC CLAIMED
- STALE
- REVERIFICATION REQUIRED
- TEMPORARILY SUPPRESSED
- REMOVED

E0. DOMESTIC VIOLENCE AID ORGANIZATION-PUBLICATION PAUSE CONDITIONS

Pause or suppress organization/service publication when:

- A location may be confidential or unsafe to publish.
- Emergency or hotline information cannot be verified as current.
- The organization disputes the profile.
- The organization appears closed, merged, renamed, relocated, or unreachable.
- Service availability, eligibility, or emergency status is unclear.
- The source does not support the displayed profile class.
- Contact information may route to a private individual.
- A safety, stalking, impersonation, or retaliation concern is reported.
- Correction or removal cannot be handled promptly.
- The exact source rights or central/local authority model is unresolved.

E1. SUPPRESSION, REMOVAL, AND STALE-RESTORATION PROPAGATION

An authorized temporary suppression, emergency safety suppression, permanent
removal, correction hold, or legal hold must be an explicit state transition,
not merely a hidden page flag. The portal must prevent stale public or
commercial reappearance across every affected surface.

At minimum, propagate the applicable state to:

- the canonical public route and entity resolver;
- public search, filters, suggestions, recommendations, and no-result recovery;
- sitemaps, feeds, structured data, alternate-language graphs, and internal
  links;
- portal and central exports, imports, projections, receipts, and
  last-known-good data;
- CDN, application, API, page, object, and search-index caches under portal
  control;
- Sponsored or Featured placement, membership-value surfaces, and public badges;
- claim, correction, outreach, notification, and follow-up queues;
- attorney-review or opportunity allocation; and
- owner/staff review, support, complaint, incident, and rollback views.

Use idempotent propagation, subsystem receipts, retries, monitoring, and a
bounded completion target appropriate to the risk. Domestic Violence Aid
emergency safety suppression must receive the fastest supported fail-closed
path and must not wait for ordinary batch refresh.

Preserve only the minimum private audit or tombstone evidence needed for
identity, duplicate prevention, dispute handling, legal hold, rollback, and
authorized restoration. Do not expose the suppression reason, confidential
location, survivor-safety concern, claimant evidence, or private support record
on the public page.

Keep these states distinct:

- correction pending;
- temporarily suppressed;
- emergency safety suppressed;
- removed by request;
- removed as invalid, duplicate, closed, deceased, suspended, or ineligible;
- legal hold or preservation hold; and
- eligible for reviewed restoration.

Restoration requires explicit authorized review, current source and status
reverification, duplicate and relationship checks, safety review where
applicable, re-publication acceptance, and fresh propagation receipts. A stale
import, retry, cache, historical release, rollback, or last-known-good snapshot
must not silently restore a suppressed or removed entity.

Required regression includes canonical-route behavior, search and sitemap
removal, internal-link removal, cache purge, export/import suppression,
outreach and opportunity exclusion, stale-replay rejection, rollback behavior,
and authorized restoration.

F. PUBLIC DISCLOSURE

An unclaimed profile should explain, in concise public language:

- It is based on identified public or professional sources.
- The professional has not necessarily claimed or endorsed the page.
- The listing does not prove availability or participation.
- The professional may claim, correct, or request removal.
- Paid placement, when present, is separately labeled.

Do not overload the page with internal governance language.

===============================================================================
41. ATTORNEY AND FIRM CLAIM-OUTREACH PROGRAM — SEPARATE, MANUAL-FIRST, AND GATED
===============================================================================

Domestic Violence Aid may also use a separate, manual-first organization correction/claim outreach program. It must use public organization-controlled contact channels, must not contact confidential locations or survivor-facing personal channels, must not imply partnership or endorsement, and must not mix organization outreach with attorney paid-membership solicitation. Organization outreach requires its own templates, authority checks, opt-out/suppression, safety pause conditions, and owner approval.

A controlled outreach program may help attorneys discover and claim their profiles.

Outreach is optional and separate from publication.

The initial outreach model should be manual or staff-approved batch outreach.

Do not activate autonomous outreach merely because profiles were published.

A. OUTREACH PURPOSE

Permitted purposes may include:

- Notify a professional or approved organization representative that a source-based public profile exists
- Invite free review and claim
- Invite correction or removal
- Explain central Smarter Justice management where the exact profile class is supported
- Explain full public portal profile pages
- Invite completion of missing supported information
- Invite firm-authority verification
- Invite organization-authority, public-safe-contact, correction, removal, or confidential-location review where applicable
- Invite an approved professional demonstration or owner-approved cohort

Outreach must not imply:

- The attorney, firm, organization, or service provider joined
- The attorney is endorsed
- The attorney is selected for a case
- The attorney is required to pay
- Claiming requires payment
- Paid membership guarantees leads
- Failure to respond causes an unfair penalty
- A fabricated deadline
- Government, court, or bar affiliation

B. INITIAL MANUAL-FIRST PROCESS

Before sending a batch:

1. Confirm each profile meets publication or private-outreach criteria.
2. Confirm the contact method is a lawful professional contact channel.
3. Confirm source and retrieval date.
4. Confirm the attorney, firm, organization, or service provider is not on the do-not-contact or safety-suppression list.
5. Confirm no unresolved death, suspension, impersonation, privacy, or dispute flag.
6. Confirm message, sender identity, subject, landing page, and claim route.
7. Confirm free claim, correction, removal, and support language.
8. Confirm applicable attorney-advertising, solicitation, privacy, electronic-communications, and anti-spam review.
9. Confirm rate limits, bounce handling, opt-out, suppression, and logging.
10. Obtain staff or owner batch approval.
11. Send a small pilot batch.
12. Review delivery, bounce, complaint, opt-out, claim, correction, and support results.
13. Pause if thresholds are exceeded.
14. Expand only after evidence.

C. OUTREACH CHANNELS

EMAIL

May be used only with:

- Lawful professional business contact
- Accurate sender identity
- Clear purpose
- Honest profile source explanation
- Free claim/correct/remove language
- Support contact
- Required physical-address or sender information where applicable
- Functional opt-out
- Bounce handling
- Complaint handling
- Suppression list
- Rate controls
- Deliverability monitoring
- Separation from transactional account email

POSTAL OR IN-PERSON

May support Roger's field outreach and attorney demonstrations.

Use:

- Portal-specific QR code or short link
- Minimum campaign context
- No sensitive information in the QR payload
- Clear Smarter Justice and portal relationship
- Free claim explanation
- Support
- Truthful optional paid-product separation

SMS OR MESSAGING

Keep closed unless an approved lawful basis, consent model, sender identity, opt-out, recordkeeping, and owner gate exist.

PHONE

Use only under an approved outreach policy with verified professional business contact, training, call records, do-not-contact handling, and applicable legal review.

D. OUTREACH DATA MODEL

Record:

- Outreach ID
- Professional, firm, or supported organization/service ID
- Portal ID
- Campaign or cohort
- Contact source
- Contact method
- Contact value or protected reference
- Source retrieval date
- Approval
- Message template version
- Sent time
- Delivery state
- Bounce
- Complaint
- Opt-out
- Do-not-contact
- Claim start
- Claim completion
- Correction request
- Removal request
- Support case
- Follow-up state
- Last contact
- Next permissible contact
- Operator
- Audit

Do not store unnecessary communication content or legal-matter information.

E. OUTREACH FREQUENCY AND RESPECT

- Avoid repeated unsolicited messages.
- Use a conservative frequency cap.
- Stop immediately after opt-out, complaint, removal request, death notice, impersonation concern, or other suppression trigger.
- Do not switch channels to evade an opt-out.
- Do not contact personal channels when a professional business channel is available and appropriate.
- Do not purchase questionable contact lists.
- Do not scrape or enrich private contact information.
- Do not use manipulative language.
- Do not hide the sender.

F. AUTOMATED OUTREACH MAY BE CONSIDERED LATER

Automation may assist only after:

- Manual pilot acceptance
- Legal review
- Privacy review
- Source-rights review
- Deliverability acceptance
- Domain authentication
- Bounce and complaint controls
- Opt-out and suppression synchronization
- Do-not-contact enforcement
- Template approval
- Contact-source quality acceptance
- Rate limits
- Monitoring
- Pause control
- Owner approval

Even then:

- Automation prepares or sends only approved classes and templates.
- Every recipient remains traceable to an approved profile and lawful contact source.
- High-risk, disputed, ambiguous, stale, suppressed, or removed records are excluded.
- The system pauses automatically on complaint, bounce, legal, deliverability, or quality thresholds.
- Automation never approves a claim.
- Automation never verifies identity.
- Automation never grants profile control.
- Automation never activates paid entitlements.

G. OUTREACH SUCCESS METRICS

Track carefully:

- Sent
- Delivered
- Bounced
- Complaints
- Opt-outs
- Claim starts
- Verified claims
- Corrections
- Removal requests
- Firm-authority claims
- Organization-authority, correction, removal, and safety-suppression outcomes
- Support cases
- Time to verification
- Profile completion improvement
- Paid-product applications only after separate lawful consent and gating

Do not optimize only for claim conversion. Accuracy, complaints, opt-outs, corrections, removals, trust, and support burden matter.

===============================================================================
42. CLAIM VERIFICATION, PUBLICATION, AND OUTREACH TESTING STANDARD
===============================================================================

Add tests for:

CLAIMANT ACCOUNT AND IDENTITY

- Unverified email
- Verified email only
- Wrong person with matching name
- Free email versus official-domain evidence
- Official business channel confirmation
- Known professional registration match
- Registration mismatch
- Inactive, suspended, revoked, retired, or deceased status
- Duplicate central account
- Account takeover risk
- Step-up authentication
- Evidence expiration
- Reverification

CLAIM AUTHORITY

- Attorney claims own profile
- Unauthorized third party
- Firm representative claims firm page
- Firm representative attempts protected attorney change
- Former firm administrator
- Competing firm authority
- Duplicate claim
- Conflicting claim
- Appeal
- Temporary suspension
- Revocation of control

SPECIALTY

- Identity verified but specialty unsupported
- Specialty approved
- Firm-level specialty without individual evidence
- Portal-specific field change
- Cross-portal specialty mismatch

PENDING AND APPROVED BEHAVIOR

- Pending claimant can submit evidence
- Pending claimant cannot publish protected changes
- Pending claimant cannot show claimed or verified state
- Approved claimant receives correct permissions
- Existing canonical URL remains
- No duplicate page
- Protected changes require review
- Material change triggers reverification

SOURCE-BASED PUBLICATION

- Approved human-reviewed public unclaimed profile
- Candidate remains private
- Duplicate blocked
- Unsupported specialty blocked
- Unsafe contact field excluded
- Stale profile held or labeled
- Suppression removes public rendering
- Removal behavior
- Automatic publication source class approved
- Unapproved source class blocked
- Quality threshold pauses batch
- Rollback restores last-known-good

OUTREACH

- Approved manual batch
- Unapproved batch blocked
- Suppressed recipient excluded
- Opt-out honored
- Do-not-contact honored
- Bounce recorded
- Complaint pauses follow-up
- Duplicate outreach prevented
- Frequency cap
- Template version
- Claim route
- Tampered route
- Unknown profile
- No verification bypass
- No automatic claim approval
- No automatic paid entitlement
- Cross-channel opt-out enforcement
- Outreach pause and rollback

END TO END

1. Build or refresh a lawful source-supported candidate.
2. Approve it for public unclaimed publication.
3. Render one canonical page.
4. Add it to search.
5. Approve a manual outreach invitation.
6. Deliver or simulate delivery only at the honestly available environment level.
7. Start the central claim flow.
8. Verify account.
9. Verify identity.
10. Verify professional status.
11. Verify claim authority.
12. Verify portal specialty separately.
13. Approve claim.
14. Preserve the same canonical page.
15. Apply approved profile update.
16. Reverify a protected change.
17. Test correction.
18. Test suppression.
19. Test revocation of control.
20. Test restoration.
21. Confirm no payment affected verification or organic ordering.

Do not claim outreach delivery, legal approval, identity verification, staging, or production from fixtures alone.
